STINGRAY EXPERIENCE - CURATION WORKING NOTES
Started: 2026.07.20
Source: Stingray Experience.txt (44,832 lines, 235 stardates, 2023.05.06 - 2026.07.11)
Method: chronological pass, chunk by chunk. Each entry tagged:
  M = keep for Builder's Manual (current-state fact, working config, verified procedure)
  F = keep for Field Notes (lesson, symptom-to-cause, principle, "don't do this again")
  N = keep for Narrative / backstory color (verbatim or lightly edited - the
      human story that gives the project a soul; goes into a "How Stormy
      came to be" chapter in the Manual, not into the tech chapters)
  C = cut (day-of noise, dead exploration with no takeaway, shipping details, stale links)
  ? = flag for James: contradiction or unresolved

CHUNK 1  lines 1 to ~1100  spans 2023.05.06 through 2025.01.12

2023.05.06  (L1)  Project mission statement
  M  Origin condensed: Parallax Stingray 2010 body + Articulated Robotics
     tutorials.  Metal plates replaced with laser-cut 1/8" and 3/8" acrylic
     (custom mounts, interior visible).  Propeller board replaced by
     Raspberry Pi 5.  Top: LIDAR, LiPo cell, low-voltage shutoff.  Front:
     fisheye or depth camera (OAK-D or RealSense), 3 headlights.  Back:
     voltage regulator, 12V power bus.  Interior: Pi 5, RoboClaw 2x7A,
     powered USB hub.
     >>> NOTE:  the 12V bus, buck converter, and USB hub have all since
     been superseded by the 52Pi PD power expansion board (per Handoff
     Note.txt).  Manual should carry the intent (power distribution +
     compute + drive + sensing) but the specifics need refresh from
     Stormy Essentials.

2024.05.06  (L~85, undated but before 2024.05.07)  Rediscovery
  N  "Found the Stingray body in a camouflage box on the bookshelf" -
     the "love story" of finding the long-lost 2010 Stingray hiding in
     plain sight on the study bookshelf.  Keep VERBATIM as the opening
     of the Manual's origin chapter.  Source: L88-L119 (approx).
  F  Arduino upload issue was "compiled suspiciously fast, upload never
     finished" - fixed by swapping to a different Arduino.  Chronic
     Arduino symptom; keep as one-liner.

2024.05.07  (L146)  Roberto donates parts.  Wish-list era.
  C  SparkFun TB6612FNG motor driver (superseded by RoboClaw)
  C  Pi Pico (never used in current build)
  C  MPU-6050 IMU (never used; BNO085 is current)
  C  LM2596 buck converter (superseded by 52Pi hat)
  M  Pololu 3779 shunt regulator - still current, prevents regen-brake
     capacitor blow-up.  Manual: keep, cite.
  M  Emergency stop pushbutton and remote kill switch - still current.
  C  YDLidar X2 (later replaced by LD19)
  F  L298N insufficient (1.2A max) for these motors (5-10A needed).  One-
     liner for Field Notes: "Motor driver selection: these Pololu
     gearmotors pull 5-10A stall; anything under 5A per channel is
     underspec."

2024.07.27  (L268)  First encoder wiring saga
  M  RoboClaw motor+encoder wiring table (definitive, still applies):
        Red    Motor power    M1A / M2A terminal
        Black  Motor power    M1B / M2B terminal
        Green  Encoder GND    (-) pin on encoder power header
        Blue   Encoder Vcc    (+) pin on encoder power header
        Yellow Encoder A      ENC1/ENC2 header inside pin
        White  Encoder B      ENC1/ENC2 header outside pin
     Post-it stuck to each motor.  Reproduce in Manual.
  F  Ghost pulses on ENCA even with nothing connected: bare Arduino on
     anti-static mat picks up static / body capacitance.  Symptom
     disappeared when Arduino placed in plastic tray.  Lesson: "if
     encoder pulses appear on unconnected inputs, the board is floating -
     ground / shield before chasing code."
  ?  Motor identified in log as Pololu 4752 (30:1, 12V, 64 CPR helical
     pinion, 979.62 counts / output rev at 20.4:1 gearbox).  Current
     spec in context.txt says "Pololu 50:1 12V 64 CPR encoder = 3200
     count/revolution."  Confirm motor swap date - somewhere later in
     the log the 30:1 was replaced with 50:1.
  M  RoboClaw setup workflow (still current): install BasicMicro Motion
     Studio on Windows, connect via USB, upload RoboClaw Arduino
     library, run PacketSerialSimplePWM to verify.

2025.01.11  (part of the pre-2025.01.12 form-feed block)  RoboClaw + Pi 5 UART
  M  Motion Studio configuration: address 128 (0x80), packet serial mode,
     baud 115200.
  ?  Log has buck converter output set to 12.8V to give RoboClaw 12.0V
     (voltage drop somewhere).  With 52Pi hat now, this whole path is
     gone.  Manual: skip.
  M  Wheel measurement procedure to derive counts-per-meter:
     Measure with Motion Studio: 32,000 encoder pulses in 10 revolutions
     (i.e. 3,200 counts / rev at output shaft, matching 50:1 x 64 CPR).
     Wheel radius 0.0615 m x 2 x pi = 0.3864 m/rev.
     1 / 0.3864 = 2.588 rev/m
     2.588 x 3200 = 8281.57, rounded to 8282 pulses/m.
     ?  Current Essentials says 8226 pulses/m from measured circ 0.389 m,
     radius 0.0619 m.  Minor refinement over time.  Manual: use the
     current 8226 figure but keep the measurement procedure.
  F  "Right motor went faster than left in synchronized PWM before
     tuning; matched after tuning."  Field-note principle: never trust
     open-loop PWM to give equal speeds; velocity tuning is not
     optional.
  F  "Disconnect the RoboClaw from the Pi during Motion Studio velocity
     tuning" - or tuning crashes.  Lesson.
  F  "9V logic battery to RoboClaw during tuning to prevent crashes
     didn't work consistently" - honest note that the workaround was
     unreliable; the fix was disconnecting the Pi entirely.
  M  Pi 5 UART enable procedure (still current on any UART use case):
       sudo raspi-config  ->  Interfaces  ->  enable I2C and Serial
     Older path was to edit /boot/config.txt (enable_uart=1) and
     disable serial-getty@ttyS0.service, but the log notes both were
     reversed and the OSR docs say the getty disable is unnecessary on
     newer Debian.  Manual: use raspi-config, skip the manual edits.
  F  Very first (early) PID values that later got refined:
       old:  m1_p 7.26239 i 1.36838 qpps 2437  (WRONG motor / gearbox)
       mid:  m1_p 1.36867 i 0.21541 qpps 14250  m2_p 1.56561 i 0.24135
              qpps 11812  (post-motor-swap, single-run tune)
       now:  m1_p 1.35657 i 0.19971 qpps 14737  m2_p 1.12181 i 0.17827
              qpps 17381  (10-run AutoTune average, per Essentials)
     Field-note principle: AutoTune single-shot values wander; average
     10 runs for something you can commit to.
  M  Michael Wimble's driver is the ROS 2 RoboClaw driver we use:
       github.com/wimblerobotics/ros2_roboclaw_driver
  F  First driver launch died with SIGSEGV (exit -11).  Fix: device_name
     "/dev/roboclaw" was undefined; changed to "/dev/serial0" and
     recompiled.  Later a udev rule was set up to bind /dev/roboclaw ->
     the actual USB device (see later entries once RoboClaw moved from
     UART to USB).

2025.01.12  (L1031)  OSR cross-pollination and DuPont jumper
  M  DuPont jumper from Pi to RoboClaw: physical improvement worth
     preserving in Manual - "solder Pi-side jumpers into a single DuPont
     housing so the connector cannot fall out and cannot short."
  N  OSR (JPL's Open Source Rover) - James's earlier hobbyist Mars-rover
     build.  Its roboclaw.py + roboclaw_wrapper.py became reference
     material when Stingray hit RoboClaw problems.  The "Stormy the
     Stingray" naming lineage (OSR-era "Audacity" logo work, Stormy
     logo work) belongs in the origin chapter as backstory:
     Stormy is the spiritual sibling of an Audacity-class rover.
     Cross-link when we find the specific OSR references later in the
     log.  Also see: Photos/Audacity Logo/ and Photos/Stormy Logo/ in
     image_index.csv.

2025.02.17 tail  (L~2400)  Nathan at BasicMicro
  N  James emails Nathan at BasicMicro asking for help.  Nathan
     confirms the pinout convention and adds context.  "Nathan at
     BasicMicro" joins the informal support cast alongside Mike
     Wimble, Sergei Grichine, and the various AI assistants.
     Manual origin chapter: name the humans who helped.
  M  RoboClaw model / firmware (definitive, from this exchange):
        Model:    RoboClaw 2x7A V5C
        Firmware: 4.2.8 originally in log; later upgraded to 4.3.6
                  (see 2025.02.18); current Essentials says 4.2.8 -
                  possible downgrade needed?  FLAG NEW #7 below.
  M  RoboClaw <-> Pi wiring convention (Nathan-confirmed, Manual-ready):
        Pi GPIO 14 TXD (physical pin 8)  ->  RoboClaw S1 signal (RC RX)
        Pi GPIO 15 RXD (physical pin 10) ->  RoboClaw S2 signal (RC TX)
        Pi GND (physical pin 6)          ->  RoboClaw S1/S2 GND
        Do NOT connect the 5V (BEC) - both boards are separately powered.
     Set the RoboClaw to Packet Serial in Motion Studio and match the
     Pi's baud (115200).
  F  BasicMicro sample code gotcha: PacketSerialSimplePWM.ino has NO
     stop command.  To halt motors during a test loop you must UNPLUG
     the Arduino.  Add a stop-and-hold step to any test sample before
     running with real motors bolted to a real robot.

2025.02.17 tail cont.  Arduino Uno test succeeds
  F  Arduino Hardware Serial wiring is the STANDARD TX-to-RX pattern:
        Arduino TX (pin 1) -> RoboClaw S1 (RC RX)
        Arduino RX (pin 0) -> RoboClaw S2 (RC TX)
        Arduino GND        -> RoboClaw S1 GND
     Motors spin correctly.  This is the "the RoboClaw definitely
     works, the fault is on the Pi side" datum that unblocks all
     later diagnosis.
  F  When Raspberry Pi wiring instructions and Arduino wiring
     instructions disagree on TX/RX polarity, both may be "right" for
     their own toolchain but ONE will be wrong for you.  Do the
     Arduino test first; use it as the ground truth for correct
     RoboClaw wiring, then match the Pi to it.

2025.02.18  (L2601)  Firmware/bootloader trap; Sergei recommends IMU
  F  RoboClaw firmware upgrade trap (Manual sidebar - REVISED
     2026.07.20 per James: this is actually broader than firmware):
        The BasicMicro MOTION STUDIO APPLICATION ITSELF can break
        things when upgraded to a newer version.  Symptom: after a
        Motion Studio upgrade, communication with a previously
        working RoboClaw fails, or the RoboClaw gets left in
        "bootloader mode" that only the legacy Motion Studio
        recognizes.  Recovery: reinstall an older/legacy version of
        Motion Studio.
     Rule for Stormy going forward: DO NOT upgrade Motion Studio
     unless there is a specific new feature you need.  If you must,
     keep the current-known-good installer archived on disk BEFORE
     upgrading.  This has bitten James at least twice.
     Related, but narrower: updating Motion Studio may prompt to
     upgrade the RoboClaw firmware (4.2.8 -> 4.3.6 in the log).
     If that leaves the RoboClaw in bootloader mode, the legacy
     Motion Studio installer is what recovers it.
  M  IMU option that entered the parts pipeline (Sergei's recommendation
     at HBRC ROS Discussion Group):
        Adafruit BNO055 - 9-DOF Absolute Orientation IMU Fusion
        Breakout, Adafruit product 2472.
     This is the direct ancestor of Stormy's current BNO085.  Manual
     origin chapter should say: "the IMU story starts with Sergei
     recommending the BNO055 at HBRC on 2025.02.18."

2025.02.19  (L2632)  rpi_gpio_test.py dies with same error
  C  Second attempt at rpi_gpio_test.py (BCM 25/26/5/6) - same
     "RuntimeError: Cannot determine SOC peripheral base address"
     error.  Confirms Pi 5 GPIO stack is incompatible with
     python3-rpi.gpio.  Nothing new to add to Chunk 2's Cut.

2025.02.21  (L2687)  Claude-3.5-Sonnet + AD2 oscilloscope loopback
  N  "Unlike before, Claude has gotten stupid."  Reset with
     "Let's start fresh with a cup of Nespresso!"  This session is
     where the "condensed context recap" pattern is invented as a
     survival technique against AI context drift.  Keep as an origin-
     chapter vignette.
  F  Debugging pattern to codify (Manual + Field Notes):
     "One command at a time, wait for output."
     James asked Claude:
        "Can you please give me a series of commands, one-at-a-time,
         that tell you the current status of my system without blindly
         configuring or installing things again so you get the results
         of the first query before going on?"
     This is the same pattern James uses with me today in the
     context.txt style guide.  Origin traced.
  F  UART hardware inventory (Manual reference, Pi 5):
        /dev/serial0 -> /dev/ttys0   Broadcom BCM7271 mini UART
                                     (base_baud = 6,000,000)
        /dev/serial1 -> /dev/ttyAMA10  PL011 rev2 UART (irq 15)
        Boot-time dmesg confirms both are initialized.  Ownership
        root:tty, mode 0660.  ubuntu user is in tty and dialout
        groups by default on 24.04.
  F  "Test the tester first" methodology:
        Before connecting the DUT (device under test), do a loopback
        on the test instrument itself.
        AD2 procedure:
          1. Jumper DIO 0 to DIO 1.  Common GND.
          2. WaveForms -> Protocol tab -> UART sub-tab.
          3. TX (DIO 0), RX (DIO 1), 115200, 8N1, standard polarity.
          4. Send&Receive mode; type "Hello"; confirm hex echoes on
             both TX and RX columns (h48 h65 h6C h6C h6F).
        Only after that pattern echoes should you fan out to the DUT.
  M  RaspberryPi 5 physical UART pinout (corrected; Manual-ready):
        Pin 6  (GND)     ->  external GND
        Pin 8  (GPIO 14, TXD) -> external RX (or AD2 DIO 1)
        Pin 10 (GPIO 15, RXD) -> external TX (or AD2 DIO 0)
     Standard "TX to their RX, RX from their TX" cross-over.
  F  AD2 hardware sanity check: the AD2's internal LEDs illuminate
     ONLY when the WaveForms application is running, not when USB-
     plugged alone.  If WaveForms is closed and the LEDs are dark,
     that's expected, not a fault.
  F  "Device or resource busy" on /dev/serial1 diagnostic recipe:
        sudo lsof | grep tty       # who holds it?
        ps aux | grep tty          # confirm process
     A stale `screen /dev/ttyAMA10 115200` from an earlier test can
     hold the port invisibly for hours.
        sudo killall screen
     clears it.  Field Notes: "if a serial device is 'busy,' someone
     is already talking to it - usually you, from an earlier terminal."
  F  AI-in-a-loop failure mode: Claude-3.5-Sonnet forgot the entire
     established context (assumed Pi 5 was CM4, then Pi 4).  Wasted
     an evening.  Countermeasure: end each such session with an
     explicit "recap in a form I can paste into a fresh chat"
     request.  This same countermeasure produced the netplan recipe
     (2025.02.14) and eventually context.txt itself.

2025.02.22  (L3617 - BEGINNING of a huge entry that runs to L5492)
     Fresh session with the recap-first pattern
  N  "Good morning!  I've had my coffee.  Before I get down with my
     voltmeter and oscilloscope, would you please recap for us in a
     very condensed output suitable for cutting/pasting for future
     reference: (1) the environment, (2) the overall problem, (3)
     what we've established via system commands."
     This IS the recap-first pattern in action.  Manual origin
     chapter: this is the template.
  M  Pi 5 UART fundamental differences from Pi 3/4 (Manual - CRITICAL
     for anyone porting Pi-3/4 UART code):
        - UART numbering renumbered:
             Pi 4 UART0 (PL011)  ->  Pi 5 UART4
             Pi 4 miniUART       ->  Pi 5 UART5
             New UART0..UART3 added on Pi 5.
        - Default GPIO pin mappings changed: GPIO 14/15 (physical
          pins 8/10) are NO LONGER auto-assigned to any UART.  They
          require explicit device-tree overlay configuration.
        - /dev naming reflects this: ttyAMA10 (not ttyAMA0), and
          ttys0 (lowercase, not ttyS0).
     Implication for James in this saga: previous Pi 3/4 tutorials do
     not port.  This is why nothing worked with defaults.
  F  Diagnostic commands specific to Pi GPIO/UART mapping (Manual
     reference):
        gpioinfo                    # dumps all GPIO controllers and
                                    # their function assignments
        raspi-gpio get 14           # single-pin status query
        raspi-gpio get 15
     Note: gpioinfo requires appropriate GPIO group membership or
     sudo; "Permission denied" is a group issue not a missing tool.
  ?  gpioinfo says "Permission denied" for user ubuntu on 2025.02.22.
     Was there a groups fix in a later entry?  Watch for it; it may
     be a straight "add ubuntu to gpio group" step.

CANDIDATE FIELD-NOTES CLUSTERS growing
================================================================
Pi 5 UART (dedicated cluster; this is the highest-density lesson zone):
  * Device names: /dev/ttys0 (lowercase) and /dev/ttyAMA10
  * UART numbering completely different from Pi 3/4:
      old UART0 (PL011) is now UART4;
      miniUART is now UART5;
      new UART0..3 added
  * GPIO 14/15 NOT auto-assigned to any UART - explicit overlay
    required (dtoverlay in /boot/firmware/config.txt)
  * python3-rpi.gpio is not compatible; use gpioinfo / raspi-gpio
  * The GPIO UART path was ultimately abandoned for the RoboClaw;
    Stormy uses USB.  UART chapter is preserved as appendix.

RoboClaw specifics (updated):
  * (existing items from Chunks 1-2)
  * V5C is the model; firmware currently 4.2.8 per context.txt (see
    flag 7)
  * Pi<->RC wiring: Pi TX (pin 8) -> RC S1; Pi RX (pin 10) -> RC S2;
    Pi GND (pin 6) -> RC GND; no 5V
  * Firmware upgrade via new Motion Studio can strand device in
    bootloader mode; keep legacy Motion Studio available
  * BasicMicro test sketches have no stop command - add one before
    running with real motors

Human-process:
  * (existing) AI in circles = walk away
  * (existing) Image the drive after any working install
  * (existing) Ask for "recap the exact steps" once problem solved
  * (existing) Vendor sample code lags real hardware/OS combos
  * (NEW) Test the tester first (loopback the instrument, then DUT)
  * (NEW) "Serial busy" means someone else has the port - lsof + ps
  * (NEW) At the end of any drift-prone AI session, ask for a
    condensed context recap you could paste into a fresh chat.
    This IS the origin of context.txt.
  * (NEW) Do the Arduino sanity test first when RoboClaw comms fail
    from the Pi.  Arduino success proves the RoboClaw is fine and
    the fault is on the Pi side.  Cuts the search space in half.

The named human collaborators to preserve in the Manual origin chapter:
  * Roberto (bespoke robot, parts donation, ChatGPT prompt seeds)
  * Michael Wimble (ros2_roboclaw_driver, gdb debugging cascade)
  * Sergei Grichine (netplan, cloud-init, IMU recommendation, fuses)
  * Nathan at BasicMicro (RoboClaw wiring confirmation, model info)
  * Poe/GPT-4o (early Python tutoring, first driver help)
  * Claude-3.5-Sonnet (netplan recipe; UART oscilloscope adventure)
  * GitHub Copilot (VS Code partner; roboclaw.py explanation)
  * HBRC ROS Discussion Group (the meeting place)

CHUNK 4  lines 3700 to ~5500  finishes the giant 2025.02.22 entry
         through the almost-empty 2025.02.23.
Theme: The Pi 5 UART overlay rat's nest.  Total defeat despite doing
       everything "right."  Two days of dead ends, then silence.
       Whatever eventually made the GPIO UART work on Pi 5 is
       NOT in this chunk.  Watch for it in later chunks.

2025.02.22 continued  (L3700-L5490)  Pi 5 UART overlay saga
  M  Pi 5 gpiochip mapping (Manual appendix, verified 2025.02.22):
        gpiochip0  = 2712 (Broadcom BCM2712 SoC-side controller)
                     Contains BT / WiFi / boot-flash / power-button
                     lines; no header pins.
        gpiochip1  = WiFi SDIO
        gpiochip2  = RP1 southbridge internal (SD card, PMIC, HDMI I2C)
        gpiochip3  = PMIC-side (HDMI SCL/SDA, PMIC SCL/SDA)
        gpiochip4  = the 40-pin header GPIOs
                     lines 2..27 map to header GPIO2..GPIO27
                     lines 14 and 15 are GPIO14 (pin 8) and GPIO15
                     (pin 10) - the "UART pins"
     Diagnostic command: sudo gpioinfo.  Without sudo, "Permission
     denied" - user needs gpio-group membership or sudo.
  F  Pi 5 UART overlay attempts (exhaustive summary, all FAILED to
     bring GPIO 14/15 up as UART on the Pi 5, per this session):
        Attempt 1:  dtoverlay=uart4    dtparam=uart4=on
                    -> creates /dev/ttyAMA4
                    -> GPIO 14/15 still show as "unused" in gpioinfo
        Attempt 2:  dtoverlay=uart0
                    -> creates /dev/ttyAMA0
                    -> GPIO 14/15 STILL "unused"
        Attempt 3:  dtoverlay=uart0    gpio=14,15=a0
                    (explicit ALT function 0 request)
                    -> no change; GPIO 14/15 still "unused"
        Attempt 4:  dtoverlay=uart0,tx=14,rx=15
                    (explicit pin assignment in the overlay)
                    -> /dev/ttyAMA0 exists, stty succeeds silently,
                       dmesg shows: "1f00030000.serial: ttyAMA0 at
                       MMIO 0x1f00030000 (irq = 128, base_baud = 0)
                       is a PL011 AXI"
                    -> BUT a physical GPIO14<->GPIO15 jumper loopback
                       still returns NO DATA.
     Chunk conclusion: on the Pi 5 running Ubuntu 24.04 with RP1
     southbridge, the standard dtoverlay recipes that work on Pi 3/4
     do not reliably route UART0 to the header pins even when both
     the device node and the kernel driver appear healthy.
     Field-Notes headline: "This is how HARD the Pi 5 GPIO UART
     fight is."  The winning configuration (which is what Stormy
     actually runs today - RoboClaw IS on Pi GPIOs, not USB, per
     James 2026.07.20) is NOT in this chunk.  Continue reading to
     find the fix.  Every attempt above is a valid "we tried this,
     it did not work by itself" reference.
  F  Silent-stty trap (Manual sidebar):
        stty -F /dev/ttyAMA0 <options>   returns silently on success.
     SILENCE IS NOT PROOF THE UART WORKS.  stty configures a Linux
     tty device abstraction; it does not verify the underlying pins
     are electrically live.  The only reliable proof is a physical
     jumper between TX and RX with a Python (or minicom) script that
     writes bytes and reads them back.
  F  CTS/DSR false-positive: an unconnected UART typically reports
     CTS: True and DSR: True in pyserial (because the input lines
     float high).  Do NOT interpret "CTS asserted" as "handshake OK"
     unless a real peer is on the other end.
  F  dmesg is the phantom-UART detector:
        sudo dmesg | grep -i tty
     shows every UART the kernel registered at boot.  On this Pi 5:
        ttyAMA10 at MMIO 0x107d001000 (irq 15, PL011 rev2)
        ttyS0    at MMIO 0x107d50c000 (irq 40, Broadcom BCM7271)
        ttyAMA0  at MMIO 0x1f00030000 (irq 128, PL011 AXI)
     ttyAMA0 registers as a real device even when the overlay fails
     to route it to header pins.  Ghost peripheral.  Field Notes:
     "device node existence is not the same as pin routing."
  F  AI-configuration-thrash antipattern (recorded from this session):
     Claude-3.5-Sonnet iterated 4-5 different dtoverlay syntaxes
     without ever pausing to test the fundamental hypothesis that
     "maybe the Pi 5 RP1 UART routing simply doesn't accept these
     older overlay names."  After 2 or 3 same-category attempts fail,
     STOP and challenge the assumption before trying a fourth
     variant.  Rule of thumb (for us, going forward): "Three in a
     row means change categories."

2025.02.23  (L5492)  Wilderness low point
  N  Entry is essentially empty in this range - just the date on
     its own line.  The prolonged Claude-3.5-Sonnet session
     concluded with no working UART.  Two days of dedicated
     debugging, nothing to show.  Preserve as one line in the

CANDIDATE FIELD-NOTES CLUSTERS growing
================================================================
Pi 5 UART (this cluster is now the most substantial):
  * Device names: /dev/ttys0 (lowercase), /dev/ttyAMA10, /dev/ttyAMA0
    (when dtoverlay=uart0 is set)
  * UART numbering completely different from Pi 3/4:
      Pi 4 UART0 (PL011) is now UART4 on Pi 5
      miniUART is UART5
      New UART0..3 added
  * GPIO 14/15 do NOT auto-map to any UART.  Explicit overlays are
    required.
  * Even explicit overlays (uart0, uart0,tx=14,rx=15, gpio=14,15=a0)
    FAIL to route UART0 to header pins on Pi 5 with Ubuntu 24.04
    + RP1 southbridge WHEN TRIED IN ISOLATION.  Physical loopback
    confirms no data on the wire.
  * python3-rpi.gpio is not compatible; use gpioinfo / raspi-gpio.
  * DESPITE THE ABOVE, Stormy's RoboClaw does today run over the
    Pi 5's GPIO UART (per James, 2026.07.20).  The winning
    configuration is somewhere in the log after 2025.02.23.  Watch
    for it and cite it as the definitive Manual entry when found.
    Every one of the failing attempts above becomes a "tried this
    first, did not work" bullet in a proper Manual walkthrough of
    the fix.
  * Diagnostic sequence when suspicious of a UART:
      1. sudo dmesg | grep -i tty       (was it registered at boot?)
      2. ls -l /dev/serial* /dev/ttyAMA*  (is the device node present?)
      3. sudo gpioinfo                   (are the pins actually
                                         assigned to UART function,
                                         or "unused"?)
      4. Physical GPIO14-GPIO15 jumper + Python loopback
         (does data actually go around?)
     Only step 4 is proof.

Human process (updated):
  * (existing) AI in circles = walk away; sleep on it
  * (existing) Image the drive after any working install
  * (existing) Ask for "recap the exact steps" once problem solved
  * (existing) Vendor sample code lags real hardware/OS combos
  * (existing) Test the tester first (loopback the instrument, then DUT)
  * (existing) "Serial busy" means someone else has the port: lsof + ps
  * (existing) Do the Arduino sanity test first when RoboClaw comms
    fail from the Pi
  * (NEW)  Silence is not success.  A silent stty return is not
    proof; nor is CTS/DSR True on an unconnected line.  Only a
    physical loopback with byte round-trip proves a serial device
    works.
  * (NEW)  Three same-category attempts in a row = change categories.
    When configuration variants of the same idea keep failing, the
    fifth variant is unlikely to help.  Challenge the assumption
    that produced them all.

2025.03.02  (L6156)  Grok era begins; FIRST WORKING UART
  N  "Then saw Joe Rogan interview with Elon Musk and thought I'd
     try Grok via X.  First chat timed out and I lost it.  Lost
     the second one too.  Third time accessed directly at grok.com
     and was able to maintain the thread."  Add Grok to the named
     collaborators list.  Note: Grok-via-X was context-flaky; Grok
     direct at grok.com was more stable.  Field Note.
  F  Digilent AD2 (Analog Discovery 2) gotchas learned:
        (a) Analog scope leads (C1, C2 - orange/blue with black
            ground clip) are DIFFERENT from digital protocol leads
            (DIO 0, DIO 1 in UART Protocol mode).  Wiring the wrong
            leads produces no signal regardless of what the target
            sends.
        (b) Analog scope default sweep rate of 1 ns/div is too fast
            to see UART bit transitions.  Raise to 100 ms/div (or
            less) to see the byte-level spikes.
        (c) T-lead trick: use two-female-one-male jumpers so the
            same signal can feed both the analog scope input AND
            the digital protocol input simultaneously.  BETTER:
            build X-leads (two-male jumpers), one for each scope
            window - avoids the toggle-between-windows workflow.
  M  ***FIRST WORKING UART on Stingray (Manual-grade):***
        Overlay: dtoverlay=uart2-pi5
        Pinmux:  gpio=4=alt5, gpio=5=alt5
        Device:  /dev/ttyAMA2 at 115200 baud
        Wiring:  Pi GPIO 4 (physical pin 7)  -> RoboClaw S1
                 Pi GPIO 5 (physical pin 29) -> RoboClaw S2
                 Pi GND                       -> RoboClaw GND
        Verify:
              from roboclaw import Roboclaw
              rc = Roboclaw("/dev/ttyAMA2", 115200)
              rc.Open()
              print(rc.ReadVersion(128))
        First-ever success output on Stormy:
              "USB Roboclaw 2x7a v4.2.8"
     ROBOCLAW FIRMWARE CONFIRMED: v4.2.8 (as in context.txt).
     Retract flag 7's "was firmware upgraded to 4.3.6?" - the log
     shows the RoboClaw is still on 4.2.8 as of this stardate.
     Whatever happened on 2025.02.18 (upgrade to 4.3.6) was either
     rolled back via the legacy Motion Studio recovery path or
     never took effect.  Flag 7 RESOLVED.
  F  Two-motor asymmetry noticed 2025.03.02: with rc.ForwardM1 for
     2 s and rc.ForwardM1(0) to stop, right wheel spun ~2 s but
     left wheel only ran ~1 s (rotations appeared roughly equal
     though).  Not diagnosed in this chunk.  Watch for follow-up.

2025.03.03  (L6374)  Grok's analysis of why GPIO 14/15 still won't
  F  Root-cause finding for the earlier chunks' failures (Grok-
     produced, worth preserving as diagnostic wisdom):
        "Ubuntu 24.04 on Pi 5 does NOT honor the UART0 pinmux
         settings the way Raspberry Pi OS does.  config.txt
         overlays and DTB pinctrl entries pointing at GPIO 14/15
         under UART0 are consistently ignored (they route to
         alt4=UART2 CTS/RTS or stay 'unused')."
     This is the specific failure mode chunks 3-4 kept hitting.
     UART2 on GPIO 4/5 works precisely because it takes an
     unclaimed pin path.
  M  Motor-move test recipe (Manual-ready):
        from roboclaw import Roboclaw
        import time
        rc = Roboclaw("/dev/ttyAMA2", 115200)
        rc.Open()
        rc.ForwardM1(128, 64)   # right wheel half speed
        time.sleep(2)
        rc.ForwardM1(128, 0)    # stop
     Scope observation of motor_test.py at 100 ms/div:
        h80 h00 h40 h73 h9E hFF h80 h00 h40 h73 h9E hFF ...
     ForwardM1 packet decoded: 0x80=address, 0x00=cmd, 0x40=speed,
     h73 h9E hFF = CRC and framing.  Keep the packet-observation
     technique as a Field Note.

2025.03.06  (L6566)  ***MICHAEL WIMBLE'S EMAIL - THE PI 5 CONFIG SECRET***
  M  THE definitive Pi 5 UART config for Stingray (per Michael Wimble,
     2025.03.05, Manual-grade - THIS IS THE ANSWER TO THE ENTIRE
     UART SAGA):
        Edit /boot/firmware/config.txt.
        Add at the END of the file:
              [pi5]
              dtoverlay=uart0-pi5
        The "[pi5]" section header is REQUIRED.  Grok had tried the
        exact same dtoverlay under "[all]" and it was silently
        ignored.  There is "some magic to [pi5]" (Mike's words).
     Complete post-boot setup:
        sudo adduser ubuntu dialout
        sudo chmod a+rw /dev/ttyAMA0
        ls -l /dev/ttyAMA*   # confirm /dev/ttyAMA0 present, dialout
     Wiring (Mike-confirmed):
        Pi pin 6  (GND)          -> RoboClaw any (-) minus pin
        Pi pin 8  (GPIO 14, TXD) -> RoboClaw S1
        Pi pin 10 (GPIO 15, RXD) -> RoboClaw S2
     ***This is the Manual's headline entry for Compute Interface
     -> RoboClaw wiring.  Cite Michael Wimble email 2025.03.05.***
  M  ***BAUD-RATE SECRET (per Michael Wimble):***
     ros2_roboclaw_driver internally uses 38400 baud, NOT the
     115200 James had set on the RoboClaw via Motion Studio.  This
     is UNDOCUMENTED in the driver README as of this stardate.
     Fix: reset the RoboClaw's Packet Serial baud in Motion Studio
     to 38400 to match the driver.  Everything then talks.
     Manual: put "38400 baud" in the definitive RoboClaw config
     block right next to "address 128, packet serial."
  M  Mike's minimal 2-terminal UART sanity test (Manual sidebar):
        Term 1:  stty -F /dev/ttyAMA0 speed 38400
                 cat </dev/ttyAMA0
        Term 2:  printf "\x80\x15" >/dev/ttyAMA0
     Ideally, Term 1 prints the RoboClaw version string.  If no
     version string but the scope shows the TX byte, wiring/baud
     are the remaining suspects.  Field Notes: "test the UART
     using shell tools before invoking the ROS 2 driver.  Cuts
     search space enormously."
  F  Even with Mike's exact config applied, ros2_roboclaw_driver
     still threw ReadVersion timeouts on James's Stingray Pi.
     Diagnosis emerging: "molested by multiple AIs" - the Pi's
     configuration state had drifted so far from clean that even
     a correct config on top of it did not work.  Cascade lesson
     leads directly to the 2025.03.08 clean-reinstall entry.

2025.03.07  (L6686)  rosdep, driver build; Grok "bad info" trust
              collapse
  M  rosdep is a ROS 2 tool, NOT an apt package
     (python3-rosdep2 doesn't exist and isn't the answer).  Correct
     install (after ROS 2 Jazzy is installed):
        sudo rosdep init
        rosdep update
        rosdep install --from-paths src --ignore-src -r -y
     Do not chase "package has no installation candidate" errors
     for rosdep on a system without ROS 2 - install ROS 2 first.
  F  "Grok is giving me bad info.  Messed up badly on GPIO pin
     assignments.  My confidence is shaken!"  Field Note: even a
     productive AI collaborator can produce a specific wrong fact
     that undermines trust.  Cross-check any AI-provided pin/wire
     assignment against a datasheet before soldering or applying
     power.

2025.03.08  (L6788)  Clean install + Mike's config; VITAL STATS crystallized
  F  "Since Mike ran successfully on a virgin Pi5 and mine has

     WHEN AN AI HAS TRIED SO MANY CONFIG CHANGES THAT YOU CANNOT
     REASON ABOUT THE CURRENT STATE, THE FASTEST PATH FORWARD IS
     A CLEAN OS REINSTALL.  Cost: a few hours.  Benefit: a
     baseline you can reason about.  This is the "AI-thrash
     recovery" pattern; every long AI collaboration on system
     config needs a snapshot-before-experimenting discipline
     (Chunk 2, 2025.02.16) AND a reinstall-as-recovery discipline
     (this stardate).

     Adafruit now recommends BNO085 as the successor; James will
     later swap to BNO085.  Keep BNO055 procedure in Manual
     appendix, BNO085 procedure in current-hardware chapter):
        Adafruit calibration tutorial:
          learn.adafruit.com/adafruit-bno055-absolute-orientation-
          sensor
        Wire per Adafruit tutorial.
        Load Adafruit Arduino demo (does 3D orientation).
        Close Arduino (else it hogs the serial port), then open the
          Chrome 3D visualizer and click Connect.
        Alternate calibration path: MATLAB (James used his Houston
          Community College ID) - run the arduinoObj +
          bno055(...,'ndof') pipeline; instrument reports
          Accelerometer / Gyroscope / Magnetometer calibrated
          in turn.
  F  IMU mounting gotcha (Manual sidebar; carried forward for
     BNO085 too):
        "The x, y, z axes of the BREAKOUT BOARD are NOT the x, y, z
         axes of the CHIP."
     Whatever axis-swap or sign flip you set in code must account
     for BOTH the chip-to-board orientation AND the board-to-robot
     mount orientation.  Verify with the manual by tilting the
     robot in each axis and confirming the expected sign appears
     on /imu/data.  This is a recurring source of confusion for
     both BNO055 and BNO085.

================================================================
CANDIDATE FIELD-NOTES CLUSTERS growing
================================================================
Pi 5 UART (now with the ANSWER):
  * [pi5] section header magic - the config lines only apply if
    they're under [pi5], not [all].
  * dtoverlay=uart0-pi5 (Pi-5-specific overlay name; the plain
    'uart0' overlay does not route pins correctly)
  * dtoverlay=uart2-pi5 with gpio=4=alt5, gpio=5=alt5 also works
    if you prefer /dev/ttyAMA2 on GPIO 4/5
  * 8250.nr_uarts= comes from the DTB, not cmdline.txt.  Interesting
    diagnostic knowledge, but NOT part of Stormy's working config.
    Only worth invoking as an appendix if you need more than one
    UART on Pi 5 for reasons unrelated to this build.

RoboClaw specifics (now with definitive config):
  * Address 128, Packet Serial, 38400 baud (NOT 115200 for use
    with the Wimble driver; DOCUMENT THIS in the Manual)
  * Firmware 4.2.8
  * Motion Studio: enable "Autodetect Max Main Battery Plus"
  * Motion Studio: 4 Cell (12V) battery cutoff
  * MOTION STUDIO APP-VERSION TRAP: upgrading the Motion Studio
    application itself can break communication with a previously
    working RoboClaw.  Keep a known-good legacy Motion Studio
    installer archived; revert to it if a new version misbehaves.
    Do not casually upgrade Motion Studio.

Human process (updated):
  * (NEW)  When multiple AIs have "molested" the config to the
    point you can't reason about the current state, the fastest
    path forward is a full clean OS reinstall + apply only the
    known-working config + reboot.  Faster than trying to unwind
    the drift.
  * (NEW)  When switching AI assistants, ask the outgoing one for
    an ENVIRONMENT / CONFIGURATION / PROBLEM / FAILED APPROACHES /
    CRITICAL CONTEXT handoff note.  Paste it as the opening
    message to the new AI.
  * (NEW)  Cross-check any AI-provided pin/wire assignment against
    a datasheet before soldering or applying power.

AD2 oscilloscope wisdom:
  * Analog scope (C1, C2) and digital protocol (DIO 0, DIO 1) use
    DIFFERENT lead sets.
  * Analog default sweep at 1 ns/div is too fast for UART bytes;
    use 100 ms/div or less.
  * T-leads (2F-1M) or X-leads (2M) let one signal feed both the
    analog scope and the digital protocol view simultaneously.

IMU (BNO055 arrives; BNO085 later):
  * Adafruit calibration flow (Arduino demo + Chrome 3D visualizer).
  * MATLAB path via Houston CC ID for calibration.
  * "Chip axes are NOT board axes are NOT robot axes" - always
    tilt-test after wiring.

The named collaborators list (updated):
  * Roberto, Michael Wimble, Sergei Grichine, Nathan at BasicMicro,
    Poe/GPT-4o, Claude-3.5-Sonnet, GPT-4o (second time), Grok
    (via X, then via grok.com direct), GitHub Copilot,
    HBRC ROS Discussion Group / HBRC mailing list.

2025.03.15 tail  Michael Wimble's USB revelation
  N  HBRC email posted (chunk 5 tail).  Michael Wimble replied off-
     list with a bombshell:
        "Don't forget that my code talks to the RoboClaw via USB.
         Just plug it in via the USB cable to the Pi and point the
         device to the USB port in /dev.  No need to set speed as
         USB uses a virtual terminal at maximum speed."
     After a month of Pi 5 UART pain, this was a valid alternative
     path.  Mike's own path had been Pi 4 for UART, or USB for Pi 5.
  F  RoboClaw via USB - first-time detection recipe (Manual sidebar
     for the USB-over-UART alternative):
        1. lsusb   before plugging RoboClaw
        2. Plug RoboClaw USB cable to Pi USB port
        3. lsusb   after - should show:
             ID 03eb:2404 Atmel Corp. The Micro
        4. ls /dev/ttyA*   should now include /dev/ttyACM0
        5. Point ros2_roboclaw_driver at /dev/ttyACM0 (or whichever
           ttyACM number appears)
        6. No baud rate needed - USB CDC virtual comport auto-
           negotiates (typically ~500 kbps)
     RoboClaw User Manual p.50 (verbatim, worth quoting in Manual):
        USB Power: "The USB RoboClaw is self-powered.  It receives
        NO power from the USB cable.  The USB RoboClaw must be
        externally powered to function."
     Result on this stardate: FIRST successful ReadVersion via USB:
        "USB Roboclaw 2x7a v4.2.8"  (yes, that string is literal -
        the leading "USB" is part of the version response even
        over UART, because the RoboClaw's firmware self-identifies
        that way; do not confuse it with the connection medium).
  F  Even with USB working, ros2_roboclaw_driver still threw
     ReadByteWithTimeout errors after ReadVersion succeeded, and
     teleop_twist_keyboard produced only tiny random motor twitches.
     Root cause: not yet clear at this stardate.  Eventually
     resolved by the switch to UART1 on 2025.03.17 and the PiRewrite
     driver branch on 2025.03.23.  Cross-reference here for the
     reader who tries the USB path.

2025.03.16  (L~7550)  Michael Wimble's updated README
  M  MICHAEL WIMBLE'S DEFINITIVE PI 5 UART DOC (Manual-grade, cite in
     Manual as: "Following Michael Wimble's README section 'Enabling
     UARTs on the Raspberry Pi', published to
     github.com/wimblerobotics/ros2_roboclaw_driver on 2025.03.16"):
        In /boot/firmware/config.txt, add at the END:
              [pi5]
              dtoverlay=uart0-pi5      # enables UART0
              dtoverlay=uart1-pi5      # enables UART1
              # include only the UART(s) you actually need
        UART0 pins: GPIO14 (TXD, physical pin 8),
                    GPIO15 (RXD, physical pin 10)
        UART1 pins: GPIO0  (ID_SD, physical pin 27),
                    GPIO1  (ID_SC, physical pin 28)
        Device nodes:
              UART0 -> /dev/ttyAMA0
              UART1 -> /dev/ttyAMA1
              (ignore /dev/ttyAMA10 for this purpose)
        Group membership (do this only after enabling):
              whoami
              sudo su
              usermod -a -G dialout <yourname>
              exit
              # log out and back in for group change to take effect
              groups   # verify dialout is listed
        UART sanity test (minicom already installed):
              # jumper the enabled UART's TX to its RX
              minicom -D /dev/ttyAMA0   # or /dev/ttyAMA1
              # typed characters should echo
              # exit: Ctrl-A, then Q, then Enter at "Leave?"
        Wiring for RoboClaw (either UART):
              Pi TX  -> RoboClaw S1
              Pi RX  -> RoboClaw S2
              Pi GND -> RoboClaw GND
              RoboClaw needs its own motor power to respond.

2025.03.17  (~L7550-L7800)  ***THE SHOCKING DIAGNOSIS***
  ?  RESOLVED HERE.  On James's specific Pi 5, UART0/GPIO14 TX pin
     is PHYSICALLY BROKEN.  Diagnostic:
        Enable UART0 (dtoverlay=uart0-pi5, [pi5]).  Jumper GPIO14
        (pin 8) to GPIO15 (pin 10).  minicom -D /dev/ttyAMA0
        -> NO echo.  Scope: no TX activity on GPIO14.
        Enable UART1 (dtoverlay=uart1-pi5, [pi5]).  Jumper GPIO0
        (pin 27) to GPIO1 (pin 28).  minicom -D /dev/ttyAMA1
        -> characters echo correctly.  Scope shows expected TX
        activity on GPIO0.
     CONCLUSION (from log): "TX on GPIO14 is broken."  This is a
     hardware fault on James's individual Pi 5 board, not a
     universal Pi 5 UART limitation.  Other Pi 5s (Michael Wimble's,
     Marco's per the config Marco emailed) have UART0/GPIO14/15
     working.
     ***This changes the Manual conclusion from Chunk 5.***
     See CORRECTIONS TO EARLIER CHUNKS section below.
  M  ***STORMY'S ACTUAL WORKING UART CONFIG (Manual-grade,
     definitive; supersedes Chunk 5's "GPIO 14/15" answer):***
        In /boot/firmware/config.txt at the end:
              [pi5]
              dtoverlay=uart1-pi5
        Physical wiring:
              Pi pin 6  (GND)         -> RoboClaw S1/S2 GND
              Pi pin 27 (GPIO 0, ID_SD, UART1 TX) -> RoboClaw S1
              Pi pin 28 (GPIO 1, ID_SC, UART1 RX) -> RoboClaw S2
              (do NOT wire the 5V BEC)
        Device node used by the ros2_roboclaw_driver:
              device_name: "/dev/ttyAMA1"
              baud_rate: 38400
     If someone tries this on a different Pi 5 and UART1 doesn't
     appear, they can try `uart0-pi5` on the same [pi5] line
     with wiring shifted to pins 8/10 (GPIO 14/15).
  F  First end-to-end teleop success on this stardate:
        `ros2 launch ros2_roboclaw_driver ros2_roboclaw_driver.launch.py`
        `ros2 run teleop_twist_keyboard teleop_twist_keyboard`
     "BUT MOTORS TURN A LITTLE BIT IN RESPONSE TO i-fwd, ,-back,
      j-left, k-right!!"  This is Stormy's first drive.
     Still has CRC errors ("Invalid ACK response") between successes
     - the PiRewrite branch and clean install later cleaned this up.
  N  Marco's config-file email (fellow HBRC responder) confirmed

2025.03.23  (L7802)  ***NVMe SSD GIFT + PiRewrite BRANCH***
  N  Roberto gifted James an Inland TN446 512GB NVMe SSD +
     ElectroCookie PCIe to M.2 KEY-M NVMe SSD Shield.  Installed
     Ubuntu 24.04 on the SSD.  Second boot-media era begins.
     (Note: the Handoff Note mentions Ediloca EN600 PRO SSD as the
     "current" SSD.  Both Inland and Ediloca show up in the log.
     Watch for a later swap of the Inland for the Ediloca.)
  M  Boot from NVMe SSD instead of microSD (Manual entry, verified
     2025.03.23):
        sudo rpi-eeprom-config --edit
     Change:
        BOOT_ORDER=0xf461   ->   BOOT_ORDER=0xf416
     The digits are read RIGHT TO LEFT.  0x_461 means "try SD first,
     then USB, then NVMe";  0x_416 means "try NVMe first, then USB,
     then SD."
        sudo reboot
     After boot, verify with lsblk that / is mounted from nvme0n1.
  M  SSD vs SD performance (Manual reference, verified numbers):
        Timing               SD (mmcblk0p2)   SSD (nvme0n1p1)
        Cached reads:        1,422 MB/sec     1,854 MB/sec
        Buffered reads:         23 MB/sec       434 MB/sec
     Buffered-read speedup ~19x.  This is why the SSD upgrade is
     worthwhile.
  M  Michael Wimble launched a rewrite of ros2_roboclaw_driver
     specifically for Pi setups on the branch `PiRewrite`:
        git clone https://github.com/wimblerobotics/ \
                  ros2_roboclaw_driver.git
        cd ros2_roboclaw_driver
        git checkout PiRewrite
        colcon build --symlink-install
     Adds `baud_rate` and `do_debug` / `do_low_level_debug` params.
     Debug output includes Read: / Write: byte traces which is what
     let James see byte-for-byte what was happening on the wire.
     This branch is the version that finally worked reliably.
  M  Full comm success on the PiRewrite branch (Manual - this is
     the first-ever "everything works" launch of the driver):
        ReadFirmwareVersion:
              WROTE: 80 15
              READ:  55 53 42 20 52 6F 62 6F 63 6C 61 77 20 32 78
                     37 61 20 76 34 2E 32 2E 38 0A 00 AC 9E
              RESULT: 'USB Roboclaw 2x7a v4.2.8'
        Then successful SetPid, SetEncoderValue, ReadEncoder,
        ReadEncoderSpeed, ReadTemperature, ReadStatus,
        ReadMainBatteryVoltage (18.5 V), ReadLogicBatteryVoltage
        (3.4 V).
  F  Motor wiring convention (Manual, corrected here):
        M1 = LEFT wheel
        M2 = RIGHT wheel
     James had them reversed initially (M1=right, M2=left).  R/L
     turn commands from teleop_twist_keyboard came out backwards.
     Fix:
        (a) Swap motor power leads M1<->M2 at the RoboClaw
        (b) Swap encoder connectors M1<->M2 at the RoboClaw
        (c) Verify with Motion Studio that forward-command produces
            forward physical motion on the correct side
        (d) Re-run AutoTune (PD-tune per HBRC)
     ***Resolves flag 9 (left/right asymmetry from 2025.03.02):***
     the "right wheel ran 2s, left wheel ran 1s" observation was

2025.03.27  (L8559)  M1 runaway; logic-battery-high error
  F  RoboClaw error LED symptom decoder (Manual sidebar - EXPANDED
     from Chunk 2's 2x-blink entry; add this to the same sidebar):
        3 flashes on ERR LED = Logic Battery High (>6.5 V measured
                               at S1/S2 5V pin or logic battery
                               connector).  Motors will freeze.
        Recovery: power-cycle the RoboClaw.  If it recurs, check
                  the logic-battery source voltage.
        (In this incident, the 5V rail read 7.6 V briefly, which
         crossed the 6.5 V threshold.  Cause not fully diagnosed;
         suspect a transient from earlier connection swaps.)
  F  AutoTune anomaly to watch for (Manual sidebar):
        Normal AutoTune behavior: the wheel rocks back and forth a
        few times then settles.
        Abnormal behavior: the wheel rocks then makes a couple of
        FULL revolutions before settling.  This has been correlated
        (in this session) with a bad current-limit setting or the
        logic-battery-high fault above.  Do not accept the tuning
        values from an abnormal AutoTune - reset the RoboClaw,
        verify current limits, and re-run.
  ?  This chunk is the closest so far to a motor-related fault event
     that could match James's "burnout from permissive current +
     carpet-hair stall" flag 1 description.  But the actual failure
     mode here is logic-battery-high (recoverable, no damage),
     NOT a stall-current winding burn.  Still watching for the
     actual burnout entry.
  C  Reference to Pololu 4752 30:1 as "POTENTIAL STINGRAY MOTOR
     REPLACEMENT" - explicitly a candidate, not adopted at this
     time.  Cut (this is one of the SKU-confusion moments James
     called out).  Manual carries only the current 50:1 spec.

2025.03.29  (L8963)  stingray.launch.py debug; Segfault Doom
  F  Common Python IndentationErrors when commenting out big
     blocks (Manual sidebar):
        When you `# `-comment a Node(...) block inside a
        GroupAction(actions=[...]), the next uncommented Node still
        needs to be at the correct indent level relative to the
        list.  Uncomment carefully, or comment the whole item
        including the trailing comma.  This bit James three times
        in a row while trying to strip out ldlidar_node, gps_node,
        and mpu9250driver_node from stingray.launch.py.
  M  ros2_control.xacro plugin resolution (Manual sidebar):
        If launch fails with:
          "class diffdrive_arduino/DiffDriveArduino ... does not
           exist.  Declared types are mock_components/GenericSystem"
        the hardware plugin isn't installed.  Options:
          (a) Install the plugin
          (b) Comment out the <plugin> line to let ros2_control
              fall back to a generic mock (fine for launch-time
              validation without actual hardware)
  F  "Suddenly getting Segmentation fault on ros2 run" doom loop
     (Manual sidebar - this whole thread is a Field Notes gem):
        Symptom: `ros2 run ...` or `ros2 topic ...` prints only
        "[ros2run]: Segmentation fault" with no other output.
        Debug order (per Michael Wimble):
          1. Check for stale ros2 processes on the same machine:
                ps aux | grep ros2
                ps aux | grep node
             Kill any you find that shouldn't still be running.
          2. Bounce the ros2 daemon:
                ros2 daemon stop
                ros2 daemon status
                ros2 daemon start
          3. Confirm ros2 resolves to the base install:
                which ros2   # should be /opt/ros/jazzy/bin/ros2
                ros2 pkg prefix teleop_twist_keyboard
                # should be /opt/ros/jazzy
          4. Move `/opt/ros/jazzy/setup.bash` to the top of your
             overlay stack (i.e. source it AFTER any user overlay).
          5. If still broken, reinstall the desktop:
                sudo apt reinstall ros-jazzy-desktop
                # NOT ros2-desktop-full (wrong package name)
          6. If still broken, purge and reinstall:
                sudo apt remove ~nros-jazzy-* && sudo apt autoremove
                sudo apt update && sudo apt upgrade
                sudo apt install ros-jazzy-desktop
  M  DDS implementation choice (Manual - lesson from Mike):
        The ROS 2 RMW implementation MUST match between machines
        that talk to each other (Stingray and LinuxBox in our case).
        Mixed cyclonertps <-> fastrtps causes weird failures,
        including Segfaults on ros2 run.  Choose ONE and set
        RMW_IMPLEMENTATION consistently in both machines'
        environments (or leave both at default fastrtps).
     ***THIS RESOLVED THE SEGFAULT DOOM on 2025.03.30***: both
     Stormy and LinuxBox were switched from cyclonertps to
     fastrtps and the segfaults stopped.

2025.03.30  (L9324, L9547)  ***DEFINITIVE CPR MEASUREMENT + STABLE DRIVE***
  M  ***DEFINITIVE WHEEL METRICS (Manual-grade, measured
     empirically 2025.03.30):***
        Method: mount motor with encoder; rotate the OUTPUT shaft
        exactly 10 times by hand while Motion Studio counts pulses.
              M1: 31986 pulses / 10 revs = 3198.6 pulses/rev
              M2: 32001 pulses / 10 revs = 3200.1 pulses/rev
              Call it 3200 CPR (counts per revolution at output).
        Wheel radius 0.0615 m -> circumference = 2 * pi * 0.0615
              = 0.3864 m/rev
        Reciprocal = 2.588 rev/m
        2.588 rev/m * 3200 pulses/rev = 8281.57 ~= 8282 pulses/m
     Final yaml values (Manual - current-hardware chapter):
        quad_pulses_per_revolution: 3200.0    (double, not int)
        quad_pulses_per_meter:      8282      (integer)
     Note: the value 8226 in the current Essentials PDF is a slight
     refinement of this same measurement (wheel radius refined
     0.0615 -> 0.0619 based on a more careful circumference
     measurement of 0.389 m).  Both are correct within measurement
     tolerance.  Preserve the DERIVATION in the Manual with the
     current 8226 number, so anyone building a fresh Stingray can
     re-measure and re-derive if their wheels differ.
  M  motor_driver.yaml (STABLE working set, 2025.03.30, Manual
     current-history reference):
        device_name: "/dev/ttyAMA0"      <- Wait: James's UART0
                                            is broken, so this
                                            should be ttyAMA1.
                                            Log has ttyAMA0 here
                                            with baud_rate 38400.
                                            LIKELY A LOG TYPO -
                                            the running system
                                            was actually on ttyAMA1
                                            per Mike's config.
                                            Flag for James to
                                            verify against current
                                            motor_driver.yaml.
        baud_rate: 38400
        device_port: 128
        m1_p: 1.68778   m1_i: 0.29692   m1_qpps: 13500
        m2_p: 1.53399   m2_i: 0.26702   m2_qpps: 15000
        max_angular_velocity: 0.05    (was 0.5, way too fast)
        max_linear_velocity:  0.3     (was 2.0, way too fast)
        quad_pulses_per_meter:      8282
        quad_pulses_per_revolution: 3200.0
        wheel_radius: 0.0615
        wheel_separation: 0.26
  ?  NEW FLAG 10 (probable log typo): the 2025.03.30 yaml snapshot
     shows device_name "/dev/ttyAMA0" but James's Pi has broken
     GPIO14 TX so UART0 cannot work.  Was James on ttyAMA1 at this
     stardate (with the yaml text just wrong in the log), or did

     current motor_driver.yaml on Stormy.
  M  Velocity limits sweet spot (Manual - default caps for teleop
     safety):
        max_angular_velocity: 0.05    rad/s (way slower than "0.5")
        max_linear_velocity:  0.3     m/s
     These are the "just right" values James arrived at after
     bisecting from the too-fast defaults.  Anything higher risks
     losing control; anything much lower and the motors don't
     start moving from stall.

================================================================
CORRECTIONS TO EARLIER CHUNKS (based on chunk 6 findings)
================================================================
The definitive Pi 5 UART configuration on STORMY is NOT UART0 on
GPIO 14/15 as I wrote in chunk 5.  James's specific Pi 5 has a
HARDWARE FAULT: GPIO14 TX is broken.  The actual working config on
Stormy is UART1 on GPIO 0/1 (physical pins 27/28):
        [pi5]
        dtoverlay=uart1-pi5
        # wiring: Pi pin 27 (GPIO 0) -> RC S1
        #         Pi pin 28 (GPIO 1) -> RC S2
        #         Pi pin  6 (GND)    -> RC S1/S2 GND
        # device: /dev/ttyAMA1 at 38400 baud
Michael Wimble's own Pi 5 works with UART0 on GPIO 14/15, and Marco's
does too.  So the Manual for a NEW BUILD should present UART0 as
the default (simpler wiring, standard "TXD/RXD" labels on the
header silkscreen) and UART1 as the fallback for units with a
broken UART0 pin.  Stormy specifically uses UART1.

Chunk 5's headline recommendation stands (Mike's [pi5] section
header + dtoverlay=uart<N>-pi5 pattern + 38400 baud + minicom
loopback test) - only the specific pin numbers change for Stormy's
build.

================================================================
CANDIDATE FIELD-NOTES CLUSTERS growing
================================================================
Pi 5 UART (with the definitive Stormy config now known):
  * [pi5] section header is required (not [all])
  * dtoverlay=uart0-pi5 for UART0 on GPIO 14/15 (pins 8/10)
  * dtoverlay=uart1-pi5 for UART1 on GPIO 0/1  (pins 27/28)
  * Multiple UARTs can be enabled - just add multiple dtoverlay
    lines under [pi5]
  * minicom + jumper loopback is the definitive test - characters
    typed in minicom should echo back if the UART hardware is good
  * INDIVIDUAL Pi 5 units can have broken UART pins; James's does.
    ALWAYS run the loopback test before trusting UART0.
  * USB alternative: ros2_roboclaw_driver on /dev/ttyACM0 (RoboClaw
    over USB CDC virtual comport, ~500 kbps auto-negotiated, no
    baud config needed)

RoboClaw specifics (updated with new symptoms):
  * (existing items)
  * ERR blink 2x = Driver Fault (Chunk 2)
  * ERR blink 3x = Logic Battery High (>6.5 V on 5V rail);
    motors freeze; power-cycle to clear.
  * "Invalid ACK response" is often a wiring or baud mismatch, but
    the PiRewrite branch also fixed some of these via CRC re-work.
  * M1 = LEFT, M2 = RIGHT (Stingray/Stormy convention)

DDS / RMW:
  * (NEW)  Both machines in a multi-machine setup MUST use the
    same RMW implementation.  Mixed cyclonertps <-> fastrtps
    manifests as "[ros2run]: Segmentation fault" on ros2 run.
    Use fastrtps consistently.

SSD / boot:
  * (NEW)  Boot from NVMe SSD: sudo rpi-eeprom-config --edit,
    BOOT_ORDER=0xf461 -> 0xf416.
  * (NEW)  SSD ~19x faster than SD for buffered reads.

Motor / drive convention:
  * (NEW)  M1 = LEFT, M2 = RIGHT (Stingray/Stormy)
  * (NEW)  Safe teleop caps: max_angular_velocity 0.05 rad/s,
    max_linear_velocity 0.3 m/s.  Bisect from too-fast defaults.

The named collaborators list (updated):
  * (existing) Roberto, Michael Wimble, Sergei Grichine, Nathan at
    BasicMicro, Poe/GPT-4o, Claude-3.5-Sonnet, GPT-4o, Grok,
    GitHub Copilot, HBRC ROS Discussion Group / mailing list
  * (NEW)  Marco (HBRC responder, alt Pi 5 UART0 config)

2025.03.31 tail  (L9600-L9963)  YDLIDAR won't enumerate
  M  YDLIDAR-SDK build recipe (historical - LD19 replaced YDLIDAR
     later; keep as appendix):
        cd ~/YDLIDAR_ws/YDLidar-SDK
        mkdir build && cd build
        cmake ..     # ok with deprecation warnings
        make         # lots of unsigned-int warnings; ok
        sudo make install
     ydlidar_ros2_driver build (checkout `humble` branch, then
     `colcon build`).  Then `source install/setup.bash` before
     `ros2 launch`.
  F  YDLIDAR X2 shows up in lsusb as "Silicon Labs CP210x UART
     Bridge" (ID 10c4:ea60) but NO /dev/ttyUSB0 appears on
     Ubuntu 24.04 Desktop.  Root cause: see 2025.04.01 - brltty
     Braille daemon captures CP210x devices.

2025.04.01  (unindexed)  ***SERGEI'S BRLTTY DIAGNOSIS***
  F  ***BRLTTY vs CP210x on Ubuntu 24.04 (Manual sidebar,
     Sergei-diagnosed):***
        Symptom: `lsusb` shows the CP210x adapter, but
                 `ls /dev/ttyUSB*` returns "no such file."
        Cause:   The Braille TTY daemon (brltty, package
                 brltty) grabs USB CDC/serial-class devices
                 before the standard serial driver can create
                 /dev/ttyUSB<N>.
        Fix:     sudo apt remove brltty
                 sudo reboot
        Reference: https://bugs.launchpad.net/ubuntu/+source/
                   brltty/+bug/874181
     Sergei corrected James's typo BRLTTY (Braille TTY) vs
     "britty" - the correct package name has no vowels between
     BRLT and TTY.
  F  Dual SD+SSD boot state can mask other USB problems:
        With SD card AND SSD both connected, James saw:
          - config.txt state ambiguous (which one the Pi actually
            read at boot is unclear without checking)
          - USB serial enumeration was quirky
        Diagnostic:
          - Remove the SD card; boot from SSD only
          - Confirm which "system" (files, packages, network name)
            is now live
        Lesson (Manual sidebar): "Boot media state is a source of
        confusion.  If you have both an SD and an SSD attached,
        remove one and confirm which is live before diagnosing
        anything else."
  M  Boot media selection - the definitive BOOT_ORDER encoding
     (Manual appendix, reverse-engineered from Chunk 6 + this
     entry; digits are read RIGHT to LEFT):
        0xf416 = try NVMe first, then USB, then SD    (SSD-primary)
        0xf461 = try SD first, then USB, then NVMe    (SD-primary)
        0xf146 = try USB first, then NVMe, then SD    (USB-primary)
        Trailing 6 = network boot; usually leave off.
     Change via:
        sudo -E rpi-eeprom-config --edit

2025.04.04  (L9963)  SSD instability
  F  NVMe controller reset messages ("CSTS=0xffffffff, PCI STATUS
     =0x10") during boot from Inland TN446 SSD - later diagnosed
     (2025.04.16) as incompatibility, not corruption.  Ediloca
     EN600 PRO works cleanly on the same hat.  Field Note: "if
     an NVMe SSD is throwing CSTS=0xffffffff on boot, check the
     controller chip against a compatibility list before trying
     to fix software."

2025.04.05-04.07  (L10189-L10290)  SSD boot workflow + udev
  M  Path of least resistance for setting up NVMe SSD boot on Pi 5
     (Manual - use Raspberry Pi OS on SD as the boot-configurator,
     even if you plan to run Ubuntu on the SSD):
        1. Fresh Raspberry Pi OS on the SD card.
        2. Boot from SD, run `sudo raspi-config`, use its
           Advanced Options -> Boot Order to prioritise NVMe.
           (Ubuntu's raspi-config on Pi 5 hides this option, per
            this chunk.)
        3. `sudo rpi-eeprom-update` to update the bootloader.
        4. Use Raspberry Pi Imager to write Ubuntu 24.04 to the
           SSD (via a USB adapter is more reliable than in-Pi).
        5. Boot to SSD; verify with `lsblk` that / is nvme0n1p2.
  M  ***UDEV RULE FOR STABLE LIDAR DEVICE NAME (Manual - live in
     current config):***
        /etc/udev/rules.d/99-robot.rules content:
          ACTION=="add", KERNEL=="ttyUSB*",
            ATTRS{idVendor}=="10c4", ATTRS{idProduct}=="ea60",
            SYMLINK+="ttyUSBLDR"
        Then:
          sudo udevadm control --reload-rules
          sudo reboot  (needed for the rule to actually create
                        the symlink on the current device)
     Result:  /dev/ttyUSBLDR -> ttyUSB0 (persistent name that
     doesn't shift when other USB devices come and go).
     This is the same pattern that will later be used for
     /dev/roboclaw and /dev/ldlidar; keep as the definitive udev
     recipe.
  M  ***BOOTLOADER CONFIG (Manual - definitive current config on
     Stormy):***
        sudo -E rpi-eeprom-config --edit
     Content:
        [all]
        BOOT_UART=1
        POWER_OFF_ON_HALT=0
        BOOT_ORDER=0xf146    (or 0xf416 for SSD-first)
        # PCIE_PROBE=1       (comment out to save boot time when
                              PCIE is not needed for boot)

2025.04.06  (L10189)  ***FIRST WORKING /scan TOPIC***
  N  First successful YDLIDAR scan.  With brltty removed + SD
     removed:
        ros2 launch ydlidar_ros2_driver ydlidar_launch.py
        # ... "Now lidar is scanning..."
        ros2 topic echo /scan
        # ... angle_min: -3.14159..., ranges: [...real distances...]
     "Success!" - first LIDAR ever to speak on this robot.

2025.04.07 (afternoon)  Michael Wimble's schematic critique
  F  Mike's power-design principle (Manual sidebar, from Mike's
     schematic-review email):
        "Keep in mind that if you run everything on one converter,
         when the motors stop, the PI stops as well.  Try it
         sometime - briefly grab the motors to stop them when
         everything is running."
     Root cause: peak motor stall/reversal current sags the shared
     bus below the Pi's operating margin.  Two design responses:
        (a) Separate the Pi's power path from the motors' path
            (this is what the current 52Pi hat does - it takes
            LiPo->12V bus but has its own regulation for the Pi
            rather than sharing the motor bus caps).
        (b) Tight RoboClaw current limits (see below) + shunt
            regulator (Pololu 3779, still installed) + inrush
            protection (STPS10L25D across the fuse).
     Stormy today uses both approaches.  Add this to the Manual's
     Power chapter as the "why" behind the current topology.
  M  Motor continuous-current estimate (Manual - RoboClaw config
     value derivation):
        When the motor datasheet gives only no-load and stall
        current (as Pololu 4752 does: no-load 0.2 A, stall 5.5 A),
        estimate continuous current as:
              I_cont = (I_stall - I_noload) / 2 + I_noload
                     = (5.5 - 0.2) / 2 + 0.2 = 2.85 A
        Set RoboClaw Motion Studio "Max Current" per channel
        slightly above this (2.85 A -> use ~3 A) but well below
        the RoboClaw's 7 A per channel maximum.
        James's original setting was 15 A per channel - WAY too
        permissive.  Corrected here to 3 A.
     Cross-reference: chunk 6 note on the burnout mechanism
     ("permissive current + carpet-hair stall").  THIS is likely
     the era where the current limit was set safely.  The
     2025.02-2025.03 tuning attempts with p=7.26 etc had M1/M2
     Max Current at 15 A, which is the actual mechanism that
     could have burned a winding under a stall.  Flag 1 evidence
     accumulating - though the specific burnout entry is still to
     be found.
  M  Max Regen setting in Motion Studio: at least 1-2 V above the
     maximum battery voltage.  For a 14.8V LiPo (16.8V max fresh),
     keep at -15V.

2025.04.16  (L10540)  ***INLAND SSD INCOMPATIBILITY DISCOVERY***
  M  ***SSD compatibility (Manual - critical BOM item):***
        Pi 5 NVMe SSD compatibility list:
          NOT compatible: SSDs with Phison PS5021-E21 controller
             (e.g., Inland TN446 512 GB).  Confirmed via
             https://wiki.geekworm.com/NVMe_SSD_boot_with_the_
             Raspberry_Pi_5 and by James's failed boots.
             `lspci` on a working Ubuntu boot shows the offending
             controller as "Phison Electronics Corporation
             PS5021-E21 PCIe4 NVMe Controller (DRAM-less)".
          Compatible (Sergei-verified, currently in Stormy):
             Ediloca EN600 PRO M.2 NVMe (Sergei uses this; James
             ordered it 2025.04.16 and it's what's in Stormy today).
             The 2 TB version:
               ediloca.net/products/ediloca-en600-pro-ssd-2tb-
               pcle-3-0x4-nvme-m-2-2280
     Add to Manual BOM sidebar: "Check the SSD controller against
     the Geekworm compatibility list before purchase.  Phison-DRAM-
     less controllers are the primary Pi-5 offender."

2025.04.17-04.20  (L10661-L10689)  Mechanical panel work
  M  Left-rear side plate (Manual - describes current physical
     layout):
        Buck converter (MidZoo 15A) - internal 15A car fuse.
        Shunt regulator (Pololu 3779) mounted here in parallel
          with the bus (matches James's 2026.07.20 topology - so
          the 3779 has been in this location since ~April 2025).
        Power bus.
        E-stop button on right rear panel (relocated for
          ergonomic reach).
     Confirms flag 6 resolution: the Pololu 3779 was installed
     onto the left-rear panel here.  Manual current-hardware
     chapter can cite 2025.04.17-04.20 as install date.
  F  Panel-thickness lessons (Manual sidebar / Field Note):
        Original 1/8" top and bottom plates - insufficient rigidity.
        Original 3/8" side plates - overkill.
        Revised design: 1/4" top and bottom (better strength +
                        room for E-stop), 1/4" (~11/32") side
                        plates.
        Battery tie-down slots: narrower (1" instead of 1.25")
        with a second row to accommodate a sideways-mounted USB
        hub without wrapping around it.
  N  FabLab drilling saga (preserve as anecdote): OAK-D block
     drilling melted plastic on the bit; Roland used inductive
     heater to melt it off.  Small bit caused chuck slip and
     broke 2 chuck teeth.  Getting home, discovered camera-
     support post holes were 4mm - too big for 6-32 tap; correct
     size is 7/64" (2.85mm).  Redesigned camera plate: 1/4"
     acrylic, screws onto upper front plate (no long through-
     holes), clearance holes for 4-40 with nut for easy assembly.
  M  Emergency-stop button source (Manual - alternative to Chunk
     6's Amazon B08D39XWS5, this is the AliExpress source):
        AliExpress push-button switch,
        Latching action, 1 NO + 1 NC, DPST,
        660 V, 10 A, 4 pins,
        Button diameter 40mm / 1.6",
        Mount hole 22mm / 7/8",
        Panel thickness up to 6mm / 15/64",
        Overall 60x70mm, red plastic/metal cap.
     Verify which E-stop (Amazon vs AliExpress) is currently on
     Stormy; both show up in the log.

2025.04.21-04.22  (L10863-L10899)  Ediloca SSD flashing
  F  SSD-flashing recipe that finally worked (Manual sidebar):
        (a) In-Pi Raspberry Pi Imager: unreliable for Pi-hostile
            SSD hats; frequent hangs and mid-write failures.
        (b) Win32 Disk Imager on Windows to make an .img of the
            existing SD; then Balena Etcher to flash that image
            onto the SSD via a USB-to-M.2 adapter:
              Anyoyo EC-6607 NVMe-to-USB adapter (Amazon B0CHNP7P89)
                is what James used.
            Balena Etcher succeeded where in-Pi Imager failed.
        (c) Put the SSD (now flashed) into the Pi's NVMe hat.
            Boot with SD REMOVED.
     Manual entry: "For a stubborn NVMe SSD on Pi 5, flash it on
     a Windows/Mac laptop via a USB-to-M.2 adapter (Anyoyo EC-6607
     or similar) using Balena Etcher.  Do not fight the in-Pi
     Imager path."

2025.04.25-05.02  (L10979-L11234)  C-COMPILER FAILURE + FIX
  F  Symptom: `gcc test.c -o test` fails with
        "collect2: fatal error: ld terminated with signal 4
         [Illegal instruction], core dumped"
     even after `sudo apt install build-essential` reports
     everything up-to-date.
     Diagnosis (from log): almost certainly a corrupted OS image
     (possibly from a bad SSD flash).  The compiler binary itself
     seems fine; the linker crashes on ANY input.
     Fix that finally worked:
        Reflash the SSD from scratch via USB adapter
        (Ubuntu 24.04 Desktop image) -> full basic install ->
        install ros-dev-tools (which pulls gcc as a dependency)
        -> hello_world compiles and runs.
     Field Note: "'ld terminated with signal 4' on ANY input =
     assume image corruption; reflash rather than debug."
  N  HBRC email drafted 2025.05.02: "To further bolster my
     reputation for 'destructive testing of software'..."  Signed
     "Expert in destructive testing.  'Think your code is bullet
     proof?  Wrong!'"  Preserve as Manual origin-chapter humor.

2025.05.17-05.26  (L11456-L11516)  ***YDLIDAR FIRST RViz VISUALIZATION***
  M  YDLIDAR install recipe that finally works end-to-end on
     Stingray (Manual - historical for YDLIDAR era; LD19 later):
        # Prerequisites
        sudo apt update && sudo apt upgrade
        sudo apt install python3 swig python3-pip
        # SDK
        cd ~/YDLIDAR_ws
        git clone https://github.com/YDLIDAR/YDLidar-SDK.git
        cd YDLidar-SDK && mkdir build && cd build
        cmake .. && make && sudo make install
        # ROS 2 driver
        cd ~/YDLIDAR_ws
        git clone https://github.com/YDLIDAR/ydlidar_ros2_driver.git
        cd ydlidar_ros2_driver && git checkout humble
        # KEY FIX: replace the node .cpp with slowrunner's fork
        # to work with X2 on Jazzy:
        cd src
        mv ydlidar_ros2_driver_node.cpp \
           ydlidar_ros2_driver_node.ORIGINAL.cpp
        # download and paste from:
        # raw.githubusercontent.com/slowrunner/ROS2-GoPiGo3/main/
        # ros2ws/src/ydlidar_ros2_driver/src/
        # ydlidar_ros2_driver_node.cpp
        cd ..
        colcon build --symlink-install
        cd params && cp X2.yaml ydlidar.yaml
     Config edits in ydlidar.yaml for Stingray:
        port:            /dev/ttyUSB0   (or /dev/ttyUSBLDR)
        baudrate:        115200
        lidar_type:      1
        device_type:     0
        sample_rate:     3
        fixed_resolution:true
        reversion:       true
        inverted:        true
        support_motor_dtr: false
        angle_min/max:   -180.0 / 180.0
        range_min/max:   0.1 / 12.0
        frequency:       10.0
        isSingleChannel: true
     Launch:
        source ./install/setup.bash
        ros2 launch ydlidar_ros2_driver ydlidar_launch.py
  F  RViz QoS mismatch on /scan:
        Symptom: RViz complains "New publisher discovered on
        topic '/scan', offering incompatible QoS.  No messages
        will be sent to it.  Last incompatible policy:
        RELIABILITY_QOS_POLICY"
        Fix: In RViz LaserScan display, set Reliability Policy
        to "Best Effort" (default is Reliable, but the YDLIDAR
        driver publishes Best Effort).
     Cosmetic follow-up: Fixed Frame = laser_frame; increase
     Size(m) from 0.01 to 0.05 to make dots visible.

2025.05.26-05.27  (L11516-L11927)  BNO055 IMU comes online
  F  flynneva/bno055 driver Python dependency gotcha:
        `sudo pip3 install smbus`  -> "externally-managed-
        environment" error on Ubuntu 24.04 (PEP 668).
        `sudo apt install python3-smbus2` -> also does not
        satisfy the driver's `from smbus import SMBus` import.
        WORKING PACKAGE NAME:
              sudo apt install python3-smbus
        Not smbus2, not pip.  Just python3-smbus.
        Sergei confirmed this as the working recipe in his
        setup guide.
  M  Sergei's BNO055 setup guide (Manual reference):
        https://github.com/slgrobotics/robots_bringup/blob/main/
        Docs/Sensors/BNO055%20IMU.md
     Manual should point at this and other Sergei docs as
     canonical companions rather than re-deriving.
  M  ***BNO055 (I2C) first-time bring-up on Stormy (Manual;
     BNO055 was later swapped for BNO085 but the workspace
     structure and I2C bus 1 remained):***
        mkdir -p ~/robot_ws/src && cd ~/robot_ws/src
        git clone https://github.com/flynneva/bno055.git
        cd ~/robot_ws
        sudo rosdep init && rosdep update
        rosdep install --from-paths src --ignore-src \
              --rosdistro=${ROS_DISTRO} -r -y
        sudo apt install python3-smbus
        colcon build
        source install/setup.bash
        ros2 run bno055 bno055 --ros-args --params-file \
              src/bno055/bno055/params/bno055_params_i2c.yaml
     Confirmed working parameters (from first successful run):
        connection_type:      "i2c"
        i2c_bus:              "1"
        i2c_addr:             "40"       (0x28 or 0x29, decimal
                                          40 = 0x28)
        frame_id:             "bno055"
        data_query_frequency: "100"
        calib_status_frequency: "0.1"
        operation_mode:       "12"       (NDOF)
        placement_axis_remap: "P2"       (BOARD axis remap;
                                          reconfirm for BNO085
                                          later)
        acc_factor: 100.0
        mag_factor: 16000000.0
        gyr_factor: 900.0
        grav_factor: 100.0
     Startup message on first success:
        "Bosch BNO055 IMU configuration complete."
  N  Arduino sanity path before bringing the IMU to the Pi:
        Load Adafruit BNO055 Arduino demo (webserial_3d or
        read_all_data).  Confirm Calibration:Sys/Gyro/Accel/Mag
        counts increment as you rotate the board (target: all 3).
        Cross-reference: this is the same "test on a known-good
        platform first" pattern as the 2025.02.17 Arduino
        RoboClaw test.  Add to Field Notes cluster.
  F  UART option for BNO055 (only if you can't use I2C):
        Connect the 3.3V pin to PS1 to select UART mode.
        Via a CP2104 USB-to-UART Bridge:
              BNO055 Vin -> CP2104 5V
              BNO055 GND -> CP2104 GND
              BNO055 SDA -> CP2104 RXD  (SDA/SCL are re-purposed
              BNO055 SCL -> CP2104 TXD   as UART RX/TX in PS1
                                         mode; swap if needed)
     Historical / appendix only - Stormy uses I2C.
