15. (NEW)  Remote key-fob kill switch (per James 2026.07.20):
    a wireless RF receiver mounted inside the chassis near the
    RoboClaw, paired with a key-fob transmitter.
    >>> WIRING RESOLVED 2026.07.20 by James (Manual-grade):
          RoboClaw S4 POS  -> Kill-switch receiver power (+)
          RoboClaw S4 NEG  -> Kill-switch receiver power (-)
          RoboClaw S5-1    -> Kill-switch YELLOW signal wire
          RoboClaw S5-NEG  -> Kill-switch BLUE signal wire
        S4 supplies the receiver's operating power (from the
        RoboClaw's 5V logic rail).  S5 is the RoboClaw's
        emergency-stop / limit-switch input; the receiver
        drives it to trip motor cutoff when the fob button
        is pressed.
        Sourcing / model of the receiver + fob still to be
        found in the log.  Watch for the entry that documents
        the actual product.

21. (NEW)  BNO085 IMU pinout on Stormy TODAY (Manual-grade,
    per James 2026.07.20):
        BNO085 pin 4 (POS / VIN)  -> Pi 3.3V rail
        BNO085 pin 6 (GND)        -> Pi GND
        BNO085 pin 3 (BLUE)       -> Pi SDA (I2C data)
        BNO085 pin 5 (YELLOW)     -> Pi SCL (I2C clock)
     Color coding matches Adafruit STEMMA-QT convention
     (blue=SDA, yellow=SCL, red=power, black=ground) and the
     BNO085 wiring notes in StingrayStormyEssentials p.11.
     I2C bus 1, address 0x28 (=40 decimal, from the flynneva
     driver params in Chunk 7-8).
     Manual-critical: the driver code uses the BNO055 name
     (`bno055` package) but the physical chip on Stormy today
     is BNO085.  The two share the same I2C interface and
     register map at a basic level, so the flynneva driver
     works for BNO085 with the same params - but a follower
     will be confused by the naming.  Add a Manual sidebar:
     "The chip is BNO085 but the ROS 2 driver package name
     is `bno055`.  This is intentional - Adafruit's BNO085
     is protocol-compatible with the older BNO055 for the
     basic Euler/quaternion pipeline; James did not swap
     drivers when he upgraded from BNO055 to BNO085."

22. (NEW)  Raspberry Pi 5 UNIT REPLACEMENT (per James
    2026.07.20): "There MAY HAVE BEEN a replacement of the
    Raspberry Pi 5 because of all the GPIO uncertainty.  I
    don't recall specifically."  This is the most likely
    explanation for the Chunk 6 vs Chunk 9 UART flip-flop
    (the older Pi genuinely had a broken GPIO14 TX; the
    replacement Pi does not).  If a specific "new Pi arrived
    / swapped in" entry surfaces in later chunks, cite it
    here.  If not, the Manual should say:
        "The Raspberry Pi 5 currently in Stormy is the SECOND
         unit (the first was retired due to intermittent /
         broken GPIO14 TX behavior; replacement date not
         precisely recorded).  Followers building a new
         Stingray should ALWAYS run the minicom loopback
         test on the intended UART BEFORE committing to
         final wiring - individual Pi 5 units can have
         broken UART pins."
    >>> RESOLVED 2026.07.20 by James: "Raspberry Pi 5 ordered
        from Amazon 2025.03.17."  This exactly matches the
        Chunk 6 timeline: on 2025.03.06 Michael Wimble emailed
        the [pi5]/dtoverlay=uart0-pi5 config, on 2025.03.17
        (11 days later) James placed the Amazon order for a
        replacement Pi.  The GPIO14-TX loopback failure at
        Chunk 6 stardate 2025.03.17 morning IS the diagnosis
        that led to the order.  Manual entry (definitive):
          "Stormy's current Raspberry Pi 5 is the SECOND unit
           (ordered from Amazon 2025.03.17, replacing an
           earlier Pi 5 with a broken GPIO14 TX pin diagnosed
           by minicom loopback that same day).  Followers
           should ALWAYS run a jumpered loopback test on the
           intended UART BEFORE committing to final wiring -
           individual Pi 5 units can have broken UART pins."
        Watch for a matching "new Pi arrived" entry in the
        log around 2025.03.20-25 (typical Amazon Prime
        delivery) to give the Manual a precise arrival date.

16. (NEW, per James 2026.07.20)  YDLIDAR -> LD19 swap mechanism
    (Manual - already present as a Field Note primer; watch for
    the specific stardate entry so we can cite it):
        The YDLIDAR X2 has a drive belt spinning the mirror
        assembly.  On Stormy the belt frayed and eventually
        broke.  Original replacement belt was impossible to
        source (OEM part unobtainable).  A best-guess O-ring
        was tried as a substitute; this experience urged the
        pivot to a direct-drive design.  The LD19 (Youyeetoo
        FHL-LD19) replaced YDLIDAR X2 - no belt.
     Manual-level Field Note (first-class):
        "Belt-driven LIDAR is a wear item; when OEM parts are
         unobtainable (typical for hobbyist Chinese-import LIDAR),
         a direct-drive replacement (e.g., LD19) removes a whole
         class of future failure modes."
     Watch for stardate of belt fray -> break -> O-ring attempt
     -> LD19 purchase.  When found, cite here.

CANDIDATE FIELD-NOTES CLUSTERS growing
================================================================
Ubuntu 24.04 on Pi 5 gotchas (dedicated cluster):
  * (existing) [pi5] section header for dtoverlay
  * (existing) UART pin routing quirks + individual-unit hardware
    faults (GPIO14 TX broken on Stormy)
  * (existing) Ediloca > Inland - controller-chip compatibility
  * (NEW) BRLTTY captures USB CDC serial devices; `sudo apt
    remove brltty` if /dev/ttyUSB<N> never appears
  * (NEW) `raspi-config` on Ubuntu Pi 5 HIDES the boot-order menu;
    do the boot-media setup from Raspberry Pi OS instead
  * (NEW) `pip3 install X` fails with "externally-managed-
    environment" - use `apt install python3-X` (PEP 668)
  * (NEW) `ld terminated with signal 4 [Illegal instruction]` on
    ANY gcc input = reflash the OS; do not debug the compiler

Power design (with Michael Wimble's guidance):
  * "One converter for everything" = motor stall sags Pi power ->
    Pi reset.  Test: briefly grab the motors while running.
  * Continuous-current estimate = (Stall - NoLoad)/2 + NoLoad
  * Set RoboClaw Max Current slightly above continuous, NOT at
    the RoboClaw's 7A per channel max.  15A was catastrophically
    permissive.
  * Max Regen = max battery voltage + 1-2V

Debugging discipline:
  * (existing) Test the tester; Arduino sanity check; walk away
    from AI loops; image before experimenting
  * (NEW)  Reflash-then-diagnose is faster than debug-then-fix
    when the failure implicates fundamental tools (compiler,
    linker, kernel).

The named collaborators list (updated):
  * (existing) Roberto, Michael Wimble, Sergei Grichine, Nathan at
    BasicMicro, Marco, HBRC list, GitHub Copilot, various AIs
  * (implicit) slowrunner (ROS2-GoPiGo3 fork of the YDLIDAR node
    that was the last-mile fix)
  * Roland Fields, Houston City College (formerly Houston
    Community College) - laser cutting, 3D printing, and CNC
    machining, particularly edge-drilling of acrylic side panels.
    The inductive-heater save (removing melted acrylic from the
    drill bit) is his craft.  A recurring and generous helper -
    Manual origin chapter should name him fully.

Component sourcing policy (Manual - James's stated preference,
2026.07.20):
  Regardless of where a part was ORIGINALLY sourced (AliExpress,
  eBay, hobbyist supplier, gift from friend, etc.), the Manual
  should list a US-accessible source that followers can actually
  order from: Amazon, DigiKey, Adafruit, Pololu, SparkFun, etc.
  Original-source URLs go in a footnote for provenance.  This
  applies to every BOM entry.

          calib_status_frequency: 0.1

2025.05.31  (L12356)  Powered USB hub charging-port trap
  N  Backup image saved: BasicAbovePlusBNO055.img (Manual
     discipline in action - continues the "snapshot before
     experimenting" rule).
  F  Powered-USB-hub power distribution trap (Manual sidebar -
     this was an early workaround; later SUPERSEDED by the 52Pi
     PD hat, but the observation stands for anyone using a
     powered USB hub):
        Symptom: Pi 5 partially boots, then reboots repeatedly.
        Cause: The powered USB hub's dedicated "CHARGING" port
                often has a lower current ceiling or different
                power path than the regular data ports.  Feeding
                the Pi 5's USB-C from the charging port produced
                brownouts.
        Fix: Feed the Pi 5 from a regular USB data port on the
                hub; move keyboard/mouse to the powered hub
                (unpowered devices are fine).
     Note: James's log annotation "WRONG: SEE p564" indicates the
     lesson evolved later.  Current answer: 52Pi PD hat is the
     proper solution (Handoff Note power topology); the powered
     USB hub is no longer in Stormy's current path.  Preserve
     this Field Note as history + a warning for anyone building
     with a powered USB hub.
  F  fastrtps port-init errors on launch startup:
        [RTPS_TRANSPORT_SHM Error] Failed init_port
          fastrtps_port7000: open_and_lock_file failed
     Root cause: mismatched rmw_fastrtps_shared_cpp versions
     between packages (local=8.4.1 vs latest=8.4.2 in the log).
     Fix: `sudo apt update && sudo apt upgrade` to sync package
     versions.  Do NOT `apt dist-upgrade` casually (see
     superuser.com question 1554163 for the caveat).

2025.06.01  (L12483)  Copilot fixes duplicate-node launch error
  F  "ExecuteLocal action 'X-N': executed more than once"
     (Manual sidebar):
        Symptom: launch dies with the above when it tries to
        start the same Node object twice.
        Cause: a Node was included both in the top-level
        LaunchDescription's list AND inside a GroupAction /
        IncludeLaunchDescription that is also in that list.
        Fix: only include the node ONCE per launch tree.  When
        modularising with GroupAction, remove the top-level
        duplicate.
     Copilot in VS Code diagnosed this directly from the file -
     good example for the Manual origin chapter of "AI as a
     coding partner, not a chatbot."

     the workflow to use if the SSD dies tomorrow):
        1. Raspberry Pi Imager (via USB-M.2 adapter) -> Ubuntu
           24.04 Desktop -> Ediloca EN600 PRO SSD.
        2. Boot Pi from SSD, run Basic Install:
              ssh, Chromium, Terminator, hwinfo, SystemConfig.sh
        3. Install ROS 2 Jazzy per Sergei's docs.

                [pi5]
                dtoverlay=uart1-pi5     # Stormy's UART (see
                                        # Chunk 6 for why UART1
                                        # not UART0)
              sudo adduser ubuntu dialout
              sudo chmod a+rw /dev/ttyAMA1
        5. Add udev rule for LIDAR symlink (see Chunk 7).
        6. Clone workspaces into ~/ros_ws/src/:
              articubot_one (slgrobotics dev branch)
              bno055 (flynneva)
              ydlidar_ros2_driver + YDLidar-SDK
              (or ldlidar_ros2 later - see below)
        7. Build in order:
              cd ~/ros_ws && colcon build
        8. Source in ~/.bashrc:
              source /opt/ros/jazzy/setup.bash
              source ~/ros_ws/install/setup.bash
        9. Verify: talker/listener + LIDAR scan + IMU calib.

          (see the Chunk 7 udev rule; the new udev rule needs the

              udevadm info /dev/ttyUSB0 | grep "ID_PATH="
              # -> use that ID_PATH in the SYMLINK rule

          or namespace ... Invalid node name: node name must not

        Fix: name='ydlidar_ros2_driver' (alphanumerics + _ only)

2025.07.01  (L13758)  ***JAZZY STATICALLY-TYPED PARAMETER FIX***
  F  ***ROS 2 Jazzy statically-typed parameter rule (Manual
     sidebar - hits every driver ported from Humble/Foxy):***
        Symptom (from ydlidar_ros2_driver_node.cpp):
          terminate called after throwing an instance of
          'rclcpp::exceptions::
           UninitializedStaticallyTypedParameterException'
             what():  Statically typed parameter 'port' must be
                      initialized.
        Cause: Jazzy is stricter than earlier distros.  A
        declared parameter without a default value must be
        supplied by the caller (yaml or CLI); otherwise the
        node dies at parameter load time.
        Two-line fix pattern in the .cpp source:
          - std::string frame_id = "laser_frame";
          - node->declare_parameter<std::string>("frame_id");
          - node->get_parameter("frame_id", frame_id);
          + node->declare_parameter<std::string>(
              "frame_id", "laser_frame");
        Or in the yaml, provide the value with correct type
        (a quoted string for strings).
     James walked every YDLIDAR node parameter through this fix
     (port, frame_id, lidar_type, device_type, sample_rate, and
     more).  Manual: this pattern applies to ANY ROS 2 Jazzy
     port from an older distro.
  F  Quoted strings in yaml matter for typed parameters:
        frame_id: laser_frame     # sometimes fails on Jazzy
        frame_id: "laser_frame"   # explicit string type, works
     Cross-reference: also applies to `ignore_array: ""` for
     YDLIDAR (empty string) - the .cpp needed a default of "".

          ydlidar_minimal_launch_file.launch.py

          from launch import LaunchDescription
          from launch_ros.actions import Node
          def generate_launch_description():
              ydlidar_node = Node(
                package='ydlidar_ros2_driver',
                executable='ydlidar_ros2_driver_node',
                name='ydlidar_ros2_driver',
                output='screen',
                parameters=['/home/ubuntu/ros_ws/src/
                    ydlidar_ros2_driver/params/ydlidar.yaml'],
                arguments=['--ros-args','--log-level','debug'])
              return LaunchDescription([ydlidar_node])

     Fix in JP_lidar.xacro by rotating the lidar's parent-link

ROS 2 Jazzy porting from older distros:
  * (NEW) Statically typed parameters MUST have a default value
    or be supplied by caller; provide defaults in the .cpp
    declare_parameter<T>(name, default) form.
  * (NEW) YAML string values often need explicit quotes on Jazzy
    to be type-inferred correctly.
  * (NEW) Duplicate Node inclusion in LaunchDescription =
    "executed more than once"; remove the top-level entry if
    also inside a GroupAction.

Workspace management:
  * (NEW) Stormy Pi 5 uses ~/ros_ws/; LinuxBox uses ~/robot_ws/.
    Do not confuse.
  * (NEW) ~/stingray_ws on LinuxBox is deprecated.
  * (NEW) URDF include list for Stingray is minimal - no camera,
    no GPS, no MPU9250 magnetometer; only OAK-D via oakd.xacro
    (later chunks), LD LiDAR (later), BNO085 IMU (later).

Boot-time robot bring-up:
  * (NEW)  bootup_launch.sh pattern under ~/launch/, invoked
    manually or by systemd/cron/user-login as needed.  Includes
    a build step (colcon build) so any recent edits take
    effect on the next launch.  Optional audio cue with aplay.

Power-hub gotchas:
  * (NEW) Powered USB hub charging port != regular data port for
    Pi 5 power.  (Historical - Stormy now uses 52Pi PD hat.)

Community/collab wisdom:
  * (NEW) File a helpful bug report on OSS tools you use; the
    author often fixes it within a day (saiaravind19 example).

The named collaborators list (updated):
  * (existing) Roberto, Michael Wimble, Sergei Grichine, Nathan at
    BasicMicro, Marco, HBRC list, GitHub Copilot, various AIs,
    slowrunner, Roland Fields
  * (NEW) saiaravind19 (Gazebo Terrain Generator - responsive
    OSS maintainer)
  * (NEW) lghrainbow (ydlidar_ros2_driver fork now used on
    Stormy, replacing slowrunner's)

2025.07.11 tail - 2025.07.12  (L14500)  RDP saga wrap-up + reset
  F  ***"GPT-4o's knowledge base is useless!  He's FIRED!" ***
     Recurring pattern (also 2025.02.10, 2025.02.24): when an AI
     session goes into a fix-things-then-break-more-things loop,
     the recovery step is to REVERT every configuration file it
     touched (from your .bak backups) and start fresh with a
     different assistant.  In this case: `sudo mv
     /etc/xrdp/startwm.sh.bak /etc/xrdp/startwm.sh`, un-comment
     `WaylandEnable=false` in /etc/gdm3/custom.conf, delete
     ~/.xsession, restart xrdp.  Field Notes discipline: keep
     .bak files of every system file an AI recommends touching.
  M  GNOME Desktop Sharing (Settings/System/Desktop Sharing)
     used with RDC on Windows (Manual sidebar for anyone wanting
     Remote Desktop into Stormy):
        - Enable password login; set a stable password (the auto-
          generated one changes on reboot).
        - Port 3390 was used (system had shifted from 3389).
        - RDC on Windows: Computer = Stingray:3390 (or
          192.168.68.66:3390), Show Options -> Username = ubuntu.
        - The Ubuntu account password is what RDP prompts for.
        - Practical annoyance: after each reboot, must manually
          re-enable Desktop Sharing and re-set password.
     Manual: this is workable but painful.  Later options (xrdp
     via LOG-OUT-then-login, NoMachine, Foxglove Studio, plain
     SSH+X) may serve better; see later chunks.

     to UART1 on GPIO 0/1.  Reconciliation possibilities:
        (a) Between 2025.03.17 and 2025.07.12, James retested and
            GPIO14 TX became functional (intermittent hardware
            fault?  Prior loopback misdiagnosis?).
        (b) The 2025.07.12 config enabled BOTH uart0 and uart1,
            so /dev/ttyAMA0 gets created for UART0 but the
            RoboClaw might have been wired to UART1 pins and
            still open on /dev/ttyAMA0 by mistake (unlikely - it
            would not have responded).
        (c) The specific Pi 5 was replaced between chunks.
     Please clarify at your convenience: which UART do the
     RoboClaw wires physically go to on Stormy today, and does
     the working GPIO14 TX support ruling out the earlier
     "broken pin" finding?
     Manual will present whichever is authoritative; for now,
     I'll note both possibilities.
     Flag 8 REVISED again: current Stormy config appears to use
     BOTH uart0 and uart1 in [pi5], with the driver's
     device_name = "/dev/ttyAMA0".  UART1 may be a spare.
     Flag 10 likely RESOLVED: the earlier "log typo" hypothesis
     is unnecessary if ttyAMA0 is actually the current working
     device.
  M  ros2_roboclaw_driver install + config recipe (Manual - the

  F  Two-computer workspace sync gotchas (Manual sidebar):
        Stormy Pi 5:      ~/ros_ws/src/articubot_one/...
        LinuxBox:         ~/articubot_one/            (old)

     (~/ros_ws/src/articubot_one) so xacro `find` and relative
     `../../../description/X.xacro` includes resolve identically.

        # rebuild: cd ~/ros_ws && rm -rf build install log

            stingray/description/battery.xacro"/>

            common_properties.xacro"/>

2025.07.21-07.22  (L15446-L15561)  ***THE FLAT-WHEEL FIX + PLUGIN FIGHT***
  F  xacro "link 'X' is not unique" error (Manual sidebar):
        Symptom:
          Error: link 'camera_link' is not unique
        Cause: same xacro is included twice (either directly OR
          once directly and once transitively through another
          include).  Often caused by "VSCode being TOO smart"
          when auto-completing an include line.
        Fix: `grep -rn 'camera_link'` (or the offending link name)
          to find both include sites; remove one.
  M  ***MOTOR_DRIVER.CPP FIX FOR FLAT WHEELS IN RVIZ (Manual -
     ros2_roboclaw_driver source patch, Michael Wimble diagnosed
     via Zoom):***
        Symptom: Stingray wheels appear flat white in Rviz;
                 robot state publisher doesn't animate them
                 when the robot moves.
        Root cause: motor_driver.cpp publishes JointState.name
                    entries "left_wheel_link" and
                    "right_wheel_link" but the URDF joint
                    names are "left_wheel_joint" and
                    "right_wheel_joint."  JointState message
                    is silently ignored for the wheels because
                    no joint matches the names.
        Fix (edit ros2_roboclaw_driver/src/motor_driver.cpp):
              joint_state_msg.name.push_back("left_wheel_link");
          ->  joint_state_msg.name.push_back("left_wheel_joint");
              joint_state_msg.name.push_back("right_wheel_link");
          ->  joint_state_msg.name.push_back("right_wheel_joint");
          colcon build --symlink-install
     After the fix, wheels animate in Rviz.
     Manual: this is a required patch on top of Wimble's driver
     for use with the Stingray URDF.  If James upstreams this,
     the patch can be dropped.
  F  Diagnosing TF trees with view_frames (Manual sidebar):
        alias frames='ros2 run tf2_tools view_frames'
        frames                        # generates
                                      # frames_YYYY-MM-DD_HH.MM.SS.pdf
                                      # in the current directory
        evince frames_*.pdf
     Look at the tree.  Every URDF link should appear.  Missing
     wheels = joint state not being published for those wheels
     (see fix above).  Wrong parent link = xacro joint parent/
     child mis-specified.
  M  Lidar_support parent-link correction (Manual - was a bug):
        In lidar_support.xacro, the parent of lidar_support_link
        was base_link; should be chassis_link.  Fix in xacro
        `<parent link=...>` value.

        # comment these out if you have no prior map:
        # map_file_name: stingray_map_serial
        # map_start_pose: [0.0, 0.0, 0.0]
        # map_start_at_dock: true
        # SLAM Toolbox will complain
        # ("Failed to open requested file: stingray_map_serial")
        # but then continues into pure Localization/SLAM mode -
        # this is expected before you have a saved map.
     Manual: this error message is NORMAL on a first SLAM run;
     it becomes an actual error only once you've saved a map
     and it can't be re-loaded.
  F  Node deduplication in multi-launch sessions:
        Symptom: `ros2 node list` shows same node twice
                 (e.g., /slam_toolbox appears twice).
        Cause: the same node was started by two different
                 launch invocations (either two `ros2 launch`
                 in different terminals, or an outer launch
                 that already included the same inner launch).
        Fix: kill all launch terminals, then start ONE
                 top-level launch that includes everything you
                 need.  ros2 daemon stop/start also helps clear
                 stale registrations.

2025.07.29-08.03  (L16427-L16694)  Nav2, controllers, plugin saga
  F  Nav2 inflation_radius vs robot circumscribed radius (Manual
     sidebar, worked example for Stormy):
        Symptom (Nav2):
          [ERROR][computeCircumscribedCost]: The inflation radius
          (0.331) is smaller than the circumscribed radius
          (0.344142).  If this is an SE2-collision checking
          plugin, it cannot use costmap potential field to speed
          up collision checking ...
        Cause: nav2_params.yaml has inflation_radius smaller
          than the maximum footprint radius derived from the
          URDF `base_footprint` link box.
        Derivation for Stormy:
          chassis_length = 0.266 m
          caster_radius  = 0.028 m
          wheel_separation = 0.26 m
          wheel_thickness  = 0.02 m
          base_footprint length = 0.266 + 2*0.028 = 0.322 m
          base_footprint width  = 0.26  + 0.02    = 0.28  m
          diagonal = sqrt(0.322^2 + 0.28^2) ~= 0.4267 m
          -> circumscribed radius ~= 0.2134 m
        So a nav2_params.yaml value like:
          inflation_radius: 0.331   # comfortably > 0.2134
        is CORRECT (safe margin); the earlier error about
        "0.331 < circumscribed 0.344142" implies Nav2 saw a
        larger footprint than James derived.  Verify the
        `footprint` list in nav2_params.yaml matches the URDF
        base_footprint box (or reduce inflation_radius knowingly).
     Rule of thumb (Manual): inflation_radius should be at LEAST
     the robot's circumscribed radius, ideally 2x that value.
  F  ***"diffdrive_arduino/DiffDriveArduino" leftover plugin
     (Manual sidebar - Sergei diagnosed this):***
        Symptom (ros2_control):
          Caught exception ... LibraryLoadException ... class
          diffdrive_arduino/DiffDriveArduino ... does not exist.
          Declared types are mock_components/GenericSystem.
        Cause: the ros2_control.xacro was inherited from the
        Dragger template, which uses an Arduino-based diff-drive
        hardware plugin.  Stormy uses RoboClaw hardware directly
        via ros2_roboclaw_driver - which is a NODE, not a
        ros2_control hardware plugin.  The two architectures do
        not compose; you either use ros2_control with a suitable
        hardware plugin, OR you use a node-based driver.
        Fix on Stormy (RoboClaw path):
              In ros2_control.xacro, COMMENT OUT the whole
              non-sim hardware clause so the real-hardware
              controller_manager is NEVER instantiated.  Keep
              only the sim clause:
                    <hardware>
                      <xacro:if value="$(arg sim_mode)">
                        <plugin>gz_ros2_control/
                          GazeboSimSystem</plugin>
                      </xacro:if>
                    </hardware>
              (per Sergei 2025.08.03)
              AND remove controller_manager, joint_broadcaster,
              diff_drive_controller spawners from
              stingray.launch.py for real-hardware launches.
              Sim launches keep them (Gazebo needs them).
     Sergei's architectural summary (worth quoting verbatim in
     Manual):
       "Once you committed to RoboClaw hardware, whole controller
        manager became obsolete.  It is a different architecture.
        So, delete all mentioning of controller_manager,
        broadcasters, sonars from your launch file.  Make sure
        you include appropriate RoboClaw launch file in it."
  F  Copilot-in-VSCode as a plausible-but-wrong debugger:
        Copilot suggested changing ydlidar node config, adding
        try/except around the roboclaw launch, and increasing
        spawner delays.  Its diagnosis was PLAUSIBLE but WRONG -
        the actual issue was the leftover diffdrive_arduino
        plugin in ros2_control.xacro.  Sergei fixed it with one
        email.
        Field Note (Manual origin chapter): "AI code assistants
        will produce plausible fixes for the wrong root cause.
        When a human expert (Sergei) is available, ping them
        FIRST when you're stuck on architecture; save the AI
        for local mechanical fixes."
        Recovery discipline: rename Copilot-modified files with
        a "CoPilot." prefix so the originals can be restored.
  F  YDLIDAR baseplate + Check Sum errors (Manual sidebar):
        Both messages come from YDLidar-SDK internals
        (src/CYdLidar.cpp and src/YDlidarDriver.cpp respectively):
          error("Fail to get baseplate device information!")
          error("Check Sum 0x%04X != 0x%04X", ...)
        They log at ERROR level but do NOT prevent scanning.
        Field Note: "SDK checksum warnings on YDLIDAR X2 are
        cosmetic; ignore unless /scan actually stops publishing."
        (These errors go away entirely with LD19, a later chapter.)

8.  RE-REVISED 2026.07.20.  Chunk 9 evidence contradicts the
    Chunk 6 "GPIO14 TX broken" diagnosis.  As of 2025.07.12,
    Stormy runs the RoboClaw over /dev/ttyAMA0 (which is UART0
    on GPIO14/15), with BOTH uart0 and uart1 enabled in
    config.txt.  Please clarify:
      (a) Which UART do the RoboClaw signal wires physically
          go to on Stormy today?  (GPIO14/15 pins 8/10, OR
          GPIO0/1 pins 27/28, OR something else?)
      (b) If GPIO14 TX now works, was the March 2025 "broken
          pin" loopback misdiagnosed, or has the hardware
          state changed?
    Manual needs the correct answer to instruct followers.
    >>> RESOLVED (with a caveat) 2026.07.20 by James.  Current-
        state Stormy wiring (Manual-grade):
              Pi pin 8  (GPIO 14 TXD0) -> RoboClaw S1 signal
              Pi pin 10 (GPIO 15 RXD0) -> RoboClaw S2 signal
                                          [see typo note below]
              Pi pin 9  (GND)          -> RoboClaw S1/S2 GND
              (do NOT connect the 5V BEC)
        In /boot/firmware/config.txt under [pi5]:
              dtoverlay=uart0-pi5
              (uart1-pi5 may also be present as a spare;
               harmless if the pins are not wired to anything)
        Driver device_name: /dev/ttyAMA0, baud 38400.
        Likely explanation for the flip-flop:  "There MAY HAVE
        BEEN a replacement of the Raspberry Pi 5 because of all
        the GPIO uncertainty."  If so, the March 2025 loopback
        finding that GPIO14 TX was broken WAS correct for that
        older physical Pi, and the current Pi is a different
        unit with working UART0.  Preserve the Chunk 6 saga in
        the Manual origin chapter as history + the reason the
        UART1/GPIO0-1 backup path exists as a documented
        fallback.
        SMALL TYPO NOTE (please confirm): James wrote "15 RXD/
        pin 19" but on the Pi 40-pin header GPIO 15 (RXD0) is
        physically pin 10 (pin 19 is GPIO 10 / SPI0 MOSI).
        I'm recording pin 10; correct me if the wire actually
        goes to a different pin than the standard UART RX.

    "device_name /dev/ttyAMA0" log entry from 2025.03.30 was
    NOT a typo.  UART0 was in use (whether physically wired or
    intended).

CANDIDATE FIELD-NOTES CLUSTERS growing
================================================================
Architecture (Sergei's summary - Manual keystone):
  * ros2_control (controller_manager + hardware plugin) AND
    node-based drivers (ros2_roboclaw_driver) are ALTERNATIVES,
    not partners.  Stormy uses the node-based path for real
    hardware.  Sim uses gz_ros2_control/GazeboSimSystem.  Do not
    keep leftover diffdrive_arduino/DiffDriveArduino hardware
    plugin blocks in ros2_control.xacro on the real-hardware
    launch.

Rviz debugging:
  * (NEW) Flat/white wheels in Rviz on a real robot = JointState
    name mismatch between the driver's `name` field and the
    URDF joint names.  Diagnose with `frames` (tf2_tools
    view_frames PDF).  Wheels missing from tree = joint state
    not being published for them.
  * (NEW) `link 'X' is not unique` xacro error = duplicate
    include (often VSCode auto-completion insertion).  grep
    the link name to find both include sites.

Nav2 sizing:
  * (NEW) inflation_radius >= robot circumscribed radius,
    ideally 2x.  Stormy's circumscribed radius is ~0.2134 m
    from the base_footprint box; inflation_radius ~0.331 m is a
    safe default.

Two-computer workflow:
  * (NEW) Same workspace path on both machines (~/ros_ws/src/
    articubot_one) so xacro finds resolve identically.
  * (NEW) scp'd BUILT trees do not work; scp source only, then
    `rm -rf` any accidentally-copied build/install/log and
    rebuild fresh on the target machine.

Remote access alternatives (Manual):
  * (NEW) GNOME Desktop Sharing + RDC works but requires
    manual re-enable per reboot; port shifted to 3390.
  * (NEW) xrdp works if you LOG OUT of the local desktop
    before connecting remotely (a running local session
    blocks xrdp).
  * (NEW) NoMachine: RPM install via `alien -d --scripts`
    then dpkg -i; runs on port 4000.
  * (NEW) Foxglove Studio + foxglove-bridge + rosbridge-suite
    (NOT rosbridge_server) provides browser-based topic
    visualisation.

Xbox joystick:
  * (NEW) 'joy_vel': 'cmd_vel' (not cmd_vel_joy) for direct-to-
    motor path; adjust to twist_mux config if you route through
    joystick priority.
  * (NEW) 'publish_stamped_twist': 'false' -
    ros2_roboclaw_driver expects Twist, not TwistStamped.

The named collaborators list (updated):
  * (existing) Roberto, Michael Wimble, Sergei Grichine, Nathan,
    Marco, HBRC, Roland Fields, slowrunner, saiaravind19,
    lghrainbow, GitHub Copilot, various AIs
  * (updated) Michael Wimble in Chunk 9 provided the flat-white-
    wheels JointState fix via Zoom - manual should credit.
  * (updated) Sergei in Chunk 9 provided the controller_manager
    architectural clarification via email - manual should
    quote him.

2025.08.04 tail  (L17000)  plugin experiments continue
  C  More diffdrive/DiffDrive vs diff_drive_controller/
     DiffDriveController vs diffdrive_arduino/DiffDriveArduino
     plugin experiments.  All fail because the fundamental
     approach was wrong (see Chunk 9 Sergei diagnosis, later
     confirmed by Michael Wimble's essay 2025.08.05).  Cut
     details; keep only the final answer in the Manual.

2025.08.05  (L17311)  ***MICHAEL WIMBLE'S ARCHITECTURAL MEGA-ESSAY***
  N  Michael Wimble's HBRC email reply is a Manual keystone.
     Verbatim excerpts to preserve in the origin chapter
     (heavily condensed here for the working notes; full
     text lives at line ~17310):
        "First, it's really jarring to see the term
         'odom_localizer'.  There isn't any localization
         occurring here, nor with 'tf_localizer'."
        "odom topic is NOT odom transform.  Get this tattooed
         on the top of your left hand.  The odom topic is a
         stream of robot position and velocity information.
         The odom transform tells where the robot is relative
         to the magic 'odom stick'."
        "The EKF filter [is] intended to take various
         odometry-related inputs, like IMUs and wheel odometry,
         and try to suss a greater truth from the various
         liars, and produce a better odom-equivalent topic.
         Everyone complains that the ekf filter was put into
         the robot_localization package when, in fact, it's
         not doing localization.  Only AMCL (part of the nav2
         stack) or Slam Toolbox is doing localization."
        "When the robot starts up, a flag is planted in the
         world.  That's right, a pointy stick is poked into
         the gray background you see in rviz2, which certain
         people we know keep thinking is somehow the floor or
         ground.  And that stick never moves.  When the robot
         does actually move, however, Uncle Ernie tells a
         great big fib about his amazing journeys and the
         result of that fib is the odom topic and transform."
        "Localizations, provided by AMCL or Slam Toolbox,
         takes the LIDAR and sextant readings and whispers
         to the nav system that Uncle Ernie's stories are a
         bit of bull shit and if you nudge his story about
         where the robot is by this bit, you can find a
         better lie about where the robot is located on the
         map.  Or, in boring terms, localization provides a
         tf (transform correction) from the map to odom."
     Whole essay is a Manual keystone.  Format it as the
     origin chapter's "How the ROS 2 mapping stack actually
     works, in bar-room vernacular" appendix.
  M  Mike's specific action items for Stormy (Manual-grade):
        (a) Get rid of tf_localizer (line 72 of the launch
            file at that stardate; also the commented-out
            usage at line 221).  Reason: odom is never a
            static transform from odom to base_link.
        (b) Get rid of joint_broad_spawner,
            diff_drive_spawner, and their delayed versions
            (was on lines 101, 107, 113, 120 and in the
            actions list at 204-206).  Reason: those are
            for the ros2_control path, which Stormy does
            NOT use (Stormy uses ros2_roboclaw_driver
            directly - see Chunk 9's Sergei diagnosis).
        (c) DO launch the joint_state_publisher:
                joint_state_publisher_node = Node(
                    package="joint_state_publisher",
                    executable="joint_state_publisher",
                    name="joint_state_publisher")
            and add it to the launched list.
        (d) map_server is only needed if you're LOADING a
            saved map.  During pure SLAM (building the map),
            skip map_server.
        (e) The many delay decorators (delayed_X) may be
            unnecessary; they don't hurt but slow launch.
     "Personally, I use the nav2 stack rather than
      slam_toolbox.  It's kind of a Boston vs California/Bay
      Area thing, but I've lived in Boston and won't go back."
     (Mike's stack preference; Stormy uses slam_toolbox per
     the existing config.)

  M  ***DEFINITIVE ros2_control.xacro for Stormy (Manual-
     grade, current-hardware chapter):***
        <?xml version="1.0"?>
        <robot xmlns:xacro="http://www.ros.org/wiki/xacro">
          <ros2_control name="stingrayRobot" type="system">
            <hardware>
              <xacro:if value="$(arg sim_mode)">

            </hardware>

              <command_interface name="velocity"/>
              <state_interface name="velocity"/>
              <state_interface name="position"/>
            </joint>
            <joint name="right_wheel_joint">
              <command_interface name="velocity"/>
              <state_interface name="velocity"/>

     was leftover from the Dragger template and CAUSED THE
     LibraryLoadException that ate a week of debugging.

     block up-front when moving to a RoboClaw-driven robot.

2025.08.16  (L18463)  ***THE YAML-PARAMETER-PASSING BUG***
  M  ***CRITICAL LAUNCH PATTERN LESSON (Manual sidebar - GPT-5
     diagnosed, dedicated Field Note):***
        Symptom: Running the standalone
                 `ros2 launch ros2_roboclaw_driver
                 ros2_roboclaw_driver.launch.py`
                 works perfectly - RoboClaw talks, motors move.
                 But invoking the same node from within
                 stingray.launch.py by passing the SAME yaml
                 file as `parameters=[yaml_path]` on a Node()
                 object FAILS with:
                   [RoboClaw::openPort] Unable to open USB
                   port: roboclaw, errno: (2) No such file
        Root cause:
                 The standalone launch file OPENS AND PARSES
                 the yaml, extracting the nested
                 `motor_driver_node -> ros__parameters` block:
                   configParams = yaml.safe_load(f)
                     ['motor_driver_node']['ros__parameters']
                 and passes the FLAT dict of parameters to
                 Node(parameters=[configParams]).
                 If you instead pass the raw file:
                   Node(parameters=['/path/to/motor_driver.yaml'])
                 ROS 2 treats keys as
                 `motor_driver_node.ros__parameters.device_name`
                 rather than `device_name` - the driver never
                 sees a device_name and falls back to the
                 default value ("roboclaw"), which fails to
                 open.
        Two acceptable fixes:
                 (A) IncludeLaunchDescription(
                       PythonLaunchDescriptionSource(
                         .../ros2_roboclaw_driver.launch.py))
                     - the recommended path.  Reuses the
                     driver's YAML parsing.
                 (B) Parse the yaml yourself in the parent
                     launch:
                       import yaml
                       with open(path,'r') as f:
                           params = yaml.safe_load(f)[
                             'motor_driver_node'
                           ]['ros__parameters']
                       Node(..., parameters=[params], ...)
     Rule of thumb (Manual): "ROS 2 params yaml files use a
     nested naming scheme (`node_name.ros__parameters.<param>`).
     When passing yaml to Node(parameters=[...]), the yaml
     must be either (a) properly-parsed to a flat dict OR
     (b) written flat at the top level.  If your driver uses
     the nested form (most do), use IncludeLaunchDescription
     to reuse the driver's own launch file rather than
     duplicating the Node definition."
  F  When you use IncludeLaunchDescription for a driver AND
     ALSO have a Node() for the same driver in the parent
     launch file, you get DUPLICATE nodes:
        WARNING: Be aware that there are nodes in the graph
        that share an exact name, which can have unintended
        side effects.
        /ros2_roboclaw_driver_node
        /ros2_roboclaw_driver_node
     Fix: pick one, delete the other.  If both are present
     and one has `respawn=True`, you can get an escalating
     process pile-up.  Field Note: `ros2 node list` should
     never show duplicate names in normal operation - treat
     any duplicate as a launch-tree bug.

2025.08.16 tail  Git divergence saga (Manual sidebar - short)
  F  Divergent-branches git recovery recipe (Manual - James
     hit this trying to push HBRC20250813):
        Symptom: `git push` fails: "Updates were rejected
                 because the remote contains work that you do
                 not have locally."
                 `git pull` fails: "You have divergent
                 branches and need to specify how to reconcile
                 them."
        Fix (one-time config):
              git config pull.rebase false    # use merge
              git pull                        # accepts merge
              git push
     Manual: pick a strategy up-front (merge or rebase); the
     tri-state ("neither") is a Ubuntu 24.04 git 2.43 default
     that halts you until you decide.

2025.08.17  (L19079)  ***NAV2 UP THROUGH DWB CRITICS FIX***
  M  Nav2 launch, definitive form (Manual - the exact
     invocation that finally started Nav2 without the
     "Couldn't load critics" error):
        cd ~/ros_ws/src/articubot_one/config
        ros2 launch articubot_one navigation.launch.py \
          params_file:=/home/ubuntu/ros_ws/src/articubot_one/\
          robots/stingray/config/nav2_params.yaml
     Or set the DEFAULT in navigation.launch.py:
        default_value='/home/ubuntu/ros_ws/src/articubot_one/
          robots/stingray/config/nav2_params.yaml'
     Reason: the default was `map_server_params.yaml` (which
     has no DWB critics defined) so the controller_server
     lifecycle transition failed with:
        Couldn't load critics!  Caught exception: No critics
        defined for FollowPath
     The correct nav2_params.yaml has the DWB critic block
     (PathAlign, GoalAlign, ObstacleFootprint, etc.).
  F  Nav2 controller_server "Couldn't load critics" =
     wrong yaml passed as params_file.  Cross-reference:
     look for a `controller_server: -> ros__parameters: ->
     FollowPath: -> critics: [...]` block in the yaml you're
     passing.  If it's absent, you're on the wrong yaml.
  F  SLAM Toolbox standalone with no /scan and no /odom:
     lifecycle configuration fails ("TRANSITION_CONFIGURE"),
     which is EXPECTED (Field Note).  Standalone SLAM
     assumes the perception + odometry stack is already
     running.  Launch the Stormy stack first, then SLAM.

CANDIDATE FIELD-NOTES CLUSTERS growing
================================================================
Michael Wimble's architecture (Manual keystone):
  * (NEW)  "odom topic is NOT odom transform" (tattoo).
  * (NEW)  EKF filter is NOT localization; it's just a
    noise-scrubber over multiple odometry sources.
  * (NEW)  Localization = AMCL or SLAM Toolbox correcting
    Uncle Ernie's odom lies against a map (tf: map -> odom).
  * (NEW)  ros2_control (with hardware plugin) OR node-based
    driver (ros2_roboclaw_driver) - not both.  Stormy uses
    the node-based path; the ros2_control.xacro has only
    the sim plugin.

Launch-file patterns:
  * (NEW)  YAML params files use nested naming.  When passing
    a nested yaml to Node(parameters=[...]) directly, ROS 2
    prepends the nesting to every key -> driver never sees
    its expected flat keys.  Use IncludeLaunchDescription to
    reuse the driver's own launch file, OR parse the yaml
    yourself in the parent.
  * (NEW)  Duplicate nodes in `ros2 node list` = launch-tree
    bug.  Never OK in normal operation.
  * (NEW)  SLAM Toolbox alone fails to configure without /scan
    and /odom - not a bug, just a wiring order issue.
  * (NEW)  Nav2 controller_server "Couldn't load critics" =
    wrong yaml.  DWB critics must be defined under
    controller_server.ros__parameters.FollowPath.

Git divergent-branches:
  * (NEW)  `git config pull.rebase false && git pull && git push`
    is the sane default.

The named collaborators list (updated):
  * (existing) Roberto, Michael Wimble, Sergei Grichine, Nathan,
    Marco, HBRC, Roland Fields, slowrunner, saiaravind19,
    lghrainbow, GitHub Copilot, various AIs
  * (NEW)  ChatGPT-5 (Poe): logo bake-off + YAML-parameter-
    passing bug diagnosis + git-branch consulting.  First
    appearance of a truly-useful AI on architectural fixes
    (contrast with Copilot in Chunk 9 which produced
    plausible-but-wrong suggestions).
  * (NEW)  Multiple image-generation AIs (LogoDesignerGen
    won the Stormy-logo bake-off; also Phoenix-1.0,
    Imagen-4-Ultra-Exp, Ideogram-v2a, Gemini-2.0-Flash-
    Preview, StableDiffusion3.5-L, FLUX-Kontext-Pro,
    FLUX-schnell-DI, Recraft-V3, Luma-Photon).

2025.08.17 continued (L19500)  Nav2 finally activates
  M  ***Nav2 MPPI controller activation (Manual-grade sequence -
     this is the moment Nav2 first came alive on Stormy):***
        Fix chain that got the controller_server past its
        lifecycle-configure error:
          (a) params_file:=<full path to nav2_params.yaml> on
              the `ros2 launch articubot_one navigation.launch.py`
              command (map_server_params.yaml has no DWB/MPPI
              critics defined, hence the earlier "Couldn't load
              critics" error - Chunk 10).
          (b) inflation_radius must be >= inscribed radius
              (Stormy inscribed radius = 0.333 m from URDF
              footprint; use 0.35 m for safety margin, per
              GPT-5).
          (c) EXPLICIT footprint polygon in local+global costmap
              blocks, not just robot_radius:
                footprint: "[[-0.24, -0.14], [-0.24, 0.14],
                             [0.10, 0.14], [0.10, -0.14]]"
              (based on chassis 0.34 x 0.28, with the robot
              origin offset forward by chassis_offset).
     After all three fixes, `[lifecycle_manager_navigation]:
     Managed nodes are active` prints.  Nav2 panel in RViz2
     is ACTIVE for the first time - milestone 2025.08.17.
  M  MPPI critics loaded on Stormy (Manual reference for
     debugging nav2_params.yaml critic block):
        ConstraintCritic (power=1, weight=4.0)
        CostCritic       (power=1, weights 300.0/0.015 for
                          inflation + footprint)
        GoalCritic       (power=1, weight=5.0)
        GoalAngleCritic  (power=1, weight=3.0, angular_thresh=0.5)
        PathAlignCritic  (aka ReferenceTrajectoryCritic;
                          power=1, weight=14.0)
        PathFollowCritic
        PathAngleCritic  (power=1, weight=2.0, "Forward Preference")
        PreferForwardCritic (power=1, weight=5.0)

2025.08.22  (L20395)  ***MOTOR IDENTITY NAILED DOWN***
  M  ***DEFINITIVE MOTOR SPECIFICATION (Manual current-hardware
     chapter; also updates flag 1 Hairball narrative):***
        Pololu product #4753:
          "50:1 Metal Gearmotor 37Dx70L mm 12V with 64 CPR
           Encoder (Helical Pinion)"
          6mm D-shaped output shaft
          Encoder: 64 CPR on the MOTOR shaft
          Gearbox output: 64 * 50 = 3200 encoder counts per
                          revolution of the OUTPUT shaft
          No-load: 200 RPM (typical for this class)
          Product URL: https://www.pololu.com/product/4753
     Verification method (2025.08.22):
        - Encoders read 3200 counts per wheel revolution
          (measured M1 and M2 to within +/-4 counts of 3200,
          both from motor-driven and hand-turned trials in
          BasicMicro Motion Studio).
        - 3200 / 64 CPR = 50:1 gear ratio.
        - Physical envelope matches 37 mm diameter x 70 mm
          long body.
     ***CORRECTION to Chunk 1's original "Pololu 4752 30:1"
     assumption***: the SKU 4752 (30:1) was a mis-read in the
     early notes.  The motor actually installed is 4753 (50:1).
     Manual: cite Pololu 4753 as the definitive motor.
  N  "Sought Stingray documentation ... the original motors do
     NOT have encoders.  So, what ARE these motors??"
     Preserve as origin-chapter note: the Parallax Stingray
     kit motors were plain 12V brushed with NO encoders; Stormy
     runs on aftermarket replacement Pololu 4753 gearmotors
     that James procured specifically to add encoder feedback.
  M  Wheel metrics derivation (Manual - the actual measurement
     that produced 8226 pulses/m):
        Wheel: BaneBots T81, 4-7/8" x 0.8", 50A blue
          Nominal dia:  4 7/8"      = 0.123825 m
          Measured dia: 4 27/32"    = 0.123031 m
          Circumference (measured tape): 0.389 m = 38.9 mm
          Radius:       0.0619 m (rounded 0.062)
        1 m / 0.389 m/rev = 2.57 rev/m
        2.57 rev/m * 3200 counts/rev = 8226 counts/m
     yaml values (current):
        quad_pulses_per_meter:      8226
        quad_pulses_per_revolution: 3200.0
        wheel_radius:               0.062
        wheel_separation:           0.26
  M  ***BaneBots T81 wheel + hub (Manual BOM):***
        Wheel: BaneBots T81, 4-7/8" x 0.8", Hub Mount, 50A, Blue
          https://banebots.com/banebots-wheel-4-7-8-x-0-8-hub-mount-50a-blue/
          $5.00 each (x 2)
        Hub:   BaneBots T81 hub for 6mm shaft
          https://banebots.com/t81-hub-6mm-shaft/
        The hub is what mates the 6 mm D-shaped Pololu output
        shaft to the T81's five-lug wheel-face pattern.  No
        adapters required beyond the hub itself.

2025.08.24-08.25  (L20838-L21125)  PID re-autotune + BNO055 topic
  M  BasicMicro Motion Studio AutoTune values that ended up in
     motor_driver.yaml at this stardate (later refined again by
     Chunk 11's roboclaw_driver v2 arrival):
        Default Accel/Decel: 2000 -> 1000 (smoother)
        Velocity PID (AutoTune, PID mode):
          M1 vP=1.44651  vI=0.23036  vD=0.0  QPPS=12562
          M2 vP=1.29896  vI=0.21047  vD=0.0  QPPS=14062
        Position PID (AutoTune, PD mode):
          M1 pP=43.91656  pI=0.0  pD=492.19844  QPPS=12562
          M2 pP=40.45782  pI=0.0  pD=497.26527  QPPS=14437
     Manual: current-hardware chapter picks the LATEST tuned
     values (10-run AutoTune average, per StingrayStormyEssentials
     p.9); the intermediate values in this chunk are historical.
  F  IMU topic-routing gotcha (Manual sidebar - resolved this
     chunk):
        Symptom: robot_localization EKF (ekf_filter_node_odom)
        was subscribing to `/imu/data`, but the flynneva bno055
        driver publishes on `/bno055imu` and `/bno055imu_raw`
        (with `ros_topic_prefix: "bno055/"` in the params).
        Nobody was writing to /imu/data -> EKF was fusing zero
        IMU data -> odometry drift.
        Fix: in ekf_odom_params.yaml, change
              imu0: imu/data
        to
              imu0: bno055imu
        rebuild, restart the launch.  After the fix,
        `ros2 topic info -v /bno055imu` shows both the bno055
        publisher and the ekf_filter_node_odom subscriber.
     Cross-reference: this was the "IMU disabled during
     debugging, then re-enabled with wrong topic wiring" bug
     that made mapping quality worse than no IMU.  Manual
     needs this called out - it's an easy repeat-mistake.
  F  IMU stream inventory (Manual reference):
        /bno055imu       - fused, calibration-adjusted quaternion
                           (orientation stable to gravity + mag)
        /bno055imu_raw   - raw accel/gyro (no fusion)
        /bno055grav      - gravity vector only
        /bno055mag       - magnetometer
        /bno055temp      - temperature
        /bno055calib_status - JSON string {sys,gyro,accel,mag}
                              each 0-3 (3=fully calibrated)
     Use `/bno055imu` (the fused stream) as EKF input.
     `/bno055imu_raw` is diagnostic-only.

2025.09.01  (L21334)  ODOMETRY VERIFIED
  M  Odometry accuracy check (Manual - reproducible verification
     procedure):
        1. Mark a 1 m line on the floor.  Position Stormy with
           robot centre on the start line, wheels straight.
        2. Read baseline:
              ros2 topic echo --once /roboclaw_status | \
                grep encoder_value
        3. Drive forward exactly 1 m (measure to a wall or
           second mark).
        4. Read final encoder_value.
        5. Delta should be 8226 +/- ~50 counts.
     Reported 2025.09.01: 1 m forward reported 1.39 m from
     robot-centre-to-wall; back 1 m reported 0.39 m from wall -
     spot on within measurement tolerance.  Odometry-scaling is
     correct.  Manual: use this same procedure for any future
     wheel/motor swap.

     motor_driver.yaml for the new roboclaw_driver package;
     Stormy-adapted from Mike's template 2025.09.20):***
        roboclaw_driver:
          ros__parameters:
            # Device
            device_name:    "/dev/ttyAMA0"
            baud_rate:      230400        # NEW - Mike raised
                                          # baud in this driver;
                                          # match in Motion Studio
            device_timeout: 100           # ms
            device_port:    128
            # Publishing rates
            odometry_rate:      30.0      # Hz  (was 67.0 default)
            joint_states_rate:  30.0      # Hz
            status_rate:        10.0      # Hz  (was 1.0 in old)
            # Physical
            wheel_radius:                  0.062
            wheel_separation:              0.26
            encoder_counts_per_revolution: 3200
            # Frames
            base_frame: "base_link"
            odom_frame: "odom"
            # Safety limits
            max_angular_velocity:            0.7   # rad/s
            max_linear_velocity:             0.3   # m/s
            max_seconds_uncommanded_travel:  0.2
            # PID
            m1_p: 1.44651   m1_i: 0.23036   m1_d: 0.0
            m1_qpps: 12562   m1_max_current: 6.0
            m2_p: 1.29896   m2_i: 0.21047   m2_d: 0.0
            m2_qpps: 14062   m2_max_current: 6.0
            accel: 3000       # counts/s^2
            # Publish
            publish_odom:         true
            publish_tf:           false   # let EKF publish tf
            publish_joint_states: true
            # Debug
            do_debug:           false
            do_low_level_debug: false
     Cross-reference the /home/ubuntu/robot_ws hardware preflight
     line in context.txt - it uses the newer parameter names
     from this driver.  Manual: use these values verbatim.
  F  Duplicate node warnings after driver rebuild (persistent
     Field Note - Manual sidebar):
        Symptom: `ros2 node list` shows the same node name
        listed multiple times after a rebuild + relaunch:
              /ros2_roboclaw_driver_node
              /ros2_roboclaw_driver_node
              ... (up to 15+ duplicates seen in this chunk)
        Cause: stale DDS discovery cache from earlier launch
        instances that didn't cleanly shut down.  `ps aux` still
        shows only one actual process.
        Fix (proven this chunk):
              # from any terminal (do NOT need to restart Pi):
              pkill -f ros2_roboclaw_driver_node
              # or the broader hammer:
              pkill -f ros2
              ros2 daemon stop
              ros2 daemon start
              # then relaunch
        For a truly stubborn case: `sudo reboot` clears it.
  N  First real map that PERSISTS during small movements

  M  Odometry verification after new driver (Manual - the
     definitive "your driver is publishing odom correctly"
     smoke test, confirmed 2025.09.20):
        ros2 topic echo --once /odom | grep -A2 position
        # baseline: (0.0, 0.0, 0.0)
        # drive forward 1 m
        # /odom position: (1.0016, -0.0034, 0.0)  <- within +/- cm
        ros2 topic echo --once /joint_states | grep -A2 position
        # /joint_states position (radians):
        # front_left:  16.161   front_right:  16.148
        # sanity: 1 m * 2.57 rev/m * 2 pi rad/rev = 16.148 rad
        # ==>  MATCH.
     If your numbers do not agree within a few percent, revisit
     encoder_counts_per_revolution or wheel_radius before
     touching anything else.
  F  Nav2 with saved map + inflation_radius (Manual sidebar,
     recurring issue):
        Symptom on 2025.09.20:
          [local_costmap.local_costmap]: The configured inflation
           radius (0.331) is smaller than the computed inscribed
           radius (0.333) of your footprint
        Fix: bump inflation_radius to 0.35 or higher in
        BOTH local_costmap and global_costmap sections of
        nav2_params.yaml.  This is the SECOND time the same
        error surfaced (first was Chunk 10 2025.07.29); the
        Manual should just default to 0.35 to short-circuit
        the debug loop.

DepthAI / OAK-D:
  * (NEW) `pip3 install depthai` fails on Ubuntu 24.04 (PEP 668).
    Use a venv per the depthai-python examples README.
  * (NEW) `export OPENBLAS_CORETYPE=ARMV8` required for OpenCV
    on Pi 5 aarch64; else "illegal instruction" at runtime.
  * (NEW) Don't clone both luxonis/depthai-ros AND depthai-ros-
    examples - duplicate `depthai_examples` package will kill
    the build.  Use just `sudo apt install
    ros-${ROS_DISTRO}-depthai-ros`.
  * (NEW) OAK-D running spatial NN + pointcloud can hit 120 F
    case temperature.  Allow airflow.

Nav2 sizing gotchas:
  * (existing) inflation_radius >= inscribed radius (footprint-
    diagonal / 2); Stormy safe value 0.35.
  * (NEW)  MPPI critic wants an EXPLICIT footprint polygon in
    the costmap block, not just robot_radius.
  * (NEW)  Nav2 will silently prefer the polygon if both are
    given but log a WARN.

Driver-generation churn:
  * (NEW) Wimble driver has now spawned three variants
    (ros2_roboclaw_driver main, ros2_roboclaw_driver:baby_steps,
    new roboclaw_driver).  Only one should be on the launch
    path.  Field Note: when a driver author releases a rewrite,
    move to the new repo cleanly - don't leave the old repo
    installed and hope the launch tree picks the right one.

The named collaborators list (updated):
  * (existing) many
  * (NEW)  jetdillo aka Steve "'dillo" Okay (HBRC), OAK-D URDF
    and mesh publisher.

2025.09.23-10.06  (L23100-L24000)  Rotation-calibration frustration
  F  Rotation-odometry symptom (Manual sidebar - PARTIALLY UNRESOLVED
     as of this chunk):
        Physical 90 deg turn -> /odom reports ~40 deg change.
        Physical 180 deg turn -> /odom reports ~57 deg.
        Physical 360 deg turn -> /odom returns to ~0 (as it
        should, since 360 mod 360 = 0), which HIDES the fact
        that each 90 deg leg is only ~40 deg in odom units.
        Linear odometry (1 m forward/back) is accurate to
        within 1-2 cm.
     Root-cause not fully pinned in this chunk.  Candidates
     considered:
        (a) wheel_separation wrong (Mike: try increasing; James
            found this counter-intuitive)
        (b) Cardboard-flat "1 m" rulered distance vs actual
            wheel-contact-point path length during a turn
        (c) IMU disagreement with wheel odom (EKF fusion drift)
        (d) diagnose_localization.py from Mike's Sigyn repo -
            useful analysis tool if Stormy's topics are remapped
            to match its expected inputs.
     Manual: this is the ONE persistent unresolved issue as of
     the mapping-first-successful stardate (2025.11.10) -
     autonomous navigation works despite the mis-scaled rotation,
     because SLAM Toolbox and AMCL correct odom against the
     laser scan match at every update.  The Manual should
     document rotation odom as "not perfectly calibrated; the
     LIDAR-corrected pose is the authoritative one."
  M  Michael Wimble's wheel-diameter calibration spreadsheet
     (Manual reference): src/Robotics_Book/book/media/
     CalibratingWheelDiameter.ods (in Mike's Robotics_Book repo)
     lets you enter measured-vs-commanded travel and solve for
     the corrected wheel radius.  Useful for LINEAR odom only;
     rotation calibration is a separate exercise.
  M  Related tuning parameter (Manual):
        max_seconds_uncommanded_travel: 0.05
     (was 0.2 - Mike lowered this to make the square-test motors
     not overshoot at 90-deg corners; smaller = smoother stops
     at cost of tighter cmd_vel timing requirement).

2025.09.28-10.05  (L23700)  Power-supply death + battery discipline
  F  Bench-power supply for a 12V-motor robot (Manual sidebar):
        Symptom: RoboClaw reads main-battery voltage far below
        the PSU nameplate voltage (e.g., 6.9 V from a 16 V PSU),
        even with nothing connected downstream of the buck
        converter.  RoboClaw shows solid ERR LED, driver quits.
        Cause: Many bench PSUs cannot handle the INRUSH surge
        of a buck converter charging its input caps.  The PSU
        current-limits or foldback-limits and the voltage
        collapses at the output.
        Recognised (per Google/AI overview 2025.09.29):
        "Many power supplies can't handle the initial amp
         surge of buck converters."
        Fix: use a LiPo battery for bench testing too, or
        buy a PSU rated for well above the peak inrush current.
     Cross-reference: Chunk 7 already noted Mike's "shared
     converter -> motor stall sags Pi power" principle.  This
     is the source-side twin: shared PSU -> buck-inrush sags
     PSU output.  Manual keystone: **for Stormy, LiPo is the
     only trusted power source for full-system tests**.  Bench
     PSU is fine only for individual-component testing.
  M  Sarvagya suggested Pi 5 power feed (Manual - alternative
     to the 52Pi hat that Stormy currently uses):
        Pololu step-down 5V 5.5A regulator (Pololu 4091, $35)
        + a USB-C breakout board (Amazon B091CRLJM2, $7)
     Not adopted on Stormy (52Pi hat wins for integration)
     but a valid alternative for a rebuild.

2025.10.10-10.13  (L24023-L24718)  Nav2 config debugging saga
  M  ***REPRODUCIBLE NAV2 LAUNCH RECIPE (Manual - the sequence
     that finally worked reliably):***
        # Terminal 1 (Stingray, if using stingray.launch.py):
        # NOTE: on 2025.11.02 this was superseded by sigyn.launch.py
        # (see Chunk 12 late).  For the intermediate era:
        ros2 launch articubot_one stingray.launch.py
        # ... this brings up rsp, roboclaw, YDLIDAR, twist_mux,
        #     ekf_odom, SLAM Toolbox (localization mode)
        # Nav2 is included as delayed_nav in stingray.launch.py
        # BUT requires proper params_file argument (see below).
     Manual sidebar - the two-line invocation for standalone
     Nav2 (when isolating from stingray.launch.py):
        ros2 run nav2_map_server map_server --ros-args \
              -p yaml_filename:=<path_to_map.yaml> \
              -p use_sim_time:=false
        ros2 run nav2_util lifecycle_bringup map_server
        ros2 run nav2_amcl amcl --ros-args --params-file \
              <path_to_nav2_params.yaml> -p use_sim_time:=false
        ros2 run nav2_util lifecycle_bringup amcl
        ros2 launch articubot_one navigation.launch.py \
              params_file:=<path_to_nav2_params.yaml>
  F  ***NAV2 YAML FOOTPRINT SYNTAX GOTCHA (Manual sidebar):***
        BAD (YAML list form):
          footprint:
            - [-0.24, -0.14]
            - [-0.24,  0.14]
            - [ 0.10,  0.14]
            - [ 0.10, -0.14]
        Symptom: parse error at YAML load:
          "Sequences cannot be key at line 46"
        GOOD (inline-string form):
          footprint: "[ [-0.24, -0.14], [-0.24, 0.14],
                        [0.10, 0.14], [0.10, -0.14] ]"
        Fix: use the inline string, not the YAML list.  Apply
        to BOTH local_costmap and global_costmap sections.
        (Diagnosed by Claude-Sonnet-4.5 2025.10.10.)
  F  ***Nav2 costmap chain of required frames (Manual):***
        map (from map_server or SLAM Toolbox in localization
             mode) -> odom (from EKF) -> base_link (from
             robot_state_publisher) -> laser_frame (URDF)
        Symptom of a break:
          "Invalid frame ID 'map' passed to canTransform ..."
        Cause: map_server didn't publish, or AMCL/SLAM Toolbox
        didn't get past its lifecycle 'active' state.
        Fix order: (a) map_server active, (b) AMCL or SLAM
        Toolbox active, (c) THEN activate Nav2's costmaps.
        If starting components separately, wait for each
        lifecycle transition before starting the next.
  F  nav2_params.yaml minimal DWB critic block needed (Manual
     - the "No critics defined for FollowPath" recovery):
     Under controller_server -> ros__parameters ->
     FollowPath -> critics list, at MINIMUM include:
        RotateToGoal, Oscillation, BaseObstacle,
        GoalAlign, PathAlign, PathDist, GoalDist
     (or the MPPI-controller-equivalent critics documented in
     the current nav2_params.yaml on Stormy).  Copy from the
     upstream Nav2 nav2_params.yaml as a starting point rather
     than trying to write one from scratch.

2025.10.19-10.24  (L24795-L25372)  ***TIMESTAMP MISMATCH SAGA***
  F  ***"Message Filter dropping message" chase (Manual - big
     Field Note):***
        Symptom:
          [local_costmap] Message Filter dropping message: frame
           'laser_frame' at time X for reason 'the timestamp on
           the message is earlier than all the data in the
           transform cache'
        Cause chain investigated across HBRC + AI collaboration:
          (1) use_sim_time must be `false` on real hardware for
              ALL nodes (Sergei).  Grep all launch files for
              'use_sim_time' assignments; any node with
              use_sim_time=true will publish sim-clock timestamps
              that don't match the wall-clock TF stream.
          (2) Time sync between machines (Marco): all machines
              in the ROS_DOMAIN must run chrony/ntpd/
              systemd-timesyncd.  Use Pito Salas's tool from
              github.com/Boston-Robot-Hackers/handy to measure
              skew between machines:
                ros2 run handy net_latency node1 node2
                ros2 run handy net_latency node2 node1
              Result on Stormy/LinuxBox 2025.10.24: 3-7 ms
              skew - within tolerance.
          (3) DDS implementation: switch from fastrtps to
              cyclonedds (per Michael Wimble):
                sudo apt install ros-$ROS_DISTRO-rmw-cyclonedds-cpp
                export RMW_IMPLEMENTATION=rmw_cyclonedds_cpp
              Add the export to ~/.bashrc; do this on BOTH
              Stingray and LinuxBox (they must match).
          (4) BNO055 driver hangs and quaternion norm=0 spikes
              cause TF publishers to stall briefly - see Sergei's
              smbus2 fix below.
        Manual: check all four in order.  #1 and #3 are the
        most common single-machine fixes; #2 matters only for
        multi-machine setups.
  M  Pito Salas's handy net_latency tool (Manual reference):
        github.com/Boston-Robot-Hackers/handy
        Purpose: measure ROS 2 topic latency between two nodes
        on different machines.  Publishes each node's local
        wall clock as a message payload; the receiver computes
        the skew.
     Add Pito Salas to the collaborators list.
  F  Reliability QoS of /scan is BEST_EFFORT on YDLIDAR.  Any
     subscriber that requests RELIABLE (Rviz2 default, some
     Nav2 nodes) will get warnings and no data.  Configure the
     subscribers to Best Effort (Manual sidebar - repeat of
     Chunk 7 lesson).

  M  ***MIGRATION TO sigyn.launch.py (Manual current-hardware
     chapter - this is the CURRENT top-level launch as of
     the mapping-first-successful era):***
     Copied from Michael Wimble's Sigyn robot repo to Stormy's
     articubot_one/robots/stingray/launch/sigyn.launch.py.
     Advantages:
        - Single command supports both mapping and localization:
              sigynln                  # localization from saved map
              sigynln make_map:=true   # SLAM Toolbox mapping mode
        - Uses composed bringup (`use_composition:=True`), all
          Nav2 nodes in one `nav2_container` process for lower
          IPC overhead on Pi 5.

          hardcoded in the launch file:
              map_path = os.path.join(articubot_package_path,
                    "assets", "maps", "Stormy.yaml")
     Manual: `alias sigynln='ros2 launch articubot_one
     sigyn.launch.py'`.  This SUPERSEDES stingln.

2025.11.08  (L25865)  Sergei's smbus2 fork of the BNO055 driver
  M  ***UPGRADED BNO055 DRIVER (Manual - use this INSTEAD of
     the flynneva/bno055 for cleaner IMU output):***
        Symptom (in the flynneva-original driver):
          When monitoring /imu/data orientation/yaw in
          PlotJuggler, frequent (~1-3 second) spikes or jumps.
          Impacts localization and navigation quality.
        Root cause (per Sergei's investigation - see
        github.com/slgrobotics/robots_bringup/blob/main/Docs/
        Sensors/BNO055%20IMU.md#issues-when-using-the-
        standard-ros-2-driver):
          The stock flynneva/bno055 uses the older `smbus`
          Python library.  smbus2 offers better I2C reliability
          on Linux; the stock driver's occasional dropped reads
          become quaternion-norm-zero spikes.
        Fix: use Sergei's fork with smbus2:
          cd ~/ros_ws/src
          rm -rf bno055
          git clone https://github.com/slgrobotics/bno055.git
          cd ~/ros_ws
          sudo apt install python3-smbus2   # not smbus
          rosdep install --from-paths src --ignore-src \
                --rosdistro=${ROS_DISTRO} -r -y
          colcon build --symlink-install
          source install/setup.bash
        Launch (params_file path relative to your yaml):
          cd ~/ros_ws/src/bno055/bno055/params
          ros2 run bno055 bno055 --ros-args \
                --params-file ./bno055_params_i2c.yaml
     Note: Sergei's fork STILL sometimes prints
       "Invalid quaternion norm: 0.0 - sensor reading ignored"
     as a warning, but the ignored reads no longer poison the
     downstream EKF; jitter observed in PlotJuggler is
     substantially reduced.
