2025.11.20  (L26514)  LD19 arrives; ldlidar_stl_ros is ROS 1
  M  ***YOUYEETOO FHL-LD19 LIDAR (Manual current hardware):***
     Replaces the YDLIDAR X2 whose drive belt was deteriorating
     (chronic problem; belt frayed off Chunk 8 era, O-ring
     jury-rig failed by Chunk 10).  LD19 = LDROBOT direct-drive
     360-deg TOF lidar, 4500 samples/sec, 12 m range, 4.5-13 Hz
     scan rate, connects via CP2102 USB-serial adaptor at
     230400 baud, 5V @ 300 mA max startup.
  F  ***REPO GOTCHA (Manual sidebar - big Field Note):***
        DO NOT clone ldlidar_stl_ros for ROS 2.  It is a ROS 1
        (catkin) package; colcon build will fail with:
          "Could not find a package configuration file provided
           by 'catkin' with any of the following names:
           catkinConfig.cmake"
        Correct package:
          git clone https://github.com/ldrobotSensorTeam/ldlidar_ros2.git
          cd ldlidar_ros2
          git submodule update --init --recursive
        Add COLCON_IGNORE to any accidentally-cloned catkin
        packages to keep them out of colcon build:
          touch ~/ros_ws/src/ldlidar_stl_ros/COLCON_IGNORE
  F  ***log_module.cpp missing #include (Manual sidebar - carry
     this pin, next colcon build might need it):***
        Symptom: colcon build ldlidar_ros2 fails with:
          error: 'pthread_mutex_init' was not declared in this
                 scope; did you mean 'pthread_mutex_t'?
                 (also lock/unlock/init variants)
        Cause: `sdk/include/ldlidar_driver/log_module.h`
        (or src/log_module.cpp) is missing the pthread include.
        Fix: add near the top of log_module.h:
          #include <pthread.h>
        Then rebuild; if the header change is already upstream
        by the time you clone, you'll never see this.

2025.11.23  (L26695)  ***NVMe FULL-DISK BACKUP RECIPE***
  M  ***BACKUP-BEFORE-YOU-CUT-CORNERS (Manual keystone -
     any hardware pivot must be preceded by a full-image
     backup of Stormy's NVMe):***
     Setup:
        External USB drive (150 GB+, exFAT).  Do NOT use FAT32;
        it caps files at 4 GB and the split-file dance is worse
        than the reformat.  Plug INTO powered USB hub (bench
        experience: bus-powered USB can drop the drive mid-copy
        after ~90 min).
     One-time drive format (empty drive, all data lost):
        # unmount any auto-mounted partition first
        sudo umount /media/ubuntu/<mount>
        # wipe partition table -> single primary spanning drive
        sudo parted /dev/sda mklabel gpt
        sudo parted /dev/sda mkpart primary 0% 100%
        sudo partprobe /dev/sda
        # exFAT is Windows AND Linux readable
        sudo apt install exfatprogs
        sudo mkfs.exfat /dev/sda1
     Backup command (gzip in-line, `sudo sh -c` wraps the whole
     pipeline so the redirection also runs as root):
        sudo sh -c 'dd if=/dev/nvme0n1p2 bs=4M \
                        conv=sync,noerror status=progress \
                    | gzip -1 \
                    > /media/ubuntu/<mount>/nvme_root.img.gz'
        # ~2250 seconds (~38 minutes) at ~115 MB/s for 238 GB
        # yielding ~13 GB compressed for a fresh Ubuntu install.
        # ALSO back up the boot partition (only ~500 MB):
        sudo sh -c 'dd if=/dev/nvme0n1p1 bs=4M \
                        conv=sync,noerror status=progress \
                    | gzip -1 \
                    > /media/ubuntu/<mount>/nvme_boot.img.gz'
        # Verify the gzip is not truncated:
        gzip -t /media/ubuntu/<mount>/nvme_root.img.gz && echo OK
        gzip -t /media/ubuntu/<mount>/nvme_boot.img.gz && echo OK
     Restore command (from a booted rescue system):
        gunzip -c /path/to/nvme_root.img.gz \
            | sudo dd of=/dev/nvme0n1p2 bs=4M status=progress
        gunzip -c /path/to/nvme_boot.img.gz \
            | sudo dd of=/dev/nvme0n1p1 bs=4M status=progress
  F  Field Note - "sudo does not extend past the pipe":
        BAD:  sudo dd if=... | gzip -1 > /media/.../file.gz
                                              ^ Permission denied
                                              (shell redirects
                                              as your user)
        GOOD: sudo sh -c 'dd if=... | gzip -1 > /path/file.gz'
        or:   sudo dd if=... | gzip -1 | sudo tee /path/file.gz \
                                                        > /dev/null
     Same principle as Chunk 6's config.txt lesson.
  F  Field Note - USB drive disconnect mid-copy:
        Symptom: partway through a long dd, drive vanishes
        (`lsblk` no longer shows sda; syslog "usb 2-1.4: device
         not accepting address ..., error -71").
        Cause: bus-powered drive + insufficient hub current.
        Fix: move to a POWERED USB hub OR direct blue USB3 port
        on the Pi.  Do NOT trust an unpowered hub for the 90+
        minutes a full-disk image takes.

2025.11.24-27  (L27000)  ***CP210x KERNEL MODULE SAGA***
  M  ***Manual sidebar - CP210x on Ubuntu 24.04 / Pi 5 kernel:***
     The FHL-LD19's USB adaptor (VID:PID 10c4:ea60, Silicon Labs
     CP2102 UART Bridge) needs the `cp210x` kernel module.
     On some Raspberry Pi kernel versions the module ships,
     on others it doesn't.  Diagnostic:
        find /lib/modules/$(uname -r) -name "cp210x*"
        # empty -> module missing from running kernel
     Quick check whether ANY installed kernel has it:
        find /lib/modules/ -name "cp210x*"
     Typical fix on Stormy (2025.11.27):
        # Verify a newer raspi kernel is already installed:
        dpkg -l | grep "linux-image.*raspi" | grep "^ii"
        # Should list e.g. linux-image-6.8.0-1040-raspi
        # Boot symlinks should point at the new one:
        ls -la /boot/vmlinuz /boot/initrd.img
        # If they do, just reboot into the new kernel.
        sudo reboot
        # Then confirm:
        ls -l /dev/ttyUSB*      # LD19 should appear
        lsmod | grep cp210x
  F  ***AI-IN-LOOP ANTIPATTERN (Manual + Field Note keystone -
     tag this in the Rules chapter):***
     What DID NOT work (do not repeat):
        Copilot/Claude-Sonnet-4.5 suggested installing
        `linux-generic-hwe-24.04` to get CP210x.  It IS Ubuntu-
        signed and legitimately contains the driver -- BUT the
        generic ARM64 kernel has NO Raspberry Pi 5 device-tree
        file (bcm2712-rpi-5-b.dtb).  flash-kernel's postinst
        script errored out during install:
          "Couldn't find DTB bcm2712-rpi-5-b.dtb on the
           following paths: ..."
          "cp: cannot stat '': No such file or directory"
        Copilot then said "Safe to reboot now."  Rebooting into
        that kernel would have bricked Stormy - Pi 5 needs its
        specific DTB to bring up hardware.
     James's key intervention (quote for Field Notes):
        > "I've been instructed to install an incompatible
        > and possibly corrupted kernel and had to be the one
        > to question the action.  If the latest kernel is
        > already in my system, why am I running an old kernel?
        > Or, how do I know that the latest kernel wasn't
        > corrupted by the previous unfinished install that
        > had errors?"
     Field Note (**AI-in-loop rule #3**):
        When an AI-suggested fix crosses a "boot integrity"
        boundary -- kernel install, initramfs regeneration,
        EEPROM edit, partition table change -- STOP and audit.
        Ask why the answer isn't the shorter, safer path first.
        In this case the shorter path (`reboot` into an already-
        installed newer kernel that shipped cp210x anyway) was
        available but Copilot didn't consider it.
     Cleanup after the bad path:
        sudo apt remove --purge linux-generic-hwe-24.04 \
            linux-image-6.14.0-*-generic \
            linux-modules-6.14.0-*-generic \
            linux-modules-extra-6.14.0-*-generic \
            linux-headers-6.14.0-*-generic \
            linux-headers-generic-hwe-24.04 \
            linux-hwe-6.14-headers-6.14.0-* \
            linux-hwe-6.14-tools-6.14.0-* \
            linux-tools-6.14.0-*-generic -y
        sudo apt autoremove -y
        # Confirm only raspi kernels remain:
        ls /lib/modules/
        # Then reboot into the newest raspi kernel:
        sudo reboot
  M  YDLIDAR udev rule is reusable for LD19 (Manual sidebar):
        `/dev/ttyUSBLDR` symlink from the old YDLIDAR install
        survives and points at the LD19's ttyUSB0 because the
        udev rule matches on the CP210x VID:PID (10c4:ea60),
        which is what the YDLIDAR used too.  Simply set the
        ldlidar_ros2 port to the friendly name:
          # ~/ros_ws/src/ldlidar_ros2/launch/ld19.launch.py
          {'port_name': '/dev/ttyUSBLDR'},
     Manual: keep the ttyUSBLDR udev rule from the YDLIDAR era;
     do NOT hard-code /dev/ttyUSB0.

2025.11.28-30  (L28273)  ***POWER CRISIS + 52Pi PURCHASE***
  M  ***RECURRING BOOT FAILURE - CAUSES + FIXES (Manual big
     block, updates the Chunk 9 power topology):***
     Symptom (2025.11.28 onward):
        Pi 5 reaches Ubuntu splash screen, then reboots in a
        loop.  Bench PSU 16V/OR LiPo 16V both feed the buck
        converter to 5.1V.  No visible sag on scope
        (verified with Digilent Analog Discovery 2).
        Pi PMIC Monitor: "Low voltage warning: please check
        your power supply."
     Diagnosed cause:
        The ANKER powered USB hub Stormy was using to fan-out
        USB from Pi to LD19+OAK-D+keyboard/mouse could not
        supply the peak amps the Pi 5 requires WHEN the
        downstream devices were plugged in.  The Pi 5 draws
        peak transients over 3A momentarily; the hub's
        upstream conductor could not source enough at 5.1V.
        In the process the official 5.1V/5A Raspberry Pi PSU
        was destroyed (whether the cause or a consequence
        of the sags is not fully proven).
     Fix chain (Manual):
        1. Enable full 5A current draw on the Pi:
             sudo rpi-eeprom-config --edit
             # add:  PSU_MAX_CURRENT=5000
             # then:
             sudo nano /boot/firmware/config.txt
             # add:  usb_max_current_enable=1
             sudo reboot
             # Verify:
             sudo vcgencmd get_config usb_max_current_enable
             #   -> usb_max_current_enable=1
        2. Replace killed PSU with two OFFICIAL CanaKit 27W
           5.1V/5A supplies (spare).  Amazon B07H125ZRL.
        3. Bought 52Pi PD Power Extension Board for Pi 5
           (Amazon B0CYPRDY9Q).  Always-on switch + auto-startup
           + manual power control.  Wires 12-24V input to
           a clean 5.1V/5A Pi feed on the GPIO header.  This
           SUPERSEDES using the pi's USB-C jack for main power.
        4. Wire upgrade for the whole power tree (Manual):
             LiPo bus:                14 AWG (up to 15A capacity
                                              of RoboClaw)
             Bus -> Pi buck feed:     16 AWG (10A for <6 ft
                                              w/ <3% drop)
             Bus -> RoboClaw:         14 AWG (silicone)
             Bus -> headlight switch: 20 AWG solder joints ok
             DPDT slide switches for headlights: Amazon B09V77VCF7
        5. Video that James followed (Manual reference):
             Articulated Robotics power fix:
             https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PddIeZP-wgw
  F  Field Note - "Bench PSU with buck converter" from Chunk 12
     confirmed again.  Corollary: even a nameplate-adequate
     powered USB hub is not always adequate to feed a Pi 5.
     Test peak inrush with a scope, not just nominal voltage.
  F  Field Note - "Killed official Pi PSU" (rare - the 5.1V/5A
     official is quite robust).  Preserve one spare Pi PSU
     always; James now keeps two Canakits on the bench.

2025.12.04  (L28537)  Sergei's "Extra RPi5 setup" diagnostics
  M  Manual sidebar - Pi 5 power/health diagnostics (adopted
     from Sergei):
       https://github.com/slgrobotics/robots_bringup/blob/main/
       Docs/Ubuntu-RPi/README-Extra.md
       # Show USB device MaxPower draws (spot the offenders):
       lsusb -v 2>&- | grep -E 'Bus 00|MaxPower'
       # Verify 5A draw permission:
       sudo vcgencmd get_config usb_max_current_enable
       # Verify no thermal/voltage throttling has occurred:
       sudo vcgencmd get_throttled
       #    -> throttled=0x0 means healthy
       #    Any nonzero = past or present throttling; look up
       #    bit meanings.
       # Read internal PMIC rails (Manual diagnostic table):
       sudo vcgencmd pmic_read_adc
       # Look especially at EXT5V_V (should be ~5.1V) and
       # VDD_CORE_A (typical 1.4A under load).
     Manual: run these three commands after every power-topology
     change and note the values in the current-hardware chapter.

2025.12.07  (L28611)  ***Pololu 3779 SHUNT REGULATOR INSTALLED
                        (Flag 6 CLOSED)***
  M  ***REGEN DUMP - Pololu 3779 (Manual current hardware -
     resolves Flag 6 from earlier chunks):***
     Product page: https://www.pololu.com/product/3779
     Purpose: protect the LiPo bus from regenerative-braking
     voltage rises when the motors decelerate.  A 100 W wire-
     wound shunt resistor conducts to ground whenever the bus
     exceeds a threshold, dumping regen energy as heat.
     Configuration:
        - Trip voltage: (Vbatt nominal) * 1.10.
          For a 4S LiPo at ~16.1V nominal -> 16.1 * 1.10
          = 17.71V.  James chose 17.0V (conservative).
        - Threshold set by potentiometer + external resistor.
          On Stormy: 1000 ohm external resistor + pot rotated
          to give 17.0V trip.
        - Wire the shunt IN PARALLEL with the main bus (not
          in-line).  Bus voltage feeds the shunt input; the
          shunt bleeds current to ground only above trip.
     Manual: this is the piece that was flagged 2025.05 as
     "still need to install shunt regulator" - installed
     2025.12.07 and confirmed operating.
  F  Small Field Note: the "* 1.1" margin comes from Pololu's
     example.  Below the trip point the shunt is invisible;
     above it, it draws essentially unlimited current until
     the bus falls back below trip.  Confirm the threshold is
     ABOVE any legitimate charging voltage you might connect
     for balance-charging while wired in.

2025.12.08-22  (L28627)  OAK-D IMU exploration + LIDAR rotation
  M  OAK-D built-in BNO086 IMU exposed on topic:
        /oak/imu/data     (frame_id: oak_imu_frame)
     Location of the IMU (per driver's TF publisher, factory
     calibration): translation (0, -0.015, -0.014) m relative
     to the camera housing origin.  DO NOT re-declare
     oak_imu_frame in URDF - the depthai_ros_driver publishes
     the static transform automatically from the camera
     calibration EEPROM.  Declaring it in URDF creates a
     conflicting TF and confuses Rviz2.
  M  URDF fix - `parent_frame:` argument to the depthai driver
     launch, so its TF tree hangs off the URDF frame:
        depthai_ros_driver camera.launch.py argument:
          parent_frame:=oakd_front_panel
        Then the URDF joint `oakd_joint` -> `oakd_front_panel`
        supplies the physical position, and the driver's
        internal `oak` frames chain under it correctly.
        Set cam_pos_x/y/z to 0 in the launch (URDF has them).
  F  Field Note - "AI overcomplication, again" (2025.12.08):
        Copilot/Claude-Sonnet-4.5 proposed a bespoke "IMU
        correction node" to align OAK-D IMU axes with robot
        axes.  Excessive for a mass-market IMU.  James reverted.
        Later (2025.12.14) the SIMPLE fix turned out to be
        setting `parent_frame:=oakd_front_panel` in the launch
        args.  Field Note keystone: **the shortest fix path
        that touches the fewest files is nearly always right;
        an AI proposing a new node to solve a static-TF problem
        is a code smell.**
  M  ***LD19 static-TF rotation (Manual current hardware):***
     Symptom: LD19's "front" (per LDROBOT convention) is 90 deg
     right of Stormy's forward direction.
     Fix in `ldlidar_ros2/launch/ld19.launch.py`, the static-
     transform-publisher argument list is quaternion-form
     (9 args): x y z qx qy qz qw parent child.  For -90 deg
     yaw:
        arguments=['0','0','0.18',
                   '0','0','-0.7071068','0.7071068',
                   'base_link','base_laser']
        # (mount height 0.18 m above base_link; -90 deg about Z)
        # If wrong direction: use '0.7071068' for qz
        # (positive 90 deg).
  M  Laser-scan-plane height: physical laser is 0.0015 m below
     the top of the LD19 cylinder.  Introduce a separate
     `base_laser` link in the xacro sitting 1.5 mm below the
     cylinder center to make Rviz2's scan display land at the
     right height.
  M  LD19 support geometry (Manual - real triangle, not the
     simple 1-column caricature):
        3 nylon standoffs (1/4"-4-40 female):
          front:  (+x = 0.0234, y = 0)
          rear-R: (-x = 0.00846, -y = 0.02333)
          rear-L: (-x = 0.00846, +y = 0.02333)
        Bottom-plate holes drilled and tapped to 4-40.

2026.01.08  (L29461)  US-100 ULTRASONIC SENSOR (future work)
  M  Manual sidebar - "planned but not yet installed":
     Adafruit 4019 / Amazon B0G4VGV3M9 - US-100 ultrasonic
     range sensor, 2.4-5V, temperature-compensated.
     Two modes selectable by rear jumper:
        - Jumper OFF: HC-SR04-compatible trigger/echo pins.
        - Jumper ON:  9600 baud UART.  Send 0x55, read 2 bytes
                      = mm distance.  Send 0x50, read temp deg C.
                      (Recommended when the Pi/host can't do
                      the tight trigger/echo timing.)
     Beam-angle math (Manual sidebar - trig you'll want later):
        Beam full-width = 15 deg -> half-angle 7.5 deg.  For
        the beam to just clear floor clutter, tilt the sensor
        upward at least 7.5 deg from horizontal.  Given
        vertical mounting-hole spacing of 17 mm:
          opposite = hypotenuse * sin(theta)
                   = 17 mm * sin(7.5 deg) = ~2.22 mm
        so a shim ~2.2 mm thick between the top mounting hole
        and the panel achieves the 7.5 deg tilt.
     Not on the robot as of L30000; keep on the "future work"
     list.

2026.01.10-11  (L29486-L29533)  FabLab hardware day
  F  ***CAST vs EXTRUDED ACRYLIC (Manual sidebar + Field Note,
     BIG one):***
        Symptom: acrylic sheet melts along the laser cut line
        into a gummy mess; edges never clean-cut.
        Cause: Amazon-sourced acrylic is often EXTRUDED
        (cheaper mfg), NOT cast.  Extruded acrylic is
        UNSUITABLE for laser cutting - it melts and beads.
        Cast acrylic vaporizes cleanly at the laser kerf.
        Fix: buy from a supplier that lists CAST acrylic
        explicitly, or use FabLab/Roland's stock.
        Roland's rule of thumb: 1/4" cast is his sweet spot;
        3/8" is possible but marginal.  Extruded is never.
     Manual: **any acrylic destined for laser cutting must be
     confirmed cast, in writing, before purchase.**
  M  Small Manual sidebar - hardware tips James wants to keep:
     - Powerpole crimper: buy the OFFICIAL Powerwerks crimper;
       spade connectors are "too unreliable and fussy" for
       Powerpole work.
     - 4-40 hand tap works well on 1/4" cast acrylic; small
       drill press is adequate for edge-drilling holes into
       the acrylic edge (was skeptical, worked fine).
     - Amazon B0FP2QGTD4 - M2.5 x 18mm + 6mm brass hex
       standoffs (extra long for the new BNO085 mount which
       needs greater plate-clearance than the old BNO055).

2026.01.16  (L29820)  ***BNO055 -> BNO085 SWAP (Flag 21 update)***
  N  Preserve for Manual origin chapter (Sergei's rule of
     thumb, verbatim):
        > "Placing IMU in between the wheels/motors on a real
        > robot is a bad idea.  I place IMUs as far as possible
        > from other components, wires - everything.  I also
        > check mag field with a real compass - the arrow
        > should point North when the robot turns."
     This is why the BNO055 (which had been mounted between
     the wheels near the motor wires) is being swapped for
     the BNO085 on an elevated, isolated mount.
  M  ***BNO085 install (Manual - CURRENT IMU as of 2026.01.16;
     supersedes the BNO055 install in earlier chunks):***
     Physical pinout (from Chunk 11 - confirmed):
        Pi pin  3 BLUE   -> SDA
        Pi pin  5 YELLOW -> SCL
        Pi pin  4 RED    -> Vin (3.3V)
        Pi pin  6 BLACK  -> GND
     I2C address on Stormy: **0x4A** (NOT the default 0x4B).
     Default in the driver config yaml is 0x4B; edit to 0x4A.
     Detect:
        sudo i2cdetect -y 1
        # should show '4a' at row 40, col a
     Driver (Sergei's fork - use this, not stock):
        cd ~/tmp_ws/src
        git clone https://github.com/slgrobotics/bno08x_ros2_driver.git
        cd ~/tmp_ws
        rosdep install --from-paths src --ignore-src -r -y \
                        --skip-keys ament_python
        colcon build
        nano ~/tmp_ws/src/bno08x_ros2_driver/config/bno085_i2c.yaml
        # edit:
        #   i2c:
        #     enabled: true
        #     bus: "/dev/i2c-1"
        #     address: "0x4A"      <-- Stormy value
        source install/setup.bash
        ros2 launch bno08x_driver bno085_i2c.launch.py
     Verify: /imu/data + /imu/mag both @ 100 Hz.
     Manual reference (Sergei's install doc):
        https://github.com/slgrobotics/robots_bringup/blob/main/
        Docs/Sensors/BNO085%20IMU.md
  F  Field Note - **"labels for SDA and SCL on the BNO085
     breakout may be swapped."** i2cdetect showed all zeros
     first try; swapping SDA/SCL physical wires yielded a
     device at 0x4A.  Sanity-check with i2cdetect BEFORE
     assuming a broken sensor.
  M  IMU placement (Manual current hardware): mount BNO085
     on the OAK-D front-panel area (upper deck), NOT between
     the drive motors.  Away from RoboClaw and motor wires.

2026.01.18  (L30105)  INA226 current logger for power characterization
  M  Manual sidebar - "how to measure Stormy's real power draw":
     Hardware: INA226 breakout, Arduino (Leonardo/Uno OK), 2 mOhm
     shunt resistor, wired in the LiPo main-bus line.
     Software: Zanshin `INA` Arduino library, custom sketch
     with start/stop/#comment serial commands (built with GPT-5.0
     assistance; sketch preserved in Chunk 14 source-log at
     L30055-L30130).
     Sampling: 100 ms interval, single-sample averaging, both
     bus and shunt continuous mode, ~140 us conversion.
     Output: one milliamp value per line to serial;
     graph in Excel or PlotJuggler with a trend line to see the
     per-subsystem contribution (base Pi vs Pi+headlight
     vs Pi+2 headlights, etc.).
     Manual: this is the reproducible-measurement tool for
     the current-hardware "power budget" table.

2026.01.19-31  (L30240)  Joystick config drift on sim
  F  Field Note - **your on-disk config yaml is NOT what the
     running node sees**.  Symptom: edit
     `~/robot_ws/src/articubot_one/config/joystick.yaml`,
     relaunch, no change.  Cause: the running node loads from
     `~/robot_ws/install/articubot_one/share/articubot_one/
     config/joystick.yaml` which is the COPY produced by
     `colcon build`.  Fix: re-run `colcon build` (or
     `--packages-select articubot_one`) after any config edit.
  F  Field Note - **duplicate `/joy_node` in `ros2 node list`
     is diagnostic of a zombie or a double-include**.  Kill
     stragglers (`kill -9 <pid>`) before assuming your config
     is wrong.
  M  Manual - Xbox 360 controller default axis + button map on
     Stormy (Manual reference table):
        axis_linear.x:            axis 1 (left stick vertical)
        axis_angular.yaw:         axis 3 (right stick horizontal)
        enable_button:            button 4 (Left Bumper LB)
        enable_turbo_button:      button 5 (Right Bumper RB)
     `require_enable_button: true` so no unintended motion.
  M  Handy aliases for `.bashrc` (adopt on both machines):
        alias build='cd ~/robot_ws && colcon build \
                     && source ./install/setup.bash'
        alias artbld='cd ~/robot_ws/ && colcon build \
                      --packages-select articubot_one'
        alias src='source ./install/setup.bash'
        alias sim='ros2 launch articubot_one stingray_sim.launch.py'
        alias joy='ros2 launch articubot_one joystick.launch.py'
        alias stingln='ros2 launch articubot_one stingray.launch.py'
        alias scram='sudo shutdown -h now'
     Also add:
        export LC_COLLATE=C.UTF-8      # so both machines sort alike
        export RMW_IMPLEMENTATION=rmw_cyclonedds_cpp
        export QT_QPA_PLATFORM=xcb     # workaround for mesa/Gazebo
                                       # crash on some intel iGPUs

2026.02.06-09  (L30637)  Joint states + IMU axes untangled
  M  ***JOINT-STATES PUBLISHING (Manual sidebar - carry into
     the current build):***
     Symptom: no `/joint_states` publisher, wheels missing in
     Rviz, RobotModel display broken.
     Cause: `publish_joint_states: false` in the roboclaw
     driver config.
     Fix: in `ros2_roboclaw_driver/config/motor_driver.yaml`
     (old driver) OR
     `articubot_one/robots/stingray/config/roboclaw.yaml`
     (Wimble driver):
        publish_joint_states: true
     Rebuild the roboclaw driver package.
  M  URDF joint names must MATCH what the roboclaw driver
     publishes (Manual - the Chunk 9 "wheel_left_joint" lesson
     re-surfaces): use `left_wheel_joint` / `right_wheel_joint`
     in BOTH `robot_core.xacro` AND the driver's joint-state
     publisher.  See also the Chunk 14 (2026.03.14) fix #5
     below where the NEW roboclaw_driver was publishing
     `front_left_wheel/front_right_wheel` and had to be aligned.
  M  Manual - Sergei's BNO085 tips wiki (adopt as the Manual's
     IMU-verification checklist):
        https://github.com/slgrobotics/articubot_one/wiki/
        IMUs-%E2%80%90-Tips-and-Lessons
     Quick sanity table:
        Accelerometer:
          flat on floor              -> ax=ay=0, az=+9.8
          tilt nose down (forward)   -> ax negative, az drops
          tilt left side down        -> ay negative
        Gyroscope:
          nose-down pitch            -> gy positive
          roll-left                  -> gx negative
          turn-left (CCW from above) -> gz positive
        Magnetometer (rotate in place, expect approximately):
                  x      y      z
          North  +20      0    -40
          East     0    +20    -40
          South  -20      0    -40
          West     0    -20    -40
          (microtesla; Earth 25-65 uT depending on location)
  M  Manual sidebar - Humble, TX magnetic-field reference values
     (NOAA WMMHR-2025 calculator, at 29.97139, -95.18611):
        Declination:            +1.67 deg
        Inclination:           +58.63 deg
        Total intensity:      46,139 nT (46.1 uT)
        X (North):            +24,007 nT
        Y (East):                +701 nT
        Z (Down):             +39,395 nT
     Manual: use these expected values when calibrating a fresh
     BNO085 mount.
  F  Field Note - **when the IMU orientation resets differently
     on every boot, the driver is using absolute mag-north for
     initial yaw**.  In an indoor environment with local
     ferromagnetic distortion (motor magnets, PC/wiring, LiPo
     tabs) the mag heading is often wildly off - AND unstable.
     Fix has TWO parts, both needed:
       (1) Disable magnetometer in the driver so /imu/data
           orientation comes from the "game rotation vector"
           (gyro + accel only, no absolute reference):
             # bno085_i2c.yaml (Sergei's driver)
             bno08x_driver:
               ros__parameters:
                 imu:                        # <-- TOP-LEVEL key,
                                             #     NOT under publish
                   use_magnetometer: false
                   orientation_yaw_variance: 0.005
       (2) Tell the EKF to ignore any absolute IMU yaw:
             # ekf_odom_params.yaml
             imu0: imu/data
             imu0_config: [false, false, false,
                           true,  true,  false,   # roll,pitch,YAW=false
                           false, false, false,
                           false, false, true,    # yaw_vel=true
                           false, false, false]
     After (1) James's IMU axes finally aligned with the robot
     (2026.03.14).  Manual sidebar: relative-orientation IMU
     is the safe indoor default; save mag-fused absolute yaw
     for outdoor / GPS-fused robots.
  F  Field Note - Sergei's rule of thumb (Manual sidebar quote
     from 2026.01.13): "placing IMU in between the wheels/motors
     on a real robot is a bad idea.  I place IMUs as far as
     possible from other components, wires - everything.  I also
     check mag field with a real compass, the arrow should point
     North when the robot turns."

2026.02.14  (L30870)  OAK-D added to URDF + base_footprint
  M  URDF include path pattern (Manual sidebar - the "which
     description folder?" gotcha):
        WRONG: filename="../../../description/BNOxxx_imu.xacro"
               (points at the top-level articubot_one/description/
                folder; the file that Sergei ships there is NOT
                the Stormy-specific one)
        RIGHT: filename="BNOxxx_imu.xacro"
               (implicit-relative, resolves to the current xacro's
                own robots/stingray/description/ folder)
        or:    filename="$(find articubot_one)/robots/stingray/
                          description/oakd.xacro"
               (explicit package-relative, best for cross-robot
                includes)
     The file `BNO055_imu.xacro` was RENAMED to `BNOxxx_imu.xacro`
     because the physical mount shape didn't change with the
     BNO055 -> BNO085 swap.
  M  base_footprint size fix (Manual sidebar): the FP box's
     length is `chassis_length + 2*caster_radius - oakd_x_length/2`
     so that when the OAK-D projects forward, the FP no longer
     under-represents the robot's footprint.  Same for width
     including wheel_thickness in the y-span.

2026.02.16  (L31200)  cmd_vel drift + button remap
  F  Field Note - **an "unwanted slow rotation" from an idle
     joystick is nearly always a stale-config problem**.
     Symptom: `ros2 topic echo /diff_cont/cmd_vel` shows
        angular.z: 0.3999...
     with the sticks centered.  Cause: previous joystick.yaml
     had non-zero default scale bindings that persisted into
     the running node.  Fix: edit `config/joystick.yaml` to
     current-hardware defaults (see Chunk 14 axis/button map
     above) and REBUILD.

2026.03.02-04  (L32000-L32200)  Zombie processes + mesa/Gazebo crash
  F  ***Field Note - ROS 2 zombie-process cleanup (Manual big
     sidebar - THIS BURNED A FULL DAY):***
        Symptom: `ros2 topic info -v /cmd_vel_nav` shows 7
        publishers all called `behavior_server`; `ps aux`
        shows several twist_mux, robot_state_publisher,
        teleop_twist_joy processes.  Ctrl-C in the launch
        terminal does not kill everything cleanly.
        `ros2 topic list` still returns topics even after
        every launch is dead.
        Cause: (a) previous launches left orphan child
        processes; (b) the `ros2` daemon caches the discovery
        graph.
        Recovery drill:
          # 1. Kill every process from previous launches:
          ps aux | grep -E "cmd_vel|robot_state|twist_mux|teleop|nav2|slam"
          # kill each PID by hand:
          kill -9 <PID1> <PID2> ...
          # 2. Kill the ros2 daemon (it will restart itself):
          ps aux | grep ros2-daemon
          kill -9 <daemon-PID>
          # 3. Verify empty:
          ros2 node list
          ros2 topic list
        Manual sidebar: whenever a launch quits abnormally
        (crash, SSH drop, Ctrl-C failure), DO THIS DRILL
        before the next launch.
  F  Field Note - **`ros2 topic list` auto-restarts the
     daemon** if you kill it.  It will re-populate from cache
     briefly.  Wait a couple seconds after the kill for a
     truly clean list.
  F  ***MESA / GAZEBO SEGFAULT (Manual sidebar, 2026.03.02-04):***
        Symptom on LinuxBox (Intel i7-4500U, Ubuntu 24.04):
          `[gazebo-7] Segmentation fault (Address not mapped
          to object [0xf0])`
          immediately on `ros2 launch articubot_one
          stingray_sim.launch.py`.  BUT: Gazebo works fine
          when run via SSH+X11 from another machine to
          LinuxBox.
        Diagnosis (via Poe/Claude-Opus-4.6, only PARTIALLY
        useful): version mismatch in libmesa after a recent
        apt upgrade.  Full transcript preserved at
        https://poe.com/s/yLhgrRFkbulEc5G2X7fe (with a
        cautionary tale at the end where the AI hallucinated
        a "fix summary" after its context window overflowed;
        see Field Note below).
        Workaround chain (add to `.bashrc` on LinuxBox):
          export QT_QPA_PLATFORM=xcb
          export LIBGL_DRI3_DISABLE=1     # optional
          # Also useful when Gazebo Harmonic hangs on newer
          # renderers:
          gz sim --render-engine ogre     # fall back to ogre1
        Manual sidebar: if you upgrade mesa on an older Intel
        iGPU laptop, expect Gazebo Harmonic (`gz sim` v8.10.0)
        to segfault at start.  Regress mesa OR use the QT/LIBGL
        env-var workarounds.
  F  ***Field Note keystone born here (2026.03.04) - "context.txt"
     as an AI-context-management discipline (preserve verbatim):***
        > "It was apparent near the end that we had exceeded
        > his context window.  I'm thinking of making a
        > 'context.txt' file to attach at the start, and
        > reattach often during, any AI conversation to which
        > I would ask the AI to suggest important additions
        > as we progress."
     Manual: this IS the origin of the `Code/context.txt`
     workflow that James now uses to shepherd Copilot/Claude
     across sessions.  Cite this as the origin.

2026.03.07  (L32800)  OAK-D mesh + material fixes
  M  Manual - **GZ Sim mesh URI fix**:
        WRONG: <mesh filename="package://articubot_one/assets/
                                meshes/OAK-D.dae"/>
               (Rviz2 handles package:// via ROS resource
                manager but Gazebo Harmonic's gz-sim does NOT;
                mesh silently missing in Gazebo.)
        RIGHT: <mesh filename="file://$(find articubot_one)/
                                assets/meshes/OAK-D.dae"/>
  M  Manual - GZ Sim Classic-plugin migration for the OAK-D
     camera sensor (in `oakd.xacro`):
        REPLACE the Classic plugin `libgazebo_ros_camera.so`
        block with a GZ Sim native `<sensor type="camera">`
        block that just names its topic:
          <sensor name='oakd_rgb' type='camera'>
            <always_on>1</always_on>
            <update_rate>30</update_rate>
            <camera>
              <horizontal_fov>1.2566</horizontal_fov>
              <image><format>RGB_INT8</format></image>
            </camera>
            <topic>gz_camera</topic>
          </sensor>
        Then bridge `gz_camera -> /camera` in
        `gz_ros_bridge.yaml` (the file already existed on
        Stormy; just add the row).
     Manual: this is the pattern for any Gazebo Classic sensor
     needing to run under Gazebo Harmonic.  The old plugin
     block is silently ignored (no error!) which makes this
     easy to miss.
  F  Field Note - typo in `horizontal_fov` value
     `1.1.2566` slipped through and was accepted as `1.1`.
     Always sanity-check FOV values by echoing the sim camera
     info topic.
  M  Manual - to color a mesh, edit the `.dae` file itself
     (RViz2's Ogre Collada loader takes material ownership;
     the URDF `<material>` tag is ignored for mesh visuals):
        Add `<library_effects>` + `<library_materials>` blocks
        defining a phong material and bind it via
        `<bind_material>` inside `<instance_geometry>` in the
        visual scene.  For Gazebo, ALSO add `<material>Gazebo/
        Black</material>` inside the `<visual>` block.

2026.03.08-09  (L33000)  Twist vs TwistStamped, Rev 3 (with the REAL robot)
  M  Manual - **Twist vs TwistStamped in Jazzy** (repeat lesson,
     but this time on real hardware):
        Nav2 in Jazzy publishes `geometry_msgs/TwistStamped`
        on `/cmd_vel_nav` and `/cmd_vel_smoothed`.  `twist_mux`
        must therefore be configured with `use_stamped: True`
        (in BOTH the include-launch args AND the parameter
        block of any node it instantiates), else it silently
        subscribes to the Twist-typed topic while the real
        publisher is on the TwistStamped topic and nothing
        flows.
     Full-chain wiring on Stormy (Manual reference):
        joy_node       -> /joy                    (unaffected)
        teleop_twist_joy_node
          param publish_stamped_twist: 'true'
          -> /cmd_vel_joy    (TwistStamped)
        twist_mux
          use_stamped: True
          -> /diff_cont/cmd_vel (TwistStamped)
        roboclaw_driver_node subscribes to /diff_cont/cmd_vel
          Must accept TwistStamped (see 2026.03.14 fix #1).
     Field Note trap - `publish_stamped_twist: 'True'` with
     capital T is a STRING that ROS silently treats as False.
     ALWAYS use lowercase 'true' / 'false'.
  M  Manual - RoboClaw baud-rate table for the driver (2026.03.09):
     The old ros2_roboclaw_driver's `roboclaw.cpp` `switch`
     supported only 9600/19200/38400/57600/115200.  For
     230400 you MUST add:
       case 230400: baud = B230400; break;
     in BOTH `cfsetispeed` and `cfsetospeed` calls.  Wimble's
     newer `roboclaw_driver` (see 2026.03.16 below) already
     supports 230400 out of the box.
  F  Field Note - **old ~/ros_ws still sourced in .bashrc**.
     Symptom: "why is Stingray still using the WRONG driver
     even after I switched?"  Cause: `.bashrc` still had a
     stale `source ~/ros_ws/install/setup.bash` before
     `source ~/robot_ws/install/setup.bash`.  Because the
     older workspace is sourced first, its package overlays
     win.  Fix: remove all references to the old workspace
     from `.bashrc`, `mv ~/ros_ws ~/orig_ros_ws` (or delete),
     open a fresh shell.  Manual sidebar - this trap is
     insidious because both installs "just work" and the
     naming is nearly identical.
  M  Foxglove Studio note (Manual): connect from a Windows
     laptop to LinuxBox via
        Websocket:  ws://192.168.68.99:8765
     NOT localhost (localhost is the Windows machine).  Get
     the LAN IP with `hostname -I` on the Linux host.  Also
     install `ros-jazzy-foxglove-bridge` and launch
     `foxglove_bridge_launch.xml`.

2026.03.13-14  (L33500)  ***THE TWELVE-BUG MOTOR-DRIVER MARATHON***
  M  ***Manual keystone - 12 interlocking bugs fixed in one
     Claude Opus 4.6 session across `motor_driver.cpp` +
     `stingray.launch.py` + URDF + YAML.  These MUST be in
     any Manual troubleshooting appendix because any one of
     them recurring will kill the whole navigation stack.***
     Fixes numbered as delivered by Claude (2026.03.14):
     1.  motor_driver.cpp subscribed to ABSOLUTE "/cmd_vel"
         which ignored the launch-file remap to
         `diff_cont/cmd_vel`.  Change to RELATIVE
         `cmd_vel` so remaps take effect.
     2.  joystick.launch.py had
           publish_stamped_twist: 'True'    # capital T
         ROS silently treats this as False.  Change to
           publish_stamped_twist: 'true'
     3.  Wheel-velocity formula in motor_driver.cpp divided
         the yaw component by `wheel_radius_`.  This made
         angular motion ~16x too aggressive.  REMOVE the
         erroneous division:
           v_left  = v - w * wheel_sep / 2
           v_right = v + w * wheel_sep / 2
     4.  MotorDriver inherited from `rclcpp::Node`, creating a
         second unintended node inside the container.  Params
         loaded to the outer node; `this->get_parameter()`
         read from the INNER node and returned defaults.
         REMOVE the inheritance; hold a `rclcpp::Node::SharedPtr
         node_` and use `node_->get_parameter(...)` everywhere.
     5.  Driver published joint names `front_left_wheel` /
         `front_right_wheel`; URDF has `left_wheel_joint` /
         `right_wheel_joint`.  RobotModel display broken.
         Change the publish strings to match URDF.
     6.  Serial-port RACE CONDITION between `cmdVelCallback`
         (ROS callback thread) and `publisherThread` (sensor
         read).  Per-command mutex was insufficient.  Add a
         COARSE-GRAINED `roboclaw_mutex_` around every serial
         transaction.
     7.  Odometry `frame_id` was `base_link` (wrong) and
         `child_frame_id` was empty.  Correct:
           odom.header.frame_id = "odom"
           odom.child_frame_id  = "base_link"
     8.  Velocity units: `getVelocity()` returned raw encoder
         pulses/sec, but the code fed those directly as m/s.
         Result: apparent velocity ~8000x too high; costmap
         complained "sensor origin out of bounds."  Scale by
         `1.0 / quad_pulses_per_meter`.
     9.  Broken differential-drive kinematics: motor M1/M2
         speeds were incorrectly interpreted as body x/y
         velocities.  Correct form:
           v     = (v_left + v_right) / 2
           omega = (v_right - v_left) / wheel_separation
    10.  "Always-positive" velocity because the RoboClaw
         direction byte was misread.  Fix: compute velocity
         from ENCODER POSITION DELTAS between reads instead
         of the direction byte + magnitude.
    11.  OAK-D TF disconnected because the DepthAI driver's
         default `parent_frame:=oak-d-base-frame` is floating.
         Set `parent_frame:=oakd_front_panel` in
         `stingray.sensors.launch.py`.
    12.  IMU YAML key mismatch: config had
           bno08x_driver:
             ros__parameters:
               publish:
                 imu:
                   use_magnetometer: false
         but the driver code reads TOP-LEVEL `imu.use_magnetometer`.
         Fix: move to top level as shown in Chunk 14 2026.02.06 F-note.
     ***All 12 must be in the Manual's "if navigation is broken,
        check these first" appendix.***
  N  ***MILESTONE (Manual origin chapter, 2026.03.14):***
     After all 12 fixes and IMU alignment: Stormy drove
     Nav2 goals correctly around the ENTIRE HOUSE.  Multiple
     waypoint sequences including kitchen island traversal.
     Manual: this is the "everything wired together works"
     milestone; the 2025.11.10 first-nav was Rviz-inside-map
     only, this is the "Nav2 on the real motor driver on the
     real sensors" completion.

2026.03.14-15  (L33900)  Nav2 tuning + Waypoint follower limits
  M  Manual - AMCL tuning that fixed "loses localization
     behind the kitchen island" (limited unique laser features
     in that area):
        max_particles:            5000    (was 2000)
        laser_max_beams:           120    (was  60)
        laser_likelihood_max_dist: 4.0    (was 2.0)
        alpha1..alpha4:            0.1    (was 0.2)
        update_min_d:              0.05   (was 0.1)
        update_min_a:              0.05   (was 0.1)
  F  Field Note - **`waypoint_follower` and
     `navigate_through_poses` BOTH shortcut through obstacles
     between poses.**  The global planner is invoked
     independently at each waypoint and may not honor an
     intermediate corridor you wanted followed.  Effective
     workaround: place tighter waypoints (~0.3 m apart) around
     corners you want the robot to honor.
  F  Field Note (open issue): RoboClaw enters ERROR state
     (3 red flashes) intermittently during navigation.  Rated
     7 A/motor, driver limits set to 6 A.  Software port
     restart cannot clear it - a physical power cycle is
     required.  Possible cause: `LBmin` (logic battery minimum)
     latched at 5.5 V in Motion Studio, but actual internal
     logic rail is ~3.9 V from the RoboClaw's own 3.3 V
     regulator.  Motion Studio refuses to save a lower LBmin.
     Workaround under investigation: send `SETMINLB = 26`
     opcode by serial (bypassing Motion Studio).  See
     `roboclaw_studio` note in 2026.03.17 below.

2026.03.16  (L34000)  ***MIGRATION - Michael Wimble's NEW `roboclaw_driver`***
  M  ***MANUAL - CURRENT ROBOCLAW DRIVER as of 2026.03.16
     (SUPERSEDES ros2_roboclaw_driver from Chunks 5-14):***
     Repo:   https://github.com/wimblerobotics/roboclaw_driver
     James's fork:
             https://github.com/JHPHELAN/roboclaw_driver
             (branch main; contains Jazzy TwistStamped fix)
     Package name: `roboclaw_driver` (distinct from the older
     `ros2_roboclaw_driver` - they coexist without conflict).
     Architectural upgrade rationale (per 2026.03.15 comparison):
        - Single-threaded timer loop @ 30 Hz - no serial
          contention (fixes the race condition of fix #6 above).
        - Sensor reads distributed across cycles - reduced
          serial-bus load (fixes CRC cascades).
        - Midpoint-integration odometry - more accurate on arcs
          than the old Euler integration.
        - Status published as `std_msgs/String` JSON (no
          custom message types needed).
        - Native 230400 baud support.
        - Configurable publish rates for odom, joint states,
          status separately.
        - `publish_tf` option (still commonly `false`; EKF
          publishes odom->base_link).
        - Apache-2.0 license (older was MIT).
     Migration recipe (Manual):
        cd ~/robot_ws/src
        git clone https://github.com/JHPHELAN/roboclaw_driver.git
        # 4-edit Jazzy TwistStamped fix in the .hpp + .cpp
        #   (already applied in JHPHELAN's fork main branch).
        # Copy the Stormy config yaml to the package:
        cp <old-roboclaw.yaml> \
           ~/robot_ws/src/roboclaw_driver/config/motor_driver.yaml
        # Edit stingray.drive.launch.py - THREE lines:
        #   package:    'ros2_roboclaw_driver'  ->  'roboclaw_driver'
        #   executable: 'ros2_roboclaw_driver_node' ->
        #               'roboclaw_driver_node'
        #   config path -> new package's config yaml
        cd ~/robot_ws && colcon build && source install/setup.bash
     Revert path: change the same 3 lines back; the old
     package is still built in the workspace.
  M  Stormy config for the new roboclaw_driver (Manual reference
     - use as the canonical Stingray yaml):
        roboclaw_driver:
          ros__parameters:
            device_name: "/dev/ttyAMA0"
            baud_rate: 115200          # 230400 also supported;
                                       # 115200 is currently the
                                       # tested-stable value on
                                       # Stormy after 2026.03.14
                                       # serial-reliability fixes
            device_port: 128
            device_timeout: 100
            odometry_rate: 67.0        # Hz - "TeensyV2-compatible"
            joint_states_rate: 30.0
            status_rate: 10.0          # halved from 20 to reduce
                                       # serial-bus load
            wheel_radius: 0.0615
            wheel_separation: 0.26
            encoder_counts_per_revolution: 3200
            base_frame: "base_link"
            odom_frame: "odom"
            max_angular_velocity: 0.7
            max_linear_velocity: 0.3
            max_seconds_uncommanded_travel: 0.05
            m1_p: 1.44651   m1_i: 2.43     m1_d: 0.0   m1_qpps: 12562
            m2_p: 1.29896   m2_i: 0.21047  m2_d: 0.0   m2_qpps: 14062
            accel: 3000
            publish_odom: true
            publish_tf:   false
            publish_joint_states: true
            do_debug: false
            do_low_level_debug: false
  M  Serial-recovery improvements landed in James's fork
     (Manual sidebar):
        - Cmd::execute() retries call lightweight `flushPort()`
          (tcflush) instead of a full port restart.
        - `recoverPort()` always does a FULL restart
          (close + 200 ms + reopen).  No counter escalation.
        - `stop()` now sends duty=0 (open-loop off) instead
          of speed=0 (which was a PID hold that could hunt).

2026.03.17  (L34000)  Wimble's `roboclaw_studio` - Python Motion Studio
  M  Manual sidebar - Michael Wimble's Motion-Studio replacement:
        https://github.com/wimblerobotics/roboclaw_studio
     Purpose: talk to the RoboClaw from Linux (no Windows /
     Wine / BasicMicro Motion Studio required); can send the
     SETMINLB/SETMAXLB opcodes that Motion Studio refuses to
     lower below 5.5 V.  Install path (Ubuntu 24.04 refuses
     `pip install` system-wide - Manual sidebar):
        cd ~/motion_studio_ws/src
        git clone https://github.com/wimblerobotics/roboclaw_studio.git
        # Preferred: venv (safe):
        python3 -m venv ~/rc_studio_venv
        source ~/rc_studio_venv/bin/activate
        pip install -r roboclaw_studio/requirements.txt
        # Or (James's chosen shortcut - risk: OS Python may
        # break; use only if the risk is understood):
        pip install --break-system-packages \
                    -r roboclaw_studio/requirements.txt
     Field Note: the "externally-managed-environment" error
     is a PEP 668 protection.  On a working robot machine,
     prefer the venv path.  On a scratch/experiment machine
     `--break-system-packages` is faster.

CHUNK 15  lines ~34000 to ~38000  spans 2026.03.17 through
          2026.04.05.
Theme: THREE ARCS PLAYED OUT.
       (1) THE ENCODER-KILLER MYSTERY:  RoboClaw EN1 destroyed
           on TWO consecutive boards during Nav2 runs.  Root
           cause traced to PWM motor-current transients coupling
           into unshielded encoder wires - permanent CMOS
           input damage.  Separating M1 motor and EN1 encoder
           wires + adding suppression caps + Schottky clamps
           = fix.
       (2) LBmin FIX + DRIVER SHOOTOUT:  New Command 58
           SETLOGICVOLTAGES header written into
           roboclaw_driver by Claude Opus 4.6, sends min=0/
           max=14V every startup - suppresses spurious 3-red-
           blink Logic Voltage error on the 2x7A V5c (which
           has no external logic-battery input, so factory
           default LBmin=5.5V mis-triggers against actual
           3.3V internal rail).  Deep comparison of
           Wimble's `roboclaw_driver` vs BasicMicro's own
           `basicmicro_ros2` (AI-generated, over-engineered) -
           Wimble wins decisively for diff-drive.
       (3) THE BLUEPRINT-TO-PGM WORKFLOW BORN 2026.04.04:
           after 40+ SLAM iterations (Stormy_v1..v43) produced
           drift-accumulated maps with pinch-point doorways too
           tight for AMCL, James imports his CorelDraw house
           blueprint (2936x1834 CMYK JPG) directly to
           Stormy_blueprint.pgm via `blueprint_to_pgm.py` -
           architect-accurate walls, dock calibrated to
           [-3.407, -8.114] in map frame.  Then hybrid
           approach: `merge_slam_onto_blueprint.py` stamps
           SLAM-observed furniture onto the pristine blueprint.

2026.03.20  (L34200)  ***THE ENCODER-KILLER MYSTERY***
  F  ***HONEY-BEE DANCE PATTERN (Manual + Field Note - big):***
     Symptom: on Nav2 goal, robot fails to leave dock; instead:
        LEFT wheel (M1) jiggles back and forth ~1/8 revolution
        RIGHT wheel (M2) tries to turn robot toward the goal
        Overall motion: erratic circling/dancing, no progress
     In BasicMicro Motion Studio (drydock):
        Manually rotating M1 wheel: encoder value NOT
        proportional; readings either stick at 0 when rotation
        stops OR latch near ~2035 regardless of position.
        Autotune on M1 diverges - motors keep spinning after
        the sequence; only RoboClaw POWER-OFF stops them.
     Isolation test (Manual sidebar - the diagnostic drill):
        Swap the M1 and M2 encoder cables at the RoboClaw
        encoder headers (leave motor power cables alone).
        Symptom stays with the M1 CHANNEL on the RoboClaw ->
        proves the fault is INSIDE THE ROBOCLAW's M1 encoder
        input circuit, NOT in the motor or encoder cable.
     Verdict:  ***RoboClaw M1 encoder-input pin dead.***
        Value 2035 is suspiciously close to half of 3200 counts
        per revolution -> classic "one quadrature channel A or
        B has failed, the other still sees intermittent edges"
        signature.
  F  ***DAMAGE MECHANISM (Manual keystone Field Note, per
     Claude Opus 4.6 diagnosis - "cumulative ESD-like damage
     to a CMOS input pin"):***
        Rate of failure: Roberto's donor RoboClaw ALSO died
        on EN1 within one Nav2 session, same symptoms.
        Two boards fried within days = systematic wiring/
        electrical issue, NOT bad luck.
        Root cause: motor wires and encoder signal wires
        run in the same cable bundle or in close parallel.
        Motor PWM switching creates fast dV/dt on the M1
        wires which couples inductively + capacitively to
        the adjacent EN1 signal wires.  Each transient injects
        a small over/under-voltage into the RoboClaw's MCU
        GPIO pin.  CMOS gate oxide takes progressive damage
        until the input buffer fails.
        NOTE: the Pololu 3779 shunt regulator (Chunk 13,
        2025.12.07) protects the POWER BUS from regen spikes;
        it does NOTHING to protect signal-level encoder inputs.
     Fix chain (Manual - all four together, ranked by impact):
        (a) SEPARATE M1 motor wires and EN1 encoder wires
            PHYSICALLY.  Never bundle.  Cross at 90 degrees
            if they must intersect.  Do the same for M2/EN2
            preemptively.
        (b) 0.1 uF ceramic capacitors at the motor itself
            (across the two motor terminals + one from each
            terminal to the motor case, all leads as short as
            possible) - source-suppresses PWM/brush noise.
        (c) Series resistor 100-330 Ohm on EACH encoder signal
            line close to the RoboClaw + Schottky clamp
            diodes (BAT54S SOT-23) to 5V rail and to ground -
            gives transients somewhere safe to go.
        (d) 5V TVS diode across each signal to ground as a
            simpler alternative to (c).
        (e) Encoder-power bypass cap 0.1 uF at the RoboClaw
            end AND at the encoder end.
        (f) Ensure encoder ground has its OWN return path to
            RoboClaw logic ground, not shared with motor
            current-carrying wires (avoids ground-bounce
            common-mode).
     James's actual field fix (Manual sidebar - what he did on
     Stormy 2026.03.21): re-routed M1 motor wires and EN1
     encoder wires to physical separation; declined the more
     invasive suppression capacitor / clamp diode retrofit.
     Report + Claude's analysis forwarded to Michael Wimble
     and to Nathan at BasicMicro.
  F  Field Note - **Motion Studio "Encoder 1 Mode" mysteriously
     changed to "Absolute" from "Quadrature".** During EN1
     recovery, discovered General Settings / I/O / Encoder 1
     Mode was set to Absolute while Encoder 2 was Quadrature.
     Corrected to Quadrature.  Root cause of the change never
     found - possibly a stray write from an earlier AI-assisted
     session or a Motion Studio autosave collision.  Manual
     sidebar: after ANY driver work, VERIFY Encoder Mode on
     both channels is Quadrature.
  M  Manual sidebar - the "reversed-polarity sanity test":
        Concern: after adding a Schottky diode inline with the
        LiPo positive lead, verify orientation before turning
        on the RoboClaw.
        Method (2026.03.21): remove the panel-mount fuse; try
        to power up.  If power flows, the diode is backwards.
        If none flows (correct polarity), replace the fuse and
        power up normally.

2026.03.22-23  (L34550)  Autotune methods (Manual sidebar)
  M  Manual - three PID-autotune methods available in the
     Wimble roboclaw_driver (BasicMicro Motion Studio offers
     only Ziegler-Nichols):
        (1) Ziegler-Nichols (closed-loop) - drive system to
            sustained oscillation via increasing Kp; measure
            ultimate gain Ku and period Pu.  Aggressive but
            can overshoot.  BasicMicro MS uses this.
        (2) Relay Feedback (Astrom-Hagglund) - replace PID
            with on/off relay, force a controlled limit cycle
            around the setpoint; back out Ku and freq.  Safer
            than closed-loop ZN; automated.
        (3) Step Response / Reaction Curve (open loop) -
            Cohen-Coon.  Apply single step, analyze the
            reaction curve for delay and time constant.
            Requires open-loop-stable plant.
     Manual: for Stormy the Wimble driver's Relay Feedback
     mode is the safer default; ZN closed-loop when you know
     the plant can handle transient overshoot.

2026.03.23-27  (L34800)  ***LBmin FIX - CMD 58 SETLOGICVOLTAGES***
  M  ***Manual keystone - suppress the false "Logic Voltage
     High" error on RoboClaw 2x7A V5c:***
     Facts:
        RoboClaw 2x7A V5c firmware 4.2.8 has NO external
        logic-battery input.  Logic power comes from an
        internal ~3.3 V regulator off the main battery.
        Factory default: Min LB = Max LB = 5.5 V.
        Motion Studio LOCKS these at 5.5 V and refuses lower
        (beeps + reverts).
        Because actual 3.3 V is outside [5.5, 5.5], the
        RoboClaw sets status bit 0x00000010 (mapped
        ERROR_LBATHIGH in manual v5.7) and blinks LED 3-red.
        Error label is misleading (3.3 < 5.5 is "low" not
        "high") but the fault IS real from firmware's POV.
     Diagnosis credit: Claude Opus 4.6 (VSCode agent mode)
     traced this via Command 90 status decode 2026.03.23-27.
     Fix applied (Manual - IN THE DRIVER, at every startup):
        New command header in James's fork of Wimble's
        roboclaw_driver:
          include/roboclaw_driver/roboclaw_cmd_set_logic_
            battery_voltages.h
        Implements RoboClaw Command 58 SETLOGICVOLTAGES which
        writes both min and max as 2-byte 0.1 V units in ONE
        packet (cleaner than separate cmds 26 SETMINLB +
        27 SETMAXLB).  Call in `initialize_roboclaw()` before
        encoder reset:
          CmdSetLogicBatteryVoltages cmd_logic(*roboclaw_,
                                               0, 140);
          cmd_logic.execute();
          // min=0 (0.0 V), max=140 (14.0 V)
     Supporting edits (Manual sidebar - what had to be
     touched):
        RoboClaw.h -
          added #define SetWORDval(arg) macro (16-bit
          big-endian split) alongside existing SetDWORDval.
          added `friend class CmdSetLogicBatteryVoltages`
          so the new command can access private members
          (portAddress_ etc.).
        roboclaw_driver_node.cpp -
          #include the new header; add the execute() call
          in initialize_roboclaw().
     Design choice (Manual sidebar):
        RAM-only (not NVM) - re-set on every driver startup;
        avoids any risk of bricking the RoboClaw via a bad
        NVM write.  Command 94 WRITENVM available if
        persistence ever needed.
        Wide window (0 - 14 V) rather than tight - defensive
        against regulator voltage variation with temperature
        and load.
     Commit + push URL (Manual reference):
        https://github.com/JHPHELAN/roboclaw_driver  main branch
     Verification (Manual - what the launch log now shows):
        [roboclaw_driver_node-3] [INFO] ... Set logic battery
        voltage limits: min=0.0V, max=14.0V
        (no more 3-red-blink at rest; no more
        ERROR_LBATHIGH published.)
  M  Manual sidebar - git recovery from divergent branches
     (this trap recurred; standard procedure now):
        git branch backup-before-rebase   # safety snapshot
        git fetch origin
        git rebase origin/main            # replay local
                                          # commits on remote
        git push
        git branch -D backup-before-rebase
     Manual: `git pull` (or `git pull --rebase`) does NOT
     overwrite local work; it combines.  When in doubt,
     `git branch my-backup` first.

2026.03.26  (L35100)  ***MICHAEL WIMBLE's STARTUP-RACE INSIGHT***
  F  ***Field Note (Manual sidebar - open TODO):***
     During a ROS Zoom meeting Michael Wimble diagnosed the
     spurious "M1/M2 Over Current Warning + Main Voltage High
     Warning" that flashes ONCE at driver startup when Stormy
     is at rest:
        > "The driver starts listening to RoboClaw before it's
        > fully configured, resulting in faulty overcurrent
        > error at rest."
     Errors don't recur once the RoboClaw is fully configured.
     Two clean fixes (Claude Opus 4.6, 2026.03.28) - both
     Manual-worthy, neither yet committed on Stormy:
        (a) Flush the error register at end of initialization:
            immediately after all config commands, read + throw
            away Command 90 status once so the stale power-on
            flags don't reach the main loop.  Cleanest.
        (b) Startup grace period: add
              int startup_status_reads_remaining_{5};
            in the header; downgrade warnings to INFOs for the
            first 5 reads.  Also acceptable; less invasive.
     Reported and deferred to Michael upstream.  Manual:
     document as known-benign transient, list workaround.

2026.03.28  (L35400)  ***DRIVER SHOOTOUT - Wimble vs BasicMicro***
  M  ***Manual keystone - why Stormy uses `roboclaw_driver`
     (Wimble) NOT `basicmicro_ros2` (BasicMicro/AI-generated):***
     Comparison performed 2026.03.28 by Claude Opus 4.6 in
     VSCode agent mode.  Both repos cloned into robot_ws.
     Key dimensions:
        Language:      C++ (Wimble)  vs  Python (BasicMicro)
        Size:          ~3700 lines   vs  ~10000 lines driver
                                          + 18300 lines tests
        Serial layer:  direct POSIX termios, no deps
                       vs external `Basicmicro_python` library
                       loaded via `sys.path.insert(...)` hack
        CRC:           implemented in C++ (Wimble); handled
                       by external lib (BasicMicro)
        Watchdog:      hardware-enforced via distance-limited
                       buffered command
                       (Wimble - motors auto-stop after 50 ms
                       without new cmd)
                       vs software-monitored error counters
                       (BasicMicro)
        Features:      diff-drive focused (Wimble)
                       vs 4 motion strategies + trajectory +
                       servo + homing + arms (BasicMicro)
        Testing:       one on-robot integration test (Wimble)
                       vs 18300 lines of mock-based tests
                       (BasicMicro; note - mock tests exercise
                       AI's understanding of the protocol,
                       not real hardware behavior)
        Provenance:    hand-written by Wimble (proven on Sigyn)
                       vs "Author: Generated by Claude Code"
                       stated in BasicMicro header
        Deploy:        colcon build, zero external deps
                       (Wimble)
                       vs external library, install.sh needed
                       (BasicMicro)
     Manual verdict (from the source): "For a differential-
     drive robot, Wimble's driver is the stronger choice.
     It's battle-tested, self-contained, performant (C++),
     and its hardware-enforced watchdog is the gold standard
     for motor safety.  BasicMicro's driver is more feature-
     rich but is over-engineered for diff-drive, has a fragile
     dependency chain, and its AI-generated provenance means
     it hasn't necessarily been hardened through real-world
     debugging cycles."
  F  Field Note - **the "Author: Generated by Claude Code"
     header line as a code smell**.  When a manufacturer's
     "official" driver is AI-generated and shipped without
     hand-hardening, the mocks may pass while real hardware
     silently misbehaves in edge cases.  Prefer battle-tested
     hand-written drivers for hardware-in-the-loop work.

2026.03.29-30  (L36000)  ***SLAM Toolbox tuning for tight-space
                          and CPU-limited operation on the Pi 5***
  M  ***Manual keystone - slam_toolbox_params.yaml tunings
     that STOPPED the mid-drive TF collapses (Message Filter
     dropping message: frame 'base_laser' ... 'timestamp
     earlier than transform cache' errors):***
        throttle_scans:                   1  -> 3
          # process every 3rd scan (~3 Hz effective)
          # instead of every scan (~10 Hz)
        max_laser_range:                12.0 -> 8.0
          # fewer points per scan for matcher; LD19 physical
          # max is 12 m but 8 m covers all indoor features
        do_loop_closing:              false -> true
          # enabled in mapping mode; catches revisits
        correlation_search_space_dimension:
                                       0.75 -> 0.5
          # smaller search window; faster match
        num_threads:                     4    # added; Pi 5 has
          # 4 cores, prevents Ceres from spawning 50 threads
          # ("50 threads requested on 4-thread build" warning)
     Rationale: on the Pi 5 the SLAM Toolbox scan matcher was
     choking in tight spaces (Stormy between desk and bookshelves
     narrow spot).  When the matcher stalls, it stops publishing
     the map->odom TF; Nav2 costmaps then fill with stale-
     timestamp scans and reject them.  Reducing scan rate +
     search space + thread count eliminates the stall.
  M  Manual sidebar - IMU I2C bus load reduction (fixes BNO085
     watchdog reset cascade seen during mapping runs):
        In bno085_i2c.yaml:
          publish:
            magnetic_field:
              enabled: false        # was true - even with
                                    # use_magnetometer:false at
                                    # top level, the /imu/mag
                                    # publisher still ran the
                                    # I2C traffic
            imu:
              enabled: true
              rate: 50              # was 100 - EKF runs 30 Hz
                                    # so 50 is plenty
     Net effect: I2C bus traffic reduced ~75 %.  BNO085
     watchdog resets (3-9 second IMU blackouts) stop.

2026.03.30  (L36400)  loadgraph with initial pose + theta trials
  M  ***Manual keystone - loadgraph pose arg (Manual sidebar
     with a Field Note on angle convention):***
     Syntax:
        loadgraph <name> <x_m> <y_m> <theta_rad>
     Angle convention: theta is counterclockwise about +Z axis,
     measured from +X (map East) toward +Y (map North).
     Values James found by trial for Stormy (Manual reference
     poses on the current blueprint frame):
        dock (study):              0.0 0.0 0.0
        bedroom door:            19.4 -1.05 +pi/2 (facing S)
        inside bedroom, aimed
            toward bathroom door: 19.5 -2.51 -1.9  (approx)
        inside bathroom door,
            aimed E-W:            15.6 -7.00 ~3.0
     Field Note - theta values in the 15.6/-7.00 batch varied
     unstably (2.91 vs 2.96 vs 3.0 giving noticeably different
     resulting orientations) because the underlying posegraph
     had accumulated drift in the bathroom area.  This drift
     is what motivated the switch to the blueprint-based map
     (2026.04.04).

2026.04.04  (L37300)  ***BLUEPRINT-TO-PGM WORKFLOW BORN***
  M  ***MANUAL KEYSTONE - `blueprint_to_pgm.py` (Manual - the
     tool the current-hardware chapter should feature):***
     Origin story:  after 40+ SLAM iterations (Stormy_v1 ..
     Stormy_v43) the accumulated drift left doorways in the
     PGM pinched to 5-7 cm clearance - too tight for AMCL
     even after Nav2 robot_radius was dropped to 0.18 m.
     Solution:  export the CorelDraw house blueprint
     (walls-only, JPG, CMYK, 2936 x 1834) to a PGM at
     Nav2-standard 0.05 m/pixel using an image-processing
     script.
     Files added:
        assets/maps/StormyHoustMapWallsOnly.jpg     source
        assets/maps/StormyHouseMapXMarksDock.jpg    dock ref
        assets/maps/blueprint_to_pgm.py             converter
        assets/maps/Stormy_blueprint.pgm            output
        assets/maps/Stormy_blueprint.yaml           metadata
        assets/maps/Stormy_blueprint.png            preview
     Scale math (Manual - carry these numbers so you can redo
     for any house):
        Blueprint total width: 81 ft = 24.69 m.
        Source image width:    2936 px.
        Source resolution:     24.69 / 2936 = 0.00841 m/px
                               (~8.4 mm/px).
        Target Nav2 resolution: 0.05 m/px (matches existing
                                Stormy_v29 map).
        Output PGM size:       ~494 x 309 px.
        Downscale via cv2.INTER_AREA (anti-aliased, preserves
        thin walls); re-threshold after resize (< 50 % gray ->
        occupied 0; else free 254).
        Flood-fill from all four image edges -> unknown (205).
     Origin calibration (Manual - the dock-as-reference method):
        Manually mark the robot's dock location on the blueprint
        with a green cross.  Locate the cross pixel in the
        source image; here (2500, 955).  Compute the YAML
        origin from bottom-left corner:
          x_origin = -2500 * 0.00841           = -21.02 m
          y_origin = -(1834 - 955) * 0.00841   = -7.39 m
        Then hand-tune 0.5 m at a time via relaunch/observe
        cycles until Rviz2 shows Stormy's LIDAR returns
        overlaying the blueprint walls.  Final for Stormy
        (2026.04.04):
          origin: [-3.407, -8.114, 0.0]
     Handling doors (Manual sidebar):
        All interior doors passable EXCEPT bedroom 2 and
        bedroom 3 closet doors.  For French doors (study,
        master bath): map the full double-door width open;
        at runtime the costmap + LIDAR handle a partially-open
        door correctly.
        Closet-door sealing implemented separately by
        painting wall pixels across the doorway gap in the
        output PGM after flood-fill.
     CorelDraw scale sanity-check formula (Manual):
        0.73" in drawing = 72" in life
        drawing_distance = life_distance * (0.73 / 72)
     Manual: the blueprint-based map is now the canonical
     starting map (2026.04.04 onward).  Cite in the Manual
     origin chapter as the drift-elimination moment.
  M  Manual sidebar - Nav2 changes required to make the
     blueprint map usable (all in nav2_params.yaml):
        robot_radius:      0.22 -> 0.18   local costmap
        robot_radius:      0.22 -> 0.18   global costmap
        inflation_radius:  0.24 -> 0.20   local
        inflation_radius:  0.26 -> 0.22   global
     Justification: Stormy is 28 cm W x 36 cm L; the earlier
     0.22 m circular radius was the WORST-CASE diagonal.
     Stormy's actual laser-cut chassis is DIAMOND-shaped,
     so half-length (0.18 m) is the correct enclosing radius.
     Also (Manual):
        local  costmap update_frequency:   5.0 -> 4.0
        local  costmap publish_frequency:  5.0 -> 2.0
        global costmap update_frequency:   5.0 -> 1.0
        global costmap publish_frequency:  3.0 -> 1.0
     These reduce CPU/net load enough to hold TF timing on
     the Pi 5 during long trips.
  F  Field Note - `map:=<yaml>` and `localizer_type:=` are
     BOTH now LaunchConfiguration args in stingray.launch.py
     (Chunk 15 edit).  Overridable at CLI:
        ros2 launch articubot_one stingray.launch.py \
             localizer_type:=amcl \
             map:=/home/ubuntu/robot_ws/src/articubot_one/
                  assets/maps/Stormy_blueprint.yaml
     Manual: DEFAULT is now `localizer_type:=amcl` for the
     blueprint-map era; `slam_toolbox` still works with
     make_map:=true for future mapping runs.

2026.04.05  (L37800)  Hybrid: SLAM furniture ONTO blueprint walls
  M  ***Manual keystone - the "hybrid map" strategy
     (SUPERSEDES pure SLAM and pure blueprint):***
     Motivation: pure blueprint has architect-precise walls but
     ZERO interior landmarks - AMCL delocalizes because the
     particle filter has nothing to match against besides the
     bare walls.  Pure SLAM builds furniture features but
     accumulates wall drift.  Solution: blueprint walls +
     SLAM-observed furniture stamped in.
     Tool: `merge_slam_onto_blueprint.py`.
     Algorithm:
        1. Load blueprint PGM (walls + closed unknowns).
        2. Load SLAM PGM (via `map_saver_cli` from a running
           SLAM Toolbox session).
        3. For each SLAM pixel that reads OCCUPIED (value <
           50), find the corresponding blueprint pixel.
        4. If that blueprint pixel is CURRENTLY FREE (254),
           stamp OCCUPIED (0) - i.e. add the furniture.
        5. Skip blueprint pixels that are ALREADY wall or
           unknown - the architect wins.
        6. Save as `Stormy_merged.pgm/yaml/png`.
     Trade-offs (Manual sidebar):
        Furniture in OPEN areas (kitchen island, hall table)
          -> works great, few-cm drift immaterial.
        Furniture AGAINST WALLS (bookshelves, desks)
          -> the against-wall side may vanish (pixel already
             occupied) or produce a thin strip 5 cm off the
             wall (drift artifact).  Both give AMCL something
             to match against and are acceptable.
        SLAM wall echoes near blueprint walls
          -> add ~2-4 px thickness to walls; harmless.
     Manual: run the merge whenever the SLAM session captures
     new furniture (e.g., holiday rearrangement, new tenant).
     Do NOT run the merge blindly if SLAM has severely
     drifted - inspect the output PNG first.
  M  Manual sidebar - `map_saver_cli` to snapshot a live
     SLAM map to PGM (needed to feed the merge script):
        ros2 run nav2_map_server map_saver_cli \
             -f /path/to/Stormy_vNN \
             --ros-args -p map_subscribe_transient_local:=true

11. Motor burnout entry STILL NOT FOUND.  Log now at 2026.04.05.
    Note: Chunk 15 has the RoboClaw ENCODER-INPUT burnout saga
    (2026.03.20-23) - two RoboClaw boards had EN1 destroyed by
    PWM coupling.  If James's "motor burnout" recollection was
    actually MISREMEMBERED as motor rather than encoder-input
    burnout, THIS is that story.  However, the earlier
    clarification "hair-strangled wheel + permissive current
    limit -> winding cooked" is a distinct MOTOR-winding failure
    mode.  Watch remaining ~7000 log lines.  If nothing turns up,
    the motor-burnout event may pre-date the log itself (i.e.,
    early Stingray-1 era 2010-2018) or occurred off-log.

45. Encoder-killer mystery - ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED 2026.07.26.
    Neither electrical damage nor cable coupling.  Cause was
    Motion Studio's General Settings / I/O / Encoder 1 Mode
    silently changed from Quadrature to ABSOLUTE.  Corrected
    2026.03.21; symptoms did not recur.  Claude Opus 4.6's
    elaborate 6-part PWM-coupling / CMOS-damage hypothesis is
    now withdrawn.  Roberto's donor board almost certainly
    exhibited the same Motion Studio setting artifact
    (likely inherited via Motion Studio configuration copy or
    autosave collision).  Manual/Field-Note principle: after
    ANY AI-assisted Motion Studio session, driver work, or
    Motion Studio upgrade, EXPLICITLY verify both Encoder
    Modes are set to Quadrature before powering motors.  See
    Field Notes #17 for the full Field-Note wording.
    Cross-reference:  This is the canonical case study for
    Field Note #8 (AI overcomplication is a code smell).
