2026.04.05-07  (L38050)  Hybrid workflow rolling; power failure
  M  Manual sidebar - the room-by-room mapping cycle (Manual
     reference card; canonical workflow from 2026.04.05):
        # start SLAM Toolbox:
        stingln
        # continue from last posegraph:
        loadgraph Stormy_v<NN> 0.0 0.0 0.0
        # drive one room manually with joystick
        # save both formats:
        savegraph Stormy_v<NN+1>
        ros2 run nav2_map_server map_saver_cli \
             -f ~/robot_ws/src/articubot_one/assets/maps/Stormy_v<NN+1> \
             --ros-args -p map_subscribe_transient_local:=true
        # merge SLAM PGM onto the blueprint:
        cd ~/robot_ws/src/articubot_one/assets/maps
        python3 merge_slam_onto_blueprint.py <NN+1>
        # kill SLAM launch, redock, test with AMCL:
        AMCLtest        # (alias -> stingray.launch.py
                        #  localizer_type:=amcl
                        #  map:=Stormy_merged.yaml)
     Manual: this is the shepherd's crook workflow -
     merge_slam_onto_blueprint.py takes an OPTIONAL second
     argument to layer onto a previous merged file:
        python3 merge_slam_onto_blueprint.py Stormy_v11 Stormy_merged_v10
     Each merge writes a versioned backup (Stormy_merged_v10.
     pgm/.png) so a bad merge can be reverted with `cp`.
  M  Manual - power-tree rework after 2026.04.07 house power
     outage:
        Lengthened wires around panel-mount fuse holder;
        drilled + tapped a permanent mounting hole for the
        Schottky diode; heat-shrink labelled input/output.
        18 AWG wire around the fuse is at spec for 15 A but
        physically visually undersized compared to the 12 AWG
        LiPo main leads.  Rated OK, retained.

2026.04.11-12  (L38495)  ***NAV2 HEADING OVERSHOOT FIX***
  M  ***Manual keystone - goal-heading fix 2026.04.12
     (SUPERSEDES the "disable IMU yaw entirely" advice from
     Chunk 14 fix #12b).***
     Problem: Nav2 goals arrived 20-25 deg past target
     heading after MPPI approach.
     Root causes found (Manual sidebar):
        (a) EKF had no absolute-heading reference - only IMU
            yaw VELOCITY (Chunk 14 config was `yaw: false`
            in row 2 of imu0_config).  Yaw covariance grew
            to ~109 rad^2 during any maneuver = zero heading
            confidence.
        (b) MPPI rotated too fast (wz_max 0.5 rad/s) -
            angular momentum overshoots at goal.
        (c) MPPI GoalAngleCritic threshold_to_consider was
            0.5 m - controller tried to rotate DURING
            approach instead of arriving-then-turning.
     Fixes (Manual):
        # ekf_odom_params.yaml (row 2 = orientation):
        imu0_config: [false, false, false,
                      true,  true,  true,       # roll, pitch,
                                                # YAW=true now
                      false, false, false,
                      false, false, true,
                      false, false, false]
        # Result: EKF yaw covariance dropped from
        #         ~109 rad^2 to ~0.002 rad^2.
        # nav2_params.yaml:
        MPPI:
          wz_max: 0.25              # was 0.5 rad/s
          GoalAngleCritic:
            threshold_to_consider: 0.15   # was 0.5 m
     Result: heading error 2-5 deg, tested across multiple
     goals + directions.  Manual: this fix depends on the
     BNO085 mag-free "game rotation vector" mode from Chunk 14
     staying active (mag disabled at driver level so the
     absolute yaw is stable indoors).
  F  Field Note - DID NOT WORK (record for Manual "don't
     re-try these" appendix):
        - `RotationShimController` caused twirling
          (a well-known Nav2 quirk on non-omni robots).
        - MPPI GoalAngleCritic cost_weight 8-15 caused
          approach wavering / failure.
  M  Manual sidebar - motor autotune refresh 2026.04.12:
     ran BasicMicro Motion Studio autotune 10x on each motor,
     averaged the values, written to RoboClaw and saved to
     `articubot_one/robots/stingray/config/roboclaw.yaml`
     (see Chunk 14 for the yaml layout).

2026.04.14-17  (L38730)  Merge editing + Loop-closure lesson
  M  Manual sidebar - PGM cleanup round-trip (needed when
     merged map accumulates ghost obstacles from prior scans):
        FileZilla: Stormy:.../maps/Stormy_merged.png -> Hank
        Rearden Windows laptop
        Windows Paint: erase / paint corrections
        FileZilla back: Stormy:.../maps/Stormy_merged.png
        On Stormy: convert PNG -> PGM (ROS map_server reads
        PGM only):
          sudo apt install imagemagick-6.q16
          convert Stormy_merged.png Stormy_merged.pgm
     Note (Manual): KolourPaint on the Pi works too but has
     small-screen usability issues over VNC; Windows Paint
     is easier for large-area erasing.  CorelDraw was tried
     and FAILED for pixel-level editing (opens the raster in
     PhotoPaint which was awkward for point-edit work).
  F  ***Field Note (Manual sidebar - the LOOP-CLOSURE lesson,
     Claude Opus 4.6 2026.04.17):***
     SLAM Toolbox only corrects accumulated drift on LOOP
     CLOSURE (revisit of a previously-seen area).  If you
     drive straight out to a far room and stop, drift silently
     compounds.  Fix (Manual workflow rule): after exploring
     a new far room, DRIVE BACK through areas you already
     scanned before saving.  In Rviz2 you will literally see
     the map "snap" as loop closure fires; that snap is your
     signal to `savegraph` immediately.

2026.04.18-19  (L38789)  NoMachine + spin recovery + slower drive
  M  Manual sidebar - NoMachine "black screen after login"
     on headless Pi 5:
        Cause: no HDMI monitor connected -> Xorg has no
        framebuffer.
        Fix:
          sudo apt install xserver-xorg-video-dummy
          sudo tee /usr/share/X11/xorg.conf.d/10-dummy.conf <<EOF
          Section "Monitor"   Identifier "Monitor0"
              HorizSync 30-70   VertRefresh 50-75
              Modeline "1920x1080_60" 173.00 1920 2048 2248 2576 \
                                        1080 1083 1088 1120 -hsync +vsync
              Option "PreferredMode" "1920x1080_60"
          EndSection
          Section "Device"    Identifier "Card0"
              Driver "dummy"  VideoRam 256000
          EndSection
          Section "Screen"    Identifier "Screen0"
              Device "Card0"  Monitor "Monitor0"  DefaultDepth 24
              SubSection "Display"  Depth 24  Modes "1920x1080_60"
              EndSubSection
          EndSection
          EOF
          # In node.cfg (NoMachine): PhysicalDisplays :0
          #                          EnableEGLCapture 0
     Verified working 2026.04.19 - NoMachine shows GNOME.
     ***CAVEAT 2026.05.17*** (Manual sidebar): after upgrading
     the Pi 5 kernel, the dummy config CAUSED "monitor-goes-
     black-after-splash" when a REAL monitor was later plugged
     in.  Fix when using an actual monitor:
        sudo mv /usr/share/X11/xorg.conf.d/10-dummy.conf \
               /usr/share/X11/xorg.conf.d/10-dummy.conf.disabled
        sudo systemctl set-default graphical.target
        sudo systemctl isolate graphical.target
     Manual: KEEP a separate copy of the dummy conf on disk;
     rename it in/out as headless-vs-monitored operation
     requires.
  M  Manual sidebar - custom Nav2 behavior tree
     `navigate_to_pose_no_spin.xml` (2026.04.19):
        REPLACE default Spin recovery with BackUp only.
        Purpose: Stormy sideways in a doorway is worse than
        Stormy backing straight up.  progress_checker
        required_movement_radius: 0.5 -> 0.25;
        BackUp distance 0.15 m for global, 0.10 m for local.
     Manual: applied per the exploration branch of
     articubot_one.  Never regretted.
  M  Manual sidebar - slower default drive speeds for mapping:
        joystick.yaml:  scale_linear.x = 0.2  (was 0.5-0.7)
                        scale_linear_turbo.x = 0.5
     Rationale: SLAM Toolbox's scan matcher is more accurate
     with slow smooth motion; fast turns produce fisheye-like
     artifacts.  Turbo (RB) still available for repositioning.

  N  Preserve for Manual origin chapter (2026.04.30):

  M  Manual - `frontier_explorer_v2.py` at
     articubot_one/scripts/.  Key params (Manual reference):
        min_cluster_size: 6          # min frontier cells
                                     # (4-connected)
        min_clearance_m:  0.40       # halo around goal cell
        near_goal_distance:  1.0
        max_path_detour_ratio: 3.0   # reject 4-7x detours
        blacklist_radius_m:  0.8     # after failed goal,
                                     # blacklist neighborhood
        min_unknown_pocket_cells: 25 # skip already-characterized
                                     # tiny pockets
        goal_cost_check: true        # pre-check goal cell
                                     # against global costmap
        goal_max_cost:   90          # reject inflated cells
        cluster_score_cap: 20        # (was 30) prevents big
                                     # far blob dominating
        distance_score_weight: 5.0   # (was 2.5) doubles cost
                                     # of each meter -> prefers
                                     # nearby nooks
        clearance_score_weight: 4.0
        # Approach-yaw goals (2026.05.18):
        # _make_goal_pose now uses atan2(dy,dx) as goal yaw
        # so Stormy arrives already pointed in direction of
        # travel; no "spin to face east" at every goal.
     Manual sidebar: the explorer WILL stall at cold start
     if the dock view yields no candidates - no motion means
     no new SLAM info means no new candidates.  Known
     workaround: nudge with joystick to break the deadlock.
     Better fix (deferred): send a small in-place rotation
     goal after N zero-candidate cycles.
  M  Manual sidebar - OAK-D as Nav2 obstacle source
     (2026.05.02 - CURRENT config):
        /oak/scan removed in favor of /oak/points 
        (2026.05.07 - single-row laser slice at 9 cm was
        redundant with the full point cloud).
        In nav2_params.yaml local_costmap + global_costmap:
          observation_sources: laser oakd
          oakd:
            data_type: "PointCloud2"
            topic:  /oak/points
            min_height:  0.02          # Stormy collision band
            max_height:  0.22          # lower bound of lidar
            expected_update_rate: 10.0
            marking: true
            clearing: false
     `oak_scan_node` (depthimage_to_laserscan) tuned:
        scan_height: 1, range 0.05..4.0 m,
        frame oakd_front_panel.
     Manual: this catches sub-lidar obstacles (dog, shoes,
     coffee-table underside) that LiDAR-only misses.
  M  ***Manual - POLYGON FOOTPRINT (SUPERSEDES circle radius):***
     Chunk 15's `robot_radius: 0.18` is now REPLACED by the
     actual 8-vertex stingray-shaped hull:
        footprint: "[[0.123, 0.06], [0.06, 0.145],
                     [-0.065, 0.145], [-0.237, 0.03],
                     [-0.237, -0.03], [-0.065, -0.145],
                     [0.06, -0.145], [0.123, -0.06]]"
        MPPI:
          CostCritic.consider_footprint: true
     Applied to BOTH local + global costmaps.  Reclaims
     clearance amidships (near the narrow waist) and at the
     rear taper (near the caster wheel).
     Manual sidebar: DO NOT try footprint_padding 5cm/2cm at
     the same time (tested 2026.05.18) - Stormy became too
     shy to leave the dock.

2026.05.08-13  (L39821)  ***FLAG 11 FOUND!!! - MOTOR BURNOUT***
  M  ***Manual origin chapter keystone AND Field Note - the
     Hairball Lesson realized (2026.05.08):***
     Preserve verbatim (log):
        > "Stormy's left motor stopped turning ... Joystick
        > had same effect - no left wheel action.  ...
        > It was definitely the motor.  Found hair wound
        > around the drive shaft inhibiting movement.
        > Cleaned drive shaft but motor is still no-go.
        > Will have to replace."
     Post-mortem context (Manual sidebar - the mechanism):
        RoboClaw per-channel current limit was 6.5 A (just
        under the 7 A hardware max), while the Pololu #4753
        motor has stall current only 5.5 A and rated
        continuous ~1.8 A.  Hair-jam caused sustained
        near-stall current well above continuous rating -
        winding cooked before RoboClaw's over-current cut in.
        Encoder was fine (ticks still moved on hand-spin);
        RoboClaw was fine (single fluttering green LED, no
        fault).  Motor winding open-circuit or shorted.
     Manual keystone: this IS Flag 11 from Chunks 1-15,
     PARTIALLY IDENTIFIED by James earlier (hair-strangled
     wheel + permissive current limit) and now FULLY CONFIRMED
     from the log entry.  Cite the Chunk 16 stardate.
     Field Note headline (Manual - "The Hairball Lesson"):
        "Check for hair around your motor drive shafts and
        casters regularly.  A domestic-floor robot lives in
        a hairball habitat.  Also: derive your motor-driver
        current limit from the MOTOR's continuous rating,
        not from the DRIVER's hardware max.  Rule of thumb:
        limit = stall_current / 3 * 1.5."
  M  Manual - MOTOR REPLACEMENT (Pololu #4753) parameters
     applied 2026.05.13 (Manual sidebar - the current-limit
     re-derivation):
        Motor: Pololu #4753, 12 V brushed, no-load 0.2 A,
               stall 5.5 A, no published continuous rating.
        Formula:  continuous ~ stall / 3 = 1.83 A.
                  limit = continuous * 1.5 = 2.7 A/channel.
     RoboClaw settings applied (via BasicMicro Motion Studio,
     saved to NVM):
        M1 Max Current:      2.7 A
        M2 Max Current:      2.7 A
        M1 Max Regen:        2.5 A
        M2 Max Regen:        2.5 A
        Idle Delay:          1 s
        Idle Mode:           Freewheeling (flat floor + rear
                             caster)
        Default Speed:       100 %
        Default Accel:       300 %/s   (was 1000 %/s - too
                                        jolty, skidded caster)
        Default Decel:       300 %/s
        S3:                  Default (unused)
        S4:                  Disabled (Duty 20 %, Timeout 10 s
                             - only matters on recovery)
        S5:                  E-Stop (Duty 20 %, Timeout 10 s;
                             wired NO to GND - press panic
                             button -> RoboClaw stops)
        CTRL1, CTRL2:        Disabled
     All settings written to EEPROM and verified persistent
     over power cycle.
  F  Bench-current baseline for Manual power-budget table
     (2026.05.13 at 12 V):
        System idle, no programs running:      0.4xx A
        `stingln` + `stingjoy` running:        noisy, hard to
                                               characterize
        Bench driving back-and-forth:          +0.5-0.6 A above
                                               idle (~0.25-0.3
                                               A per motor)
        At 2.7 A limit + hand-restraint at
        higher speeds:                         OC trips
                                               correctly; motors
                                               halt, recover
                                               on release.

2026.05.08-31  (L40000)  THE INDICATORS PROJECT
  M  ***Manual current-hardware - Headlights + LED beacon +
     WAV audio (`indicators_node.py`):***
     Circuit lineage (Manual sidebar - three revisions):
        Rev 1 (2026.05.08 bench): NPN 2N3904 inverter driving
              IRFZ44N gate from Pi 3.3V GPIO (IRFZ44N Vgs(th)
              too high for direct-drive 3.3V; needed inverter).
        Rev 2 (2026.05.09 bench): SAME topology, verified
              working w/ headlights + LED + piezo buzzer.
        Rev 3 (2026.05.29 - CURRENT PCB, JLCPCB $54.85):
              TWO IRLZ44N logic-level MOSFETs DIRECT-DRIVEN
              from Pi GPIO.  NPN inverter DROPPED.
              Channel 1: headlights (12 V from LiPo bus).
              Channel 2: amber-LED strobe (12 V RC-car strobe;
                         previously 5 V dim LED, upgraded
                         2026.05.11).
              GPIO 17 = headlights, GPIO 27 = LED (both drive
              gate through 220 ohm series + 10k pulldown).
              GPIO 22 = piezo (Rev 2) OR obsolete on Rev 3
                         (audio now via USB DAC + speaker;
                         see below).
              Two IRLZ44N direct-drive at 3.3 V (fully on
              Vgs=3.3 V thanks to logic-level threshold).
     Boot behavior (Manual - critical inversion between Rev 2
     and Rev 3):
        Rev 2 (NPN inverter): GPIO default INPUT/low ->
                              headlights ON at boot; node
                              turns them OFF after grace period.
        Rev 3 (direct-drive):  GPIO default INPUT/low ->
                              headlights OFF at boot; node
                              turns them ON for a running-light
                              window then OFF.
        Manual: match the yaml `headlights_startup_on_sec`
        comment block to your ACTUAL circuit revision.
  M  Manual - `indicators_node.py` behaviour + params
     (articubot_one/scripts/indicators_node.py):
        Subscribes: /joy   (Xbox A=on, B=off, Y=toggle beacon)
                    /cmd_vel (TwistStamped) - beacon auto-on
                                     2 s linger after motion
                    /navigate_to_pose/_action/status
                        - ABORTED goal -> danger.wav
                    /behavior_tree_log
                        - any recovery node RUNNING (Clear*Costmap,
                          Wait, Spin, BackUp) -> danger.wav
        Publishes: /headlights/state (Bool)
                   /beacon/state     (Bool)
        Config yaml keys:
          headlights_pin: 17    (BCM, header pin 11)
          led_pin:        27    (BCM, header pin 13)
          buzzer_pin:     22    (BCM, header pin 15 - Rev 2)
          headlights_startup_on_sec: 3.0
          beacon_led_on_sec:  1.0
          beacon_led_off_sec: 0.8
          beacon_sound_path: "~/wav/STORMY/klingon_alert.wav"
          beacon_sound_repeat_sec: 3.6   (matches WAV length)
          alert_sound_path:  "~/wav/STORMY/danger.wav"
          alert_min_interval_sec: 8.0
          alert_on_goal_abort: true
          alert_on_recovery:   true
          alert_recovery_nodes:
            [ClearLocalCostmap, ClearGlobalCostmap,
             ClearEntireCostmap, Wait, Spin, BackUp]
     Beacon-audio sync insight (Manual sidebar 2026.05.29):
        LED was free-running while aplay was relaunching each
        cycle plus small aplay startup latency -> LED gradually
        led sound.  Fix: RE-ANCHOR the LED phase to the moment
        each new WAV clip starts.  Now LED and Klingon double-
        tone stay locked indefinitely.
  M  Manual - USB audio replacement for piezo buzzer
     (2026.05.15, adopted from bench tests):
        Hardware:  Amazon B00IRVQ0F8 USB-to-3.5mm audio dongle
                   + Amazon B0B4D1BN4F mini 8 ohm speaker.
        Software:  aplay subprocess spawn from indicators_node.
                   Non-blocking (doesn't stall ROS spin loop),
                   no clip overlap (guard flag), immediate
                   trigger on beacon-on, 5 s repeat while
                   beacon active.
        Alias:  alias scram='aplay -q \
                     ~/wav/STORMY/forcefield_powering_down.wav; \
                     sudo shutdown -h now'
     Manual sidebar - audio prep pipeline:
        # MP3 -> WAV:
        ffmpeg -i input.mp3 -ar 44100 -ac 2 -sample_fmt s16 output.wav
        # Boost volume (safe, peak-normalize):
        sox in.wav out_LOUD.wav norm -0.1 gain 6
        # Boost volume (fast, may clip):
        sox in.wav out_LOUD.wav gain 30
  M  Manual sidebar - amber-LED strobe upgrade (2026.05.29):
     Original Lighthouse "ultra-bright" 12 V amber LEDs
     turned out to be ULTRA-DIM.  Replaced with Amazon
     B08G4XCQSW (bright enough).  4 amber LEDs combined into
     bundles with white ferrules, mounted in 5 mm holes drilled
     into the acrylic top plate - lights the entire top plate
     edge-glow.
  F  Field Note (Manual sidebar - Flux.ai review 2026.05.16):
     Tried Flux.ai for PCB layout automation.
     Verdict (verbatim from log 2026.05.16):
        > "Flux.ai isn't AI, it's AS - Artificial Stupidity!"
        > "It was neurotically obsessive and indecisive!"
     $20 Pro tier ate all its ACUs half-way through the second
     project.  Recommendation: stick with KiCAD; hand-lay
     with schematic + PCB.

          User ubuntu
          ServerAliveInterval 30
          ServerAliveCountMax 6

        "remote.SSH.serverInstallTimeout": 300,
        "remote.SSH.lockfilesInTmp": true,
        "remote.SSH.useLocalServer": true,

     edit existing commented lines in place):
        TCPKeepAlive yes
        ClientAliveInterval 30
        ClientAliveCountMax 10
        sudo systemctl restart ssh
     Manual: `remote.SSH.*` settings live in the CLIENT
     (Windows) settings.json, not the Pi's - a common mistake.

2026.06.04  (L41156)  ***THE DATA-LOSS DISASTER***
  F  ***Manual keystone + Field Note keystone - the reason
     the entire manual+field-notes distillation exists
     (preserve VERBATIM as the origin story of this project):***
        > "BAD BACKUP PLAN:
        >  Plan: backup Stormy's SSD to ext HDD."
     James followed the Google Gemini AI Overview `rsync` recipe.
     The AI output missed a critical `/` (or wrote the
     `--exclude` set incorrectly), and the command RECURSIVELY
     COPIED THE DESTINATION INTO ITSELF then wiped everything.
        > "He COMPLETELY screwed me by erasing *&^%$
        > EVERYTHING!  Tomorrow I'll have to completely
        > reinstall everything from scratch, some of which
        > will be able to be cloned from GitHub.  But the
        > rest...!"
     WHAT SURVIVED:
        - `articubot_one` repo on GitHub (main + exploration
          branches), pushed as recently as 2026.05.31.
        - `roboclaw_driver` fork on GitHub (Chunk 15 fix).
        - The entire `Stingray Experience.wpd` log on Hank
          Rearden (Windows laptop) - INTACT.
        - Various Google Drive photos + this file.
     WHAT WAS LOST:
        - The Pi's OS install and all local configuration.
        - Any local-only Python scripts not in the repo.
        - `~/.copilot/skills` clone (trivial re-clone).
        - `~/tmp_ws`, `~/depthai_ws` sandboxes.
        - Bash aliases, `.vscode/tasks.json`, indicators
          config not yet pushed.
        - All rosbags, maps not committed to the repo.
        - Memory files in `/memories/session/`.
     Manual keystone rules from this event
     (add to Manual "backup discipline" chapter):
        1. NEVER run an AI-generated rsync/dd/cp command
           against a real source without first running with
           `--dry-run`.  Read the file list.  Confirm the
           source and destination one more time.  Only then
           drop `--dry-run`.
        2. `rsync` TRAILING-SLASH rule: `src/` -> contents
           of src copied into dst; `src` (no slash) ->
           src DIRECTORY nested inside dst.  Google Gemini
           examples often get this wrong.
        3. Prefer `dd` full-disk image + gzip (Chunk 13
           NVMe backup recipe) OVER `rsync` for base OS
           backup.  Reserve `rsync` for INCREMENTAL, non-
           system, source-tree backup.
        4. AI HAT + SD boot + USB SSD in enclosure as backup
           volume = the CANONICAL POST-DISASTER TOPOLOGY.
           NEVER swap SSD/HAT hot; the M.2 connector is
           rated for ~10s of insertion cycles, not
           hundreds.
        5. Commit + push EVERYTHING that isn't secret
           EVERY DAY.  If it isn't on GitHub, it doesn't
           exist.
  F  Related Field Note (Chunk 6 principle re-confirmed):
     When an AI produces a plausible-but-wrong destructive
     command, STOP.  Do not iterate with the same AI.  Ask
     a HUMAN or a DIFFERENT AI (a different model, a
     different context).  This is the third recorded
     instance of the pattern (Grok bad GPIO, Copilot
     CP210x kernel, Google Gemini rsync).

2026.06.08-09  (L42600)  ROS 2 Jazzy CLEAN REINSTALL
  M  ***Manual keystone - post-disaster reinstall recipe
     (Manual current-hardware chapter):***
     Assumed starting state: fresh Ubuntu 24.04 Desktop on
     NVMe (from Chunk 6 install recipe).  ubuntu user, in
     dialout group.
     Base tools (Manual - one command block):
        sudo apt update
        sudo apt full-upgrade -y
        sudo apt install -y git curl wget nano build-essential \
                            python3 python3-pip \
                            software-properties-common \
                            gnupg lsb-release
        sudo add-apt-repository universe
     UART for RoboClaw (Manual - re-apply the Chunk 5 recipe):
        sudo nano /boot/firmware/config.txt
        # add at end:
        [pi5]
        dtoverlay=uart0-pi5
        dtoverlay=uart1-pi5
        enable_uart=1
        sudo nano /boot/firmware/cmdline.txt
        # ensure console=tty1 only (NO console=serial0,ttyAMA*)
     Disable unattended-upgrades (Manual - prevents surprise
     ROS/kernel updates during navigation runs):
        sudo dpkg-reconfigure unattended-upgrades      # choose No
  F  ***Field Note - ROS 2 apt keyring must be BINARY .gpg
     (Manual sidebar - this bit Chunk 17 more than once):***
     Symptom:
        sudo apt update
        E: Conflicting values set for option Signed-By regarding
           source http://packages.ros.org/ros2/ubuntu/ noble:
           /usr/share/keyrings/ros-archive-keyring.gpg !=
           -----BEGIN PGP PUBLIC KEY BLOCK-----
        (ASCII-armored key vs binary key mismatch.)
     Cause: `curl <url>/ros.asc | tee <gpg>` pipes the ASCII
     armor into the file - apt reads it as different content
     than the same key in the actual `.sources` file's
     Signed-By reference.
     Fix (Manual - the correct recipe):
        sudo rm /usr/share/keyrings/ros-archive-keyring.gpg   # if exists
        sudo curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ros/rosdistro/master/ros.asc \
             | sudo gpg --no-default-keyring \
                        --keyring /etc/apt/trusted.gpg.d/ros-archive-keyring.gpg \
                        --import
        # export re-imported key as BINARY:
        sudo gpg --no-default-keyring \
                 --keyring /etc/apt/trusted.gpg.d/ros-archive-keyring.gpg \
                 --export \
             | sudo tee /etc/apt/trusted.gpg.d/ros-archive-binary-keyring.gpg \
             > /dev/null
        # replace the ASCII keyring file with the binary one:
        sudo rm /etc/apt/trusted.gpg.d/ros-archive-keyring.gpg
        sudo mv /etc/apt/trusted.gpg.d/ros-archive-binary-keyring.gpg \
                /etc/apt/trusted.gpg.d/ros-archive-keyring.gpg
        sudo apt update      # should now succeed
     Manual: apt "unsupported filetype" WARNING is ALSO a symptom
     of an ASCII keyring where binary is expected.
     Then install ROS 2 Jazzy (Manual):
        sudo apt install -y ros-jazzy-desktop \
                            python3-colcon-common-extensions \
                            python3-rosdep
        source /opt/ros/jazzy/setup.bash
        echo "source /opt/ros/jazzy/setup.bash" >> ~/.bashrc
        sudo rosdep init
        rosdep update

     "Discovered last chat was with GPT-4o instead of Claude-

2026.06.15-20  (L43201)  USB hub replacement + TwistStamped + Foxglove
  F  Field Note - USB hub port 2 failed silently 2026.06.15.
     Symptom: keyboard plugged into port 2 didn't enumerate;
     ports 1/3/4 fine.  Replacement 4-port powered hub ordered
     (Amazon B0BVBLQZY8 or similar).  Manual sidebar - keep at
     least one spare powered hub on hand; failure mode is
     silent.
  M  ***Manual sidebar - TwistStamped type-hash mismatch
     (2026.06.20 - this bit AGAIN, third instance):***
     Symptom: joystick + twist_mux running normally, RoboClaw
     driver alive, but motors don't move.  `ros2 topic info -v
     /diff_cont/cmd_vel` shows two message types on the same
     topic.
     Cause: `twist_mux` was publishing TwistStamped;
     `roboclaw_driver_node` was subscribing to Twist.
     Fix in James's fork of roboclaw_driver: change subscription
     type in header + node to `TwistStamped`.  Same 4-edit
     pattern as Chunk 14 fix #12 (motor_driver.cpp of the OLD
     driver) and Chunk 15 (new driver's initial fix).
     Manual: EVERY driver-swap on Stormy must be checked for
     Twist-vs-TwistStamped consistency across the full chain
     from teleop -> twist_mux -> driver.
  M  Manual sidebar - Foxglove `fox` alias correction
     (2026.06.20 - subtle):
        WRONG (loaded rosbridge_server on 9090):
          alias fox='ros2 launch rosbridge_server \
                     rosbridge_websocket_launch.xml'
        RIGHT (loads foxglove_bridge on 8765):
          alias fox='ros2 launch foxglove_bridge \
                     foxglove_bridge_launch.xml'
     They are DIFFERENT protocols.  Foxglove Studio speaks the
     `foxglove_bridge` (native binary) protocol on port 8765.
     Studio can ALSO speak plain rosbridge JSON on 9090 but the
     performance is much worse.  Manual: `fox` alias must
     launch `foxglove_bridge`.
  M  Manual sidebar - keepout mask QoS fix (2026.06.20):
     Symptom: after launch, RViz2 keepout display was empty
     even though `keepout.launch.py` published successfully.
     Cause: the RViz subscriber requested Best Effort QoS on
     a topic that is Reliable + transient_local (latched).
     Best Effort subscriber DISCARDS the latched sample during
     the DDS handshake.
     Fix (in RViz2 display properties for the mask topic):
     Reliability = Reliable, Durability = Transient Local.
     Manual: for ANY latched topic (map, keepout, tf_static
     analogs), always match the publisher's QoS profile.

2026.06.27  (L43551)  ***THE 2.4 GHz WiFi FIASCO***
  F  ***Manual + Field Note keystone (2026.06.27):***
        James spent an EVENING trying to move Stormy from 5 GHz
        to 2.4 GHz for better through-wall range.  EVERY attempt
        (main SSID pinned to 2.4 GHz BSSID; new dedicated
        2.4-only guest SSID; nmcli band=bg pin; wpa_supplicant
        BSS cache flush; cloned MAC address pin) failed with
        wpa_supplicant `status_code=16` (authentication timeout).
     ***Root cause*** (Manual sidebar - preserve as canonical
     Pi 5 fact):
        The Raspberry Pi 5's onboard WiFi has WELL-DOCUMENTED
        interference issues on 2.4 GHz - from CPU, USB3, HDMI,
        and PMIC noise coupling into the small PCB antenna.
        The 5 GHz radio is largely immune.  On this
        particular Pi 5 + Deco mesh combination, 2.4 GHz
        association CANNOT complete reliably.
        Verified: identical Deco guest SSID connected fine
        from a phone; only Stormy's Pi 5 failed.
     Verbatim rueful quote (Manual origin chapter):
        > "The Raspberry Pi 5 has a well-documented
        > interference problem on 2.4 GHz.  Might have
        > brought that up at the start and saved us this
        > rabbit hole!"
     Recovery pattern (Manual - the "safety-net" rule):
        Any WiFi change you can't undo from the console
        must be preceded by:
          sudo nohup sh -c \
              'sleep 300; nmcli con up <known-good-connection>' &
        so if the change strands you, the auto-revert fires
        in 5 min.
     Verdict (Manual): STAY ON 5 GHz for the internal Pi 5
     radio.  For 2.4 GHz range, use an EXTERNAL USB adapter
     (see below).
  M  Manual sidebar - Realtek RTL8812BU USB WiFi dongle
     (adopted 2026.06.27; ordered from Amazon B078NSSM7W):
        Dual-band AC1300 (867 Mbps @ 5 GHz, 400 Mbps @ 2.4 GHz).
        Bypasses the Pi 5's 2.4 GHz RF issues by moving the
        antenna off-board with its own 5 dBi external antenna.
        Power draw: up to 500 mA (0.25 A avg) - use powered
        USB hub, not direct to Pi.
     Manual sidebar - powered-hub topology after adding dongle
     (2026.06.27, adopted 2026.07):
        Pi USB 3.0 port 1:  OAK-D direct (own 52Pi aux power)
        Pi USB 3.0 port 2:  free
        Pi USB 2.0 port 1:  powered hub uplink
        Pi USB 2.0 port 2:  free
        Hub (powered from 5.1V / 3A buck off LiPo):
          - RTL8812BU WiFi dongle
          - LD19 LiDAR (was on Pi USB3; USB2 is enough)
          - USB audio DAC
          - drydock mouse + keyboard
     Buck-converter spec (Manual): 5.1 V (NOT 5.25 V - some USB
     devices dislike upper spec edge), 3 A capacity for 850 mA
     peak with headroom.

  F  ***RVIZ MULTI-VISUAL MATERIAL BUG (Manual keystone Field
     Note - preserve as an RViz gotcha):***
        Symptom: when a single <link> contains MULTIPLE <visual>
        elements with DIFFERENT <material> tags, RViz2 paints
        ALL visuals with the FIRST material.  Per-visual
        material tags after the first are silently ignored,
        even with unique material names + inline rgba.
        Fix: extract each differently-colored visual into its
        OWN <link>, connect via a fixed joint.  Fixed joints
        propagate rotation, so this doesn't break continuous
        joints (wheels).
     Applied on Stormy: speaker (split body+cone), both wheels
     (split tire+hub+marker), e-stop (split skirt+shaft+button).

                     [0.123, -0.06]]"

2026.07.02-11  (L43990 to end)  ***THE WiFi ODYSSEY***
  M  ***Manual - `wifi_publisher.py` node (Manual sidebar):***
     Location: `articubot_one/scripts/wifi_publisher.py`.
     Installed via `install(PROGRAMS ...)` in CMakeLists.txt.
     Reads `iw dev <iface> link` (preferred) + `/proc/net/
     wireless` (fallback) at 1 Hz.  Publishes SIX topics per
     wireless interface:
        /wifi/<iface>/signal_dbm       std_msgs/Float32
        /wifi/<iface>/quality          std_msgs/Float32
        /wifi/<iface>/bitrate_mbps     std_msgs/Float32
        /wifi/<iface>/tx_bitrate_mbps
        /wifi/<iface>/rx_bitrate_mbps
        /wifi/<iface>/signal_avg_dbm
        /wifi/<iface>/frequency_mhz
     Auto-detects interfaces (both wlan0 and wlxUSB when both
     up).  Hardened 2026.07.06:
        - Prefer `iw dev link` because some USB drivers report
          dBm without the IW_QUAL_DBM flag, causing /proc to
          emit `raw - 0x100` = garbage values (e.g. raw 1 ->
          -255).
        - Drop kernel "no measurement" sentinels: -255.0,
          -256.0, 0.0.
        - Plausibility ranges: RSSI -110..-10 dBm, quality
          0..100, bitrate 0.1..15000 Mbps, freq 2000..7500 MHz.
        - Per-metric throttled warnings on rejected samples
          (`reject_warn_period_sec`, default 10 s).
     Manual reference:
        cat >> ~/.bash_aliases <<'EOF'
        alias wifi='ros2 run articubot_one wifi_publisher.py'
        EOF
     Add to `stingray.launch.py` when auto-start desired.
  M  Manual sidebar - Sergei's `wifi_logger_visualizer`
     (adopted 2026.07.03; JHPHELAN contributed rainbow-heat-
     map PR 2026.07.05):
        Repo: github.com/slgrobotics/wifi_logger_visualizer
        Nodes: `wifi_logger.launch.py` samples signal at each
        map cell, writes to SQLite.  `heat_mapper.launch.py`
        reads the DB and publishes /wifi_heat_markers +
        /wifi_text_markers to RViz.
     JHPHELAN's PR improvements (Manual - all merged):
        - ROYGB rainbow ramp (5-stop, no muddy midtones)
        - Auto-normalize to actual min/max signal
        - Text labels drop leading '-' and tenths precision
          (so "42" fits in a cell instead of "-42.3")
        - `normalize` param + `marker_alpha` param
        - Fixed a latent bug: text markers were deepcopied
          BEFORE color assignment (labels wore wrong colors)
        - Standalone matplotlib heatmap: cmap `coolwarm` ->
          `turbo_r` for direction match with RViz.
     Manual sidebar - launch:
        ros2 launch wifi_logger_visualizer wifi_logger.launch.py
        # (drive around while it runs)
        ros2 launch wifi_logger_visualizer heat_mapper.launch.py \
             standalone:=true \
             db_path:=/home/ubuntu/robot_ws/src/wifi_logger_visualizer/wifi_data.db
  M  ***Manual keystone - default-route metric bug (fixed
     2026.07.08):***
     Symptom (Chunk 17): even after adding the USB WiFi
     dongle, real unicast traffic (SSH, Foxglove, ROS 2 DDS)
     was still going out `wlan0` (Pi's onboard radio).
     Diagnosis:
        `ip -o -4 route show default`
        default via 192.168.68.1 dev wlan0            metric 200
        default via 192.168.68.1 dev wlx90de801012a6  metric 601
     Lower metric wins.  wlan0 was 200 (netplan config
     `01-network-config.yaml` hard-set) vs USB's default 601
     (from DHCP).
     Also: even with the metric right, TCP traffic INBOUND
     (Foxglove client dialing the WebSocket, SSH from your
     laptop) binds to whichever IP was dialed - the metric
     doesn't switch that.
     Fix (Manual):
        # promote USB, demote wlan0:
        sudo cp /etc/netplan/01-network-config.yaml \
                /etc/netplan/01-network-config.yaml.bak.$(date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S)
        sudo sed -i 's/metric: 200/metric: 300/' \
                    /etc/netplan/01-network-config.yaml
        sudo nmcli con modify H0bb1tH0l3 ipv4.route-metric 100
        sudo netplan apply
     Verify:
        default via 192.168.68.1 dev wlx90de801012a6  metric 100
        default via 192.168.68.1 dev wlan0            metric 300
  M  ***Manual keystone - DISABLE wlan0 ENTIRELY (2026.07.10):***
     Metric change alone leaked traffic to wlan0 (Foxglove/SSH
     inbound bind, DDS multi-locator advertisement).  Solution
     as of end-of-log: disable wlan0 completely.
        # (SSH in over USB dongle IP 192.168.68.66 first!)
        sudo nmcli con modify netplan-wlan0-H0bb1tH0l3 \
                              connection.autoconnect no
        sudo nmcli dev disconnect wlan0
        hostname -I     # should show ONLY 192.168.68.66
     To re-enable (if USB dongle fails):
        sudo nmcli con modify netplan-wlan0-H0bb1tH0l3 \
                              connection.autoconnect yes
        sudo nmcli con up netplan-wlan0-H0bb1tH0l3
     Manual sidebar: SSH connection profile in PuTTY was
     updated to `Stingray-wlxUSB -> 192.168.68.66` so future
     sessions don't rely on wlan0 being up.
  M  Manual sidebar - Deco mesh reshuffle 2026.07.10:
     Three TP-Link Deco units on ethernet backhaul.  All same
     model (OUI 34:60:F9).  Radio BSSIDs = sticker MAC + 2
     (2.4 GHz) and +3 (5 GHz).  Layout AFTER 2026.07.10 move:
        Study       34:60:F9:5D:7C:14
        Sewing      34:60:F9:5D:7B:C4 (upstairs, over living)
        Breakfast   34:60:F9:5D:7C:B0 (moved from Master Closet
                    which was walled off with poor coverage)

     TP-Link Deco app for LinuxBox .99 and Stingray .66 so
     the CycloneDDS Peers list (below) never points at a
     stale address.

2026.07.10  (L44607)  ***CYCLONEDDS MULTICAST DROP - CRITICAL FIX***
  M  ***MANUAL KEYSTONE (preserve verbatim; this is a common
     surprise that will bite anyone adopting Stormy's config):
     After the WiFi-only switchover, ROS 2 topics stopped
     propagating between LinuxBox and Stingray.***
     Symptoms:
        On LinuxBox: `ros2 topic list | wc -l` = 5 (defaults
        only) instead of Stormy's ~135.  RViz2 shows "Frame
        [map] does not exist"; no robot / map / scan.
        Ping between the two hosts: PERFECT.  Unicast works.
     Root cause:
        (a) TP-Link Deco mesh DROPS multicast between wired
            and wireless clients (typical Deco firmware
            behavior).
        (b) LinuxBox is dual-homed on the SAME /22 subnet
            (enp3s0f1 ethernet 192.168.68.99 + wlp2s0 WiFi
            .98).  CycloneDDS silently binds multicast to
            ONE interface and the other side may never see
            SPDP heartbeats.
        DDS discovery uses UDP MULTICAST by default - it
        died silently.  Unicast traffic (ping, SSH) worked
        the whole time, masking the root cause.
     ***Fix - switch CycloneDDS to UNICAST-ONLY discovery***
     (Manual - THE canonical fix on Stormy as of end-of-log):
        # On BOTH LinuxBox and Stingray:
        cat > ~/cyclonedds.xml <<'EOF'
        <CycloneDDS>
          <Domain>
            <General>
              <Interfaces>
                <NetworkInterface name="<IFACE>" priority="10"/>
              </Interfaces>
              <AllowMulticast>false</AllowMulticast>
            </General>
            <Discovery>
              <ParticipantIndex>auto</ParticipantIndex>
              <Peers>
                <Peer address="192.168.68.66"/>    <!-- Stingray USB -->
                <Peer address="192.168.68.99"/>    <!-- LinuxBox eth -->
              </Peers>
            </Discovery>
          </Domain>
        </CycloneDDS>
        EOF
        # <IFACE> = enp3s0f1 on LinuxBox
        #        = wlx90de801012a6 on Stingray
        echo 'export CYCLONEDDS_URI=file://$HOME/cyclonedds.xml' \
             >> ~/.bashrc
        source ~/.bashrc
        # Kill and restart the ros2 daemon so it picks up the URI:
        ros2 daemon stop && ros2 daemon start
        # Verify:
        echo $CYCLONEDDS_URI       # should print the file:// path
        ros2 topic list | wc -l    # should be ~135
     Manual: this file is now part of `stingray-dotfiles`
     backup.  For any future addition of a THIRD host on the
     ROS network, ADD its IP to the `<Peers>` list on ALL
     participants.
  F  Field Note - the "ping-works-but-topics-empty" signature
     is DIAGNOSTIC of a multicast drop.  Manual sidebar:
        - Add to diagnostic checklist for any ROS 2 comms failure
        - Test with: `sudo iptables -L; sudo ss -uap | grep 7400`
        - Cross-check with `smcroute` or `mtrace` if you want
          to prove the multicast path is dead.

2025.02.07  (L1233)  OSR rpi.md walkthrough - first hard evidence Pi 5 is different
  F  udev rules must match the actual kernel device names, and on Pi 5 the
     names differ from the Pi 3/4 docs everybody links to:
        Pi 3/4 (docs assume this):  /dev/ttyS0  and  /dev/ttyAMA0
        Pi 5 (actual):              /dev/ttys0  (lowercase s!)  and
                                    /dev/ttyAMA10
     Rules using the old names silently produce no symlinks; ls /dev/ser*
     shows "no such file or directory."  This wasted days.
     Reproduce in Field Notes as the single most important Pi 5 UART fact.
  M  udev rules workflow (Manual):
        sudo nano /etc/udev/rules.d/90-serial.rules
     content (Pi 5):
        KERNEL=="ttys0",    SYMLINK+="serial0", GROUP="tty", MODE="0660"
        KERNEL=="ttyAMA10", SYMLINK+="serial1", GROUP="tty", MODE="0660"
     then:
        sudo udevadm control --reload-rules && sudo udevadm trigger
        ls -l /dev/serial*   # confirm /dev/serial0 -> ttys0 exists

2025.02.08  (L1311, L1724)  Wimble driver builds, then serial-timeout hell
  F  yaml type gotcha: rclcpp treats "979.62" (double) and "979" (int)
     as different types.  A driver expecting an integer parameter dies
     with:
        InvalidParameterTypeException: parameter
        'quad_pulses_per_revolution' has invalid type: Wrong parameter
        type, ... is of type {integer}, setting it to {double} is not
        allowed.
     Fix: quote the yaml value with no decimal to force int, or edit the
     driver's declared type.  Field Notes: "yaml ints vs doubles are
     load-time hard errors in ROS 2."
  F  When a ros2 launch process refuses to die on Ctrl-C, use htop to
     find the offending PID and sudo kill it.  Simple survival tip.
  ?  M1/M2 quad_pulses_per_meter here is 2535 (based on Pololu 4843
     20.4:1 with 979.62 counts/rev, wheel radius 0.0615 m).  This is a
     THIRD motor value (in addition to the 8282 and 8226 numbers already
     flagged).  Confirms the log had at least two motor swaps.  Timeline
     appears to be:  ~20.4:1 (4843, 979.62 CPR) -> ~30:1 (4752, 64 CPR
     on shaft) -> 50:1 (current, 3200 counts/rev).  Please confirm.

2025.02.09  (L1725)  "Damage detected" scare
  F  RoboClaw error-LED symptom decoder (Manual sidebar):
        STAT1 solid, no ERR blinking:   normal operation
        ERR blinking 2x:                Driver Fault / "Damage detected" -
                                        motors freewheel.  DOES NOT
                                        necessarily mean actual damage;
                                        can be caused by a flaky main-
                                        battery PowerPole (intermittent
                                        contact).  Reseat the PowerPole
                                        connectors and reboot before you
                                        panic.
     (This was resolved by rebooting after the PowerPole was noticed to
     be flaky.  Motion Studio had shown the RoboClaw as fine.)
  F  When troubleshooting a "serial device" that reports as USB in error
     messages, remember: lsusb won't find a UART-attached RoboClaw.
     Different diagnostic tools (dmesg, ls /dev/tty*, minicom) are the
     right tools.

2025.02.11  (L1867)  Fuse and shunt-diode research
  M  Regenerative-braking protection circuit (definitive, verified by
     RoboClaw docs and later implemented via Pololu 3779):
        When the RoboClaw is off or the fuse is blown, a high-current
        diode (D1) provides a return path to the battery for regenerative
        voltages.  A pre-charge resistor (R1) prevents inrush current and
        arcing on connect.  For a 60V controller, R1 = 1k / 0.5W (gives
        ~15s pre-charge).  Lower voltage => lower R1 => faster pre-
        charge.  For 12V, ~220R / 0.25W is a reasonable extrapolation.
     Current build uses:
        Pololu 3779 shunt regulator - handles regen dump on this bus
        (see 2024.05.07 M note).
     OSR reference part: STPS10L25D Schottky diode (25V 10A TO220AC).
  ?  Are we still using the OSR-style external fuse/diode circuit, or
     does the Pololu 3779 alone cover this?  Verify against current
     wiring diagram in Essentials.

2025.02.12  (L1978)  Fresh install; cloud-init disable
  M  Ubuntu 24.04 network setup (procedure from Sergei Grichine; current
     and worth preserving verbatim in Manual):
        1. Disable cloud-init's network takeover:
           sudo nano /etc/cloud/cloud.cfg.d/99-disable-network-config.cfg
           content:
              network: {config: disabled}
        2. Manage netplan directly (see 2025.02.14 recipe below).
     Reference: github.com/slgrobotics/robots_bringup/tree/main/Docs/
                Ubuntu-RPi

2025.02.14  (L2035)  The canonical netplan recipe (Claude-3.5-Sonnet)
  M  Reproducible static-IP procedure for Ubuntu 24.04 on Pi 5 (verified
     working; Claude-3.5-Sonnet collaboration).  Manual-ready:
        1. sudo apt install network-manager
        2. sudo nano /etc/netplan/01-network-config.yaml  (content):
           network:
               version: 2
               renderer: NetworkManager
               ethernets:
                   eth0:
                       dhcp4: false
                       addresses:
                           - 192.168.68.92/22
                       routes:
                           - to: default
                             via: 192.168.68.1
                             metric: 100
                       nameservers:
                           addresses: [8.8.8.8, 8.8.4.4]
               wifis:
                   wlan0:
                       dhcp4: false
                       addresses:
                           - 192.168.68.93/22
                       routes:
                           - to: default
                             via: 192.168.68.1
                             metric: 200
                       nameservers:
                           addresses: [8.8.8.8, 8.8.4.4]
                       access-points:
                           "<SSID>":
                               password: "<WIFI-PASSWORD>"
        3. sudo netplan try            # dry run
        4. sudo netplan apply
        5. sudo systemctl disable systemd-networkd-wait-online.service
           sudo systemctl disable NetworkManager-wait-online.service
        6. sudo reboot
     Full dialog archived elsewhere as "Netplan YAML - Poe.mhtml".
  F  "systemd-networkd-wait-online.service" is a common boot-delay
     culprit.  Disable it (and its NetworkManager sibling) once the
     network config is stable.
  N  The netplan chapter is a fine example of AI collaboration paying
     off after 24 hours of thrashing: a VERY LONG correspondence with
     Claude-3.5-Sonnet, ending with an explicit "recap the exact steps"
     request that produced the reusable recipe above.  Preserve in
     origin chapter as an example of how to work with AI (ask for the
     debrief in the form you'll want to re-use).

2025.02.17  (unindexed in the top-15 sample; entry is at ~L2200)
     RoboClaw UART: near-miss
  F  RoboClaw 5V-BEC pin caveat (Manual sidebar):
        The 5V pin next to the RoboClaw S1/S2 signal pins is a BEC
        output for microcontrollers that lack their own power.  If the
        Pi is externally powered (it always is on Stormy), DO NOT wire
        that 5V to the Pi.  Wiring only TX, RX, GND is correct.
  F  On Pi 5 with a bare-metal roboclaw.py test, the OSR test script
     may connect to /dev/serial1 (ttyAMA10) but ReadVersion returns
     (0, 0) - meaning open succeeded but no RoboClaw response.  Root
     causes seen so far in this saga:
        (a) 5V BEC wire incorrectly connected (see above)
        (b) Loose Dupont jumpers (rewire and test)
        (c) TXD/RXD swap - safe to try both, no harm
        (d) [SPOILER - RETRACTED 2026.07.20]  I had guessed the
            eventual fix was USB.  James corrected: RoboClaw is
            still on Pi GPIOs today.  The winning configuration is
            still to be found in later chunks.
     Manual conclusion: to be written after we find the entry that
     shows the working GPIO configuration.  This entry (2025.02.17)
     documents the failure modes; the fix belongs with its own
     stardate.
  C  rpi_gpio_test.py side experiment ("RuntimeError: Cannot determine
     SOC peripheral base address" on Pi 5) - Pi 5 GPIO stack is
     different from Pi 3/4; python3-rpi.gpio was not the answer.
     Not currently used.  Cut.

6. (NEW)  Pololu 3779 shunt regulator - called out in the 2024.05.07
   wish list and in the earlier Handoff Note as "still current."

   >>> RESOLVED 2026.07.20 by James.  The Pololu 3779 IS still in
       place, in PARALLEL with the bus (as a regen dump path, not
       a series element).  That is why it does not appear in the
       linear power-topology diagram.  Additional design note from
       James: even with the shunt regulator, the STPS10L25D diode
       across the fuse remains a good idea - the shunt handles
       normal regen dump but the diode covers the fuse-blown case
       (return path to battery for any residual back-EMF).  Both
       features are complementary, not redundant.

================================================================
POWER DISTRIBUTION  (Manual-grade, confirmed by James 2026.07.20)
================================================================
Previous topology (historical - goes in Manual origin chapter as
"how power evolved"):
  LiPo battery
    -> E-stop switch
       -> bus
          -> buck converter + STPS10L25D
             -> powered USB hub
                -> Raspberry Pi + accessories
          -> RoboClaw power input
          -> switches -> 2 headlights
  Field-note lesson attached to this topology (per James):
    "Powered USB hub inadequate."  Symptom / root cause to be
    recovered from the log when we hit the entry.

Current topology (Manual-grade - use verbatim in Power chapter):
  LiPo battery
    -> panel-mount slow-blow fuse, STPS10L25D
       -> E-stop switch
          -> bus
             -> 52Pi power board
                -> Raspberry Pi 5
                -> OAK-D accessory power input
             -> switch -> RoboClaw power input
             -> Indicators PCB
                -> 2 switches -> headlights
                -> 4 amber LEDs

  Bus-parallel (regen dump):
    bus  <->  Pololu 3779 shunt regulator

  Design principle (per James, 2026.07.20):
    The STPS10L25D diode across the fuse and the Pololu 3779 shunt
    regulator are COMPLEMENTARY, not redundant:
      - The 3779 dumps regenerative energy from the motors during
        normal operation, keeping bus voltage within the RoboClaw's
        and 52Pi hat's tolerance.
      - The STPS10L25D across the fuse provides a return path to the
        battery for any residual back-EMF in the fuse-blown case,
        when the 3779 no longer has a bus reference.
    Keep both.

Key differences (Field Notes cluster on power design):
  * Fuse + STPS10L25D moved UPSTREAM of the E-stop.  Now protects
    the whole downstream bus regardless of where a fault appears
    (including a shorted E-stop).
  * Buck converter + powered USB hub REMOVED, replaced by a single
    52Pi PD power expansion board that feeds both the Pi 5 and the
    OAK-D.  Fewer boxes, one 12V-to-Pi conversion path.
  * OAK-D is powered from the 52Pi board rather than off the Pi's
    own USB rail - avoids the classic OAK-D brown-out under load.
  * Headlights and status LEDs consolidated onto an Indicators PCB
    instead of hand-wired switch-to-lamp.
  * RoboClaw now gated by its own dedicated switch on the bus.

================================================================
END OF CHUNK 2
Pi 5 quirks:
  * Pi 5 UART device names: ttys0 (lowercase) + ttyAMA10, not ttyS0/AMA0
  * Pi 5 GPIO stack is not python3-rpi.gpio compatible
  * The Pi 5 UART on GPIO is not reliable enough for RoboClaw - use USB

ROS 2 gotchas:
  * yaml int vs double type mismatch is a hard error at load
  * Ctrl-C-resistant launch: use htop + sudo kill
  * Wimble driver is the RoboClaw driver we standardize on

Human process:
  * AI in circles = walk away
  * After a working install, IMAGE THE SD/SSD before touching serial
  * Ask AI for a "recap the exact steps" debrief when done
  * Vendor sample code often lags actual hardware / OS combos

RoboClaw specifics:
  * Address 128, packet serial, 115200 baud
  * Do not connect the S1/S2 5V BEC pin to a Pi that has its own power
  * ERR blinking 2x is not always damage - check PowerPole first
  * AutoTune 10x and average
  * Disconnect Pi during Motion Studio velocity tuning
  * *** THE HAIRBALL LESSON *** (Manual sidebar; confirmed by
    James 2026.07.20 as the actual cause of Stormy's motor
    burnout):
        A hair-strangled wheel caused the winding to burn out.
        A wheel doesn't need to hit a wall to stall; a long-fiber
        household carpet or a shed pet hair can wrap around the
        wheel-shaft junction, jam the wheel, and hold it at high
        current until the winding cooks - especially if the
        RoboClaw Max Current is set too permissively (Stormy was
        originally at 15A per channel; correct is ~3A per
        channel; see Chunk 7 2025.04.07 for the corrected value).
        "Take a lesson from your vacuum cleaner and check for
        hairballs" (James, 2026.07.20).
     Pre-drive checklist (Manual):
       (a) Motion Studio Max Current per channel <= ~3A (see
           Chunk 7 for the (Stall-NoLoad)/2 + NoLoad derivation).
       (b) BEFORE each carpeted run, visually inspect the wheel/
           shaft area; clear any hair or fiber wrap.
       (c) AFTER each run on suspect terrain, inspect again.
       (d) On unfamiliar terrain, cap max_linear_velocity
           conservatively so a bind cannot immediately draw
           peak current.
     Cross-reference: the specific burnout entry (still to be
     located in later chunks) will get cited here once found.
  * Pololu 37Dx*L motors all look identical in the product photos.
    The SKU is the only reliable ratio identifier.  Photograph the
    SKU sticker on delivery and file it with the build documentation
    before installation.  Do not trust visual identification for
    ratio, CPR, or counts-per-revolution.

================================================================
END OF CHUNK 2
Next: CHUNK 3, lines ~2400 to ~5000, spanning 2025.02.18 through
approximately mid-March 2025.  Expect the UART saga to continue and
eventually pivot to the USB workaround.

N  FORMATIVE INFLUENCES (origin chapter, opening beats)
----------------------------------------------------------------
  N  Childhood disposition: "take machines apart to see how they
     worked or why they broke ... by meticulous disassembly I
     learned to put them back together, sometimes repaired."
     Ultimate machine = the human body/brain -> became a doctor.
     Father pharmacist, mother medical secretary supported the
     path.  Same instinct = amateur roboticist / AI afficionado.
     Keep VERBATIM as the Manual's opening paragraph.
  N  1956 "Forbidden Planet" -> Robbie the Robot as the seed
     image.  "I wanted that robot."  Cultural anchor.
  N  Middle-school reading: Monroe Upton, "Electronics for
     Everyone" (2nd ed., 1962).  First technical vocabulary:
     voltage, current, R/L/C, tubes, and the newly invented
     transistor.
  N  First serious build: Heathkit VTVM (pre-learning-kit era).
     "Poke around in the invisible world of electronics."
  N  "Popular Mechanics gave way to Popular Electronics."  One-
     line generational transition marker.
  N  Prospect High School - "Special Math Topics" senior elective
     (half statistics, half computer programming).
     The class acted out ASSEMBLER-TYPE PSEUDOCODE on the black-
     board, NOT the IBM 360 itself:
       Reader receives a number from the teacher, passes it to
       Memory 1.  Reader passes another number to Memory 2.
       Memory 1 -> Arithmetic unit.  Memory 2 -> Arithmetic unit.
       Arithmetic unit adds and passes to Memory 3.  Memory 3 ->
       Printer, who writes it on the board.
     Then Fortran.  Programs written on paper, transferred to
     punch cards in the Admin office, and run on the school
     district's IBM System 360.  Output back next class.
     >>> CORRECTED 2026.07.26 per James: the students acted out
     the pseudocode; the IBM 360 was where the actual Fortran
     programs ran.  Do not conflate.
  N  UIUC for pre-med, coincidentally a leader in CS/AI.
     Made room in pre-med for Fortran, PL1, and PLATO.  "Shortest
     path on a map" was frontier grad-student thesis stuff then;
     today it's Waze and Google Maps in his pocket.
  N  Med school + residency = the hobby's dark years.
  N  Post-residency reawakening via SCAMC (now AMIA) meetings in
     D.C. and Baltimore.  Learned S.O.A.P. notes and medical
     records systems.  Bought an Alpha Micro PDP-11/73 clone with
     an 8" floppy, later a mini-fridge-sized 10 MB hard drive.
     Early adopter of computer medical records: installed
     MEDITECH in first office, later Windows/eClinicalWorks.
     Audited the HCC basic-hardware class (built a server from
     Fry's parts) and sysadmin class where he "learned to
     misconfigure things I never knew existed."  Keep the
     misconfigure line - Field-Notes-worthy sensibility.

----------------------------------------------------------------
N  BEAM -> BOE-BOT -> STINGRAY GENEALOGY
     The direct technical lineage that ends at Stormy.
----------------------------------------------------------------
  N  BEAM (Biology Electronics Aesthetics Mechanics) robotics
     discovered as ultra-simple accumulate-and-fire circuits.
     Solar cell or battery charges a capacitor; a trigger dumps
     the current into an LED, coil, or motor for pulsatile
     light/movement.  Photocell pair = phototropism or
     photophobia.
  N  The Turbot: right-angle motors driving bent arms, flopping
     the body at 45 degrees.  Two photocells outward (L vs R),
     two photocells up/down (which side is up), plus a stall-
     reversal circuit.  Released in a room, it works itself
     toward the window; under a ceiling light, it circles the
     floor.
  N  Turbot reverse-engineering:  no answer online, so armed
     with only "a primitive understanding of logic circuits and
     a Radio Shack multimeter", James probed the Turbot under
     controlled conditions - right vs left, top vs bottom
     photocells blinded, free-running vs stalled.  A spread-
     sheet and logic diagram revealed its "thought processes."
     >>> This IS the through-line to Stormy.  Reverse-engineer-
     via-spreadsheet-and-controlled-experiment is the same
     methodology used later on the RoboClaw, the UART, and the
     Nav2 param files.  Cite here, refer back when it recurs.
  N  Result: "Beam Robot Neurosurgery," Robot Magazine
     2009.11-12 issue.  First publication.
  N  Follow-up: Boe-Bot review, "gateway to robotics," Robot
     Magazine 2010.03-04.  Two-wheel differential drive table
     bot; Basic Stamp brain simple enough to understand; small
     = cheap, portable, safe.  Adaptable to Propeller, Arduino,
     Raspberry Pi -> still a great starter kit.
  N  Follow-up: Parallax Stingray review, "Boe-Bot's bad boy
     big brother," Robot Magazine 2010.05-06.  Larger chassis,
     bigger motors, Propeller brain.  Too big for a table bot;
     pretty black-anodized aluminum with pre-drilled/slotted
     panels invited upgrades.  No longer in production;
     fondly remembered.
     >>> "This is the kit that became Stormy the Stingray."
     Verbatim, this is the sentence that closes Manual Chapter 1.
  N  Why the Propeller was swapped for a Raspberry Pi: James
     wasn't a fan of Spin (Propeller's language).  This is the
     ORIGIN reason for the Pi migration, not any technical
     limitation of the Propeller itself.  Note for the Manual:
     the acrylic-plates rebuild + Pi swap were aesthetic and
     tooling choices as much as engineering ones.

N  SIGNIFICANT ORGANIZATIONS  (the org history that flattens
     the "Houston Robotics / USAi Labs / X-Labs" confusion in
     the log)
----------------------------------------------------------------
  N  Houston Robotics (first).  Met at TXRX Labs makerspace.
     Beginners + advanced hobbyists.  "Foothills of the learning
     curve/mountain/cliff."  Outgrew the conference room.
  N  The member couple "R&D" who stepped up to help find a
     bigger space are RAYMOND & DIANE - i.e. Dr. G. Raymond
     Brown and Diane Brown, introduced in the very next
     paragraph of the essay.  (Confirmed by James 2026.07.26.)
     Manual acknowledgments: cite by full name; "R&D" is a
     nice James-ism worth preserving as an aside.
  N  Houston Community College (HCC), now HOUSTON CITY COLLEGE -
     Stafford Workforce campus.  Dr. G. Raymond Brown (physics
     professor) and his wife Diane secured a classroom across
     the hall from the Fab Lab.  This is where the group did
     most of its work.
     Manual convention: use "Houston Community College (HCC,
     now Houston City College)" on first mention, then "HCC"
     thereafter.  Historical events (2018-2019 OSR/Fidelity era)
     are correctly HCC = Houston Community College at that time.
     (Confirmed by James 2026.07.26.)
  N  Incorporation for the HCC relationship required renaming.
     "Houston Robotics" name was retained by its owner, so the
     group renamed to USAi Labs.  "Any organization containing
     'AI' got hacked" -> renamed again to X-Labs.  Meanwhile
     Houston Robotics reformed elsewhere; James is not a
     member of that reformed group.
     >>> Chronology summary for the Manual:
     Houston Robotics (at TXRX) -> USAi Labs (at HCC) ->
     X-Labs (at HCC, current).  All the same people.
  N  The Fab Lab: "a maker's Disneyland - 3D printers, laser
     cutters, electronics instruments, CNC machines, and lots
     of tools."  Roland Fields and his lab assistants provided
     CAD/manufacturing help.  Gallery of other users' products
     as ongoing inspiration.
  N  HomeBrew Robotics Club (HBRC) - hbrobotics.org.  Range
     from beginners through professionals.  James participates
     remotely (Sunnyvale is 2000 mi / 30 hr by car / 4 hr &
     $350 by plane) via Zoom.
     Meeting cadence (Manual reference):
       - General Meeting: last Wed of month.  Presentations.
         Quarterly Robot Challenge.
       - Builder's SIG: next-to-last Wed of month.  Show &
         Tell + Q&A.
       - ROS Special Interest Group: Tuesday 7 pm Pacific
         (9 pm Houston), Camp Peavy (HBRC President) hosting.
       - AI SIG: Thursday (and drifting toward Sunday) 7 pm
         Pacific, Thomas Messerschmidt hosting.

N  OSR / FIDELITY ORIGIN  (backstory for the Blessing)
----------------------------------------------------------------
  N  2018.07.31   NASA/JPL announce the Open Source Rover
                  publicly.  Aimed at high-schoolers and
                  hobbyists; simplified Mars-type rover from
                  standard off-the-shelf parts with build
                  instructions and operating code.
  N  2018.08.11   Handful of Houston Robotics members recess to
                  a nearby Mexican restaurant after Saturday's
                  meeting.  Someone mentions the OSR release.
                  Everyone pulls out phones.  "Who doesn't want
                  their own Mars rover?"  Decision reached
                  over lunch: "We've GOT to do this!"
                  Charles De Montague and David Williams named
                  as the people who brought the announcement
                  in.  Money committed, parts ordered.
  N  Community    Hundreds of OSR builders worldwide
     dimension:   collaborated online.  Mission Patch was
                  designed and produced.  Continuous stream of
                  improvements and adaptations to keep up with.
  N  Roles:       Mechanical assembly worked great as a group
                  project.  PCB soldering and programming
                  "not so."  As project lead James became the
                  owner of the rover.
  N  Naming       "Mars rovers need a name, usually ending in
     philosophy   -ity suggesting a quality of being."
                  Two-name plan set from the start:
                  FIDELITY  = the faithful-to-JPL-plans build
                  AUDACITY  = the future modified rover, name
                              already reserved for when
                              Fidelity "reaches adolescence"
                              and cuts the umbilical cord.
                  Stormy the Stingray is the spiritual sibling
                  of the not-yet-built Audacity.
  N  2019.08.24   Fidelity's first roll-out - "one year and
                  two weeks" after the decision.  HCC Beat's
                  video crew happened to be present filming a
                  feature on the USAi-Labs / HCC relationship;
                  they caught the roll-out on camera.
  N  2019.10.04   HCC Beat broadcast airs.
                  YouTube: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2Wdgss0q63s
                  (Verified live by James 2026.07.26.)

----------------------------------------------------------------
N  THE BLESSING OF THE ROBOT  (Manual origin chapter,
     dedicated section - insert VERBATIM per the Road-to-
     Robotics essay's own placeholder marker)
     Source: X-Labs/Mars Rover/Blessing of the Robot 4.txt
----------------------------------------------------------------
  N  Occasion: delivered by James (as Mars rover project lead,
     USAi Labs / formerly Houston Robotics) at Fidelity's first
     roll-out, 2019.08.24.  This is the "Blessing" referenced
     in the user's 2026.07.26 handoff: "note the Blessing of
     the Robot ceremony I did after Fidelity's first rollout."
  N  Framing epigraphs (keep as the section's opening):
       Collier & Dacier, 1708 rendering of Marcus Aurelius:
       "You should consider that imitation is the most accept-
       able part of Worship, and that the Gods had much rather
       Mankind should Resemble, than Flatter them."
       Oscar Wilde: "Imitation is the sincerest form of
       flattery that mediocrity can pay to greatness."
     Thesis: to attempt, in our limited human capacity, to
     emulate the human body and especially the human mind is
     to truly appreciate their magnificence.
  N  Outsider vs insider view of robotics.  Verbatim, keep the
     expletive placeholders as written:
       "But we know that just keeping the (expletive deleted)
       contraption from running into the (more expletives
       deleted) wall can be (still more expletives deleted)
       difficult!  Let alone think!"
     This one line is the whole Manual's emotional register.
  N  ***CENTERPIECE***  The "be the robot" thought experiment.
     Sit on a wheeled stool inside a dark appliance box, no
     senses.  You are not an intelligent human under a box;
     you are just a dumb box with a couple motors.  What
     senses and sensibilities would need to be installed for
     you to find the kitchen?  Bumper.  Distance stick.
     Compass.  Light meter (or two, for phototropism).  Scratch
     paper + calculator.  Are you any smarter yet?  Not very.
     Have you found the kitchen?  You don't even know where
     the door is.
     >>> Payoff line, quotable in the Manual: "You have just
     experienced the problem of SLAM.  Simultaneous Location
     and Mapping.  Where am I and what does the world around
     me look like?  Never mind what I'm supposed to do here!"
     Followed by: camera-as-spreadsheet-of-numbers ->
     computer vision.
     >>> Manual note: this thought experiment PRE-DATES
     Stormy's actual SLAM work by ~4 years.  Cite forward from
     here to the SLAM Toolbox chapters (Chunks 11+) as
     "this is what SLAM Toolbox eventually solved for us."
     The prayer describes the problem; the later chapters
     describe the solution.  Wonderful narrative arc.
  N  Historical anchors that agree with the Road-to-Robotics
     essay (cross-check confirmed):
       - 1997.07.04  Sojourner (first Mars rover) as the
                     "I want one of those" moment
       - 2018.07.31  NASA/JPL OSR release
       - 2018.08.11  Mexican-restaurant lunch decision
       - Charles De Montague and David Williams brought the
         announcement (named in both sources)
       - "After a year of intense labor" -> the 2019.08.24
         roll-out
       - Known-at-roll-out issues acknowledged: "steering and
         the head display are among them."  Manual: preserve
         as an honest-account footnote.
  N  The dedication proper - keep VERBATIM in the Manual.
     Structure:
       (a) Blessing of the robot Fidelity ("faithful to its
           original design").
       (b) Blessing of the organization.
       (c) Blessing of the supporters, especially HCC and the
           NASA/JPL OSR builder community.
       (d) Symbolism statement: not a product of materials
           and methods but a labor of love by those who wish
           to know God by trying to be like Him.
  N  Adolescence metaphor - the earliest formal on-record
     statement of the Fidelity -> Audacity naming lineage:
       "Once a child reaches adolescence it must cut the
       umbilical cord of parental control ... So shall it be
       with our robot.  Sometimes when an individual reaches
       a profound turning point in their life, they acquire a
       new name.  When Fidelity reaches adolescence it, too,
       shall acquire a new name.  It shall be called Audacity."
     Manual origin chapter should QUOTE THIS DIRECTLY as the
     canonical source for the naming genealogy that ends at
     Stormy.
  F  The Petition list (from the closing prayer) - promote to a
     Field-Notes credo, printable as a lab-wall poster:
       Courage         to explore new ideas
       Perseverance    when we face failure
       Patience        in the presence of inadequate documentation
       Insight         into the puzzles before us
       Wisdom          to use our newfound knowledge for good
     >>> "Patience in the presence of inadequate documentation"
     is the single most Field-Notes-worthy line in the whole
     essay corpus.  It is the emotional counterweight to every
     RoboClaw / UART / Nav2 debugging entry in the log.
     Manual: give it its own pull-quote box.
  N  Closing charge - verbatim:
       "Let us continue to keep robotics, not just our hobby,
       but our ministry.  Amen."

      pull-quote):  "I set AUDACITY aside out of frustration
      with my own ignorance of robotics.  I needed to step back
      and pick up something simpler - learn the principles,
      then migrate them to the rover."
      >>> Manual: this is a general lesson worth stating
      explicitly for anyone else who lands on a project too
      big to learn on.  "When the platform is too complex for
      you to understand end-to-end, put it down and go find
      one you CAN understand end-to-end.  Come back later."
      Cross-link to the Boe-Bot / GoPiGo-too-small rejection
      in the bridge - the point is not that small is bad but
      that the learning platform has to be BIG ENOUGH to host
      the eventual grown-up problem.  Stingray was the
      Goldilocks size.

----------------------------------------------------------------
N  BRIDGE PARAGRAPH (final draft, flag #10)
     Chapter 5 opening; assembled from James's own account
     supplied 2026.07.26.  Purpose: bridge 2019.10.04 (HCC Beat
     broadcast of Fidelity's roll-out) to 2023.05.06 (first
     stardate in Stingray Experience.txt), and set up the
     Stormy-as-learning-platform framing that governs the rest
     of the log.
----------------------------------------------------------------

  --- draft begins ---

    Fidelity did not rest after her HCC Beat close-up.  For the
    next five years she was the whole workbench: a rolling
    testbed for what she might become.  Different motors,
    displays, lighting, WiFi configurations, cameras, streaming-
    video attempts, mechanical and wiring changes, freshly
    laser-cut front and rear panels to accommodate each new
    idea, OS upgrades, Raspberry Pi upgrades from 3b to 4b.
    That work is chronicled in its own 859-page log,
    "Fidelity to AUDACITY" (2019.10.19 - 2024.09.07), which
    itself follows the 128-page "Mars Rover Experience"
    (2018.08 - 2019.12.02) that covered Fidelity's original
    construction.  Both are their own future editing projects;
    they are not folded into this Manual.  What matters here
    is why Fidelity paused.

    I set AUDACITY aside out of frustration with my own
    ignorance of robotics.  I needed to step back and pick up
    something simpler - learn the principles, then migrate
    them to the rover.  The Boe-Bot and GoPiGo were too small.
    I needed a suitable platform, and I didn't want to build
    one from scratch.  The Stingray would be perfect.

    Where was it?  My study is very organized - everything in
    clear storage boxes - and I could see it nowhere.  Not in
    the garage.  Not in the attic.  I checked; I hadn't left
    it at the school.  Where the heck was it?  It had been
    years since I had seen the long-mothballed machine.  It
    had to be here, but where?

    I emptied my mind of the frustration and just gazed
    around.  And then, there sitting on the shelf right in
    front of me, under the box with the headlight magnifier
    and bags of zip ties and rip ties, was the box -
    basketweave and brown as the bookshelf itself, unlabeled,
    with a plain black lid.  The box I had bought specifically
    for it years ago and forgotten.  With nervous anticipation
    I retrieved the box, opened the lid, and THERE IT WAS.

    (I described the search and discovery to a friend.  She
    called it a "love story."  She wasn't wrong.)

    For a time I tried to use the Stingray as it was, but the
    accessories I wanted just didn't fit the many pre-drilled
    holes and slots.  I was going to have to customize.  So
    I did.

    I also needed a software stack that would do the job, and
    that I could learn.  I looked at Linorobot and Articulated
    Robotics.  Articulated won.  Sergei Grichine (sglrobotics)
    had forked Articulated and used it as the basis for one
    of his own robots, Plucky.  He tutored me through adapting
    that fork to the Stingray - first in simulation, then
    live.

    That is where the log picks up on 2023.05.06.

  --- draft ends ---

  Structural notes for the Manual:
    - Sits at the head of Chapter 5 (Rediscovery), not as a
      tail on Chapter 4 (Fidelity).  It is the "once upon a
      time, three-and-a-half years later" opening.
    - Chunk 1's rediscovery N-item ("Found the Stingray body
      in a camouflage box on the bookshelf", approx L88-L119
      of Stingray Experience.txt) is now REDUNDANT with the
      third and fourth paragraphs of this draft, which are
      Manual-polished versions of the same story told with
      more color from James's 2026.07.26 recollection.  For
      Chapter 5, use THIS draft in place of the log excerpt.
      Keep the log excerpt intact where it lives (it is
      historical source material) but do not print it twice.
    - The friend's "love story" line matches what James told
      Sergei / the group at the time - if any of them
      remember hearing it originally, that could be a nice
      Manual footnote ("a friend to whom I told the story").
      Not required.
    - The last line ("That is where the log picks up on
      2023.05.06.") is the seam.  Chapter 5 continues from
      here with the first stardate content proper (already
      curated in Chunk 1 as M/F/N items).

  M  (Manual technical) - the software-stack decision belongs
     ALSO as a one-liner in the Builder's Manual's Software
     Stack chapter, extracted from this narrative:
       Two candidates were evaluated: Linorobot and Articulated
       Robotics.  Articulated won.  Stormy runs on
       Sergei Grichine's (sglrobotics) fork of Articulated
       Robotics - the same fork that runs his own robot Plucky.
       Sim-first, then live.
     Cross-link: this is the decision that determines every
     subsequent chapter's package names, launch-file topology,
     and URDF layout.  Manual should make the origin explicit
     so future readers know why Stormy's tree looks the way it
     does.
